\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota^{\prime}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Im}(\iota^{\prime})(\subseteq\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}));[h,\epsilon]\longmapsto[h,\widetilde{s}(h)^{-1}\epsilon].

(3.16)

3.6.
The decomposition

For any
h\in\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we write:

h=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{|\det(h)|}&0\\
0&\sqrt{|\det(h)|}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\Big[\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{|\det h|}^{-1}&\\
&\sqrt{|\det h|}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\Big].

So
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\cdot\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
denote the corresponding subgroups of
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
respectively.

Lemma 3.17
.

(1)

\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\times\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(2)

\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\times\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(3)

\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\times\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

For
h_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
h_{2}=t\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\subseteq\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, write
y_{1}=\det h_{1}
,
g_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
y_{1}^{-1}c&y_{1}^{-1}d\end{pmatrix}
,
y_{2}=t^{2}
,
g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&t^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
,
g_{1}^{y_{2}}g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}at&bt\\
y_{1}^{-1}t^{-1}c&y_{1}^{-1}t^{-1}d\end{pmatrix}=g_{2}g_{1}
. Then:

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})=\nu_{2}(y_{2},g_{1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}^{y_{2}},g_{2})=1\cdot\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}^{y_{2}},g_{2})=1,

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{1})=\nu_{2}(y_{1},g_{2})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}^{y_{1}},g_{1})=1\cdot\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2},g_{1})=1,

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})=\nu(y_{2},g_{1})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}^{y_{2}},g_{2})=\nu(y_{2},g_{1})=1,

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{1})=\nu(y_{1},g_{2})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}^{y_{1}},g_{1})=\nu(y_{1},g_{2})=1,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(g_{1})\overline{s}(g_{2})\overline{s}(g_{1}^{y_{2}}g_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(g_{1})\overline{s}(g_{2})\overline{s}(g_{2}g_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(g_{1})\overline{s}(g_{1})^{-1}=1,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},h_{1})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2}h_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{2}h_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h_{1})\overline{s}(h_{2})\overline{s}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})=1.

∎

Under the above isomorphisms,
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\nsimeq\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}\times\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\nsimeq\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}\times\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\nsimeq\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}\times\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. For any
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, we write:

h=\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{|\det(h)|}&0\\
0&\sqrt{|\det(h)|}\end{pmatrix}}_{h_{1}}\cdot\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{|\det(h)|}^{-1}&0\\
0&\sqrt{|\det(h)|}\end{pmatrix}}_{h_{2}}\cdot\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c|\det(h)|^{-1}&d|\det(h)|^{-1}\end{pmatrix}}_{h_{3}}.

y_{3}=\det h_{3}=\frac{\det h}{|\det h|},g_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c\det(h)^{-1}&d\det(h)^{-1}\end{pmatrix},y_{2}=\det h_{2}=1,g_{2}=h_{2}

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{3})=\nu(y_{3},g_{2})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}^{y_{3}},g_{3})=\nu(y_{3},g_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chap3alpp})}}{{=}}1

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{3})=\nu_{2}(y_{3},g_{2})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}^{y_{3}},g_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chap3alppu2})}}{{=}}1\cdot\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}^{y_{3}},g_{3})=1.

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},h_{3})=\nu_{z_{0}}(y_{3},g_{2})\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}(g_{2}^{y_{3}},g_{3})=\nu_{z_{0}}(y_{3},g_{2})=\nu_{2}(y_{3},g_{2})=1.

Thus, for
\overline{h}=[h,t_{1}]\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\widetilde{h}=[h,t_{2}]\in\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\widehat{h}=[h,t_{3}]\in\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, we can write:

\overline{h}=h_{1}\cdot h_{2}\cdot\underbrace{[h_{3},t_{1}]}_{\overline{h}_{3}},\qquad\widetilde{h}=h_{1}\cdot h_{2}\cdot\underbrace{[h_{3},t_{2}]}_{\widetilde{h}_{3}},\qquad\widehat{h}=h_{1}\cdot h_{2}\cdot\underbrace{[h_{3},t_{3}]}_{\widehat{h}_{3}}.

Note that if
\det h\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times 2}
, then all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
belong to
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Lemma 3.18
.

The centers of
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
are
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
is the center of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, the claim follows from the cocycle identities established in the proof of Lemma
3.17
.
∎

3.7.
The parabolic group

Lemma 3.19
.

For
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
p=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
0&a_{1}^{-1}\det p\end{pmatrix}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

•

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)=1
;

•

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(\det p,a)_{\mathbb{R}}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
(c\det h,\det p)_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(\det p,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Let
g_{h}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c(\det h)^{-1}&d(\det h)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
, and
g_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
0&a_{1}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)=\nu(\det h,g_{p})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}((g_{p})^{\det h},g_{h})=(\det h,a_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}=1;

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)=\nu(\det p,g_{h})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}((g_{h})^{\det p},g_{p})=\nu(\det p,g_{h})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(\det p,a)_{\mathbb{R}}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
(c\det h,\det p)_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(\det p,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

∎

Lemma 3.20
.

For
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
p=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
0&a_{1}^{-1}\det p\end{pmatrix}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

•

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)=1
;

•

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(\det p,a)_{\mathbb{R}}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
1&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Let
g_{h}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c(\det h)^{-1}&d(\det h)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
, and
g_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
0&a_{1}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\det h,g_{p})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}((g_{p})^{\det h},g_{h})

\displaystyle=(\det h,a_{1})_{\mathbb{R}}(x(g_{p}^{\det h}),x(g_{h}))_{\mathbb{R}}(-x(g_{p}^{\det h})x(g_{h}),x((g_{p})^{\det h}g_{h}))_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle=1;

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\det p,g_{h})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}((g_{h})^{\det p},g_{p})=\nu_{2}(\det p,g_{h})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(\det p,a)_{\mathbb{R}}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
1&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

∎

Lemma 3.21
.

For
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
p=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
0&a_{1}^{-1}\det p\end{pmatrix}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(p,h)=1
.

Proof.

\overline{s}(p)=1,\overline{s}(ph)=\overline{s}(pp_{h}k_{h})=\overline{s}(k_{h})=\overline{s}(h)=1;

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(p,h)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)\overline{s}(p)\overline{s}(h)\overline{s}(ph)^{-1}=1.

∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, with
a>0,d>0
and
h\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p)=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,h)=1
.

Proof.

Decompose
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&b/a\\
0&d/a\end{pmatrix}
.
By Lemma
3.17
the results hold for the first factor; by Lemmas
3.19
and
3.20
they hold for the second. Hence the result follows.
∎

3.8.
The maximal compact subgroups
K

We consider the maximal compact subgroup
K=\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
in
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
or
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 3.23
.

The restriction of
[\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}]
on
\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
is not trivial.

Proof.

The orthogonal group sits in the split exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\overset{\det}{\longrightarrow}\mu_{2}\longrightarrow 1,

and we fix the section

s\colon\mu_{2}\longrightarrow\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad s(\varepsilon)=\operatorname{diag}(1,\varepsilon).

3.8.0.1
Step 1: restricting to
\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

By Lemma
2.6
(with
\psi=\psi_{0}
) we have

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\overline{s}(g_{1}g_{2})\,\overline{s}(g_{1})^{-1}\overline{s}(g_{2})^{-1},\qquad g_{1},g_{2}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),

so
[\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}|_{\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}]=0
.
Extend
\overline{s}
to all of
\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
by setting

\overline{s}(h)=1
whenever
h\notin\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and define

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(h_{1},h_{2}):=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})\;\overline{s}(h_{1})\,\overline{s}(h_{2})\,\overline{s}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1},\qquad h_{1},h_{2}\in\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Then
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}
is cohomologous to
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}
and

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(g_{1},g_{2})=1\quad\text{for all }g_{1},g_{2}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

For
g\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s(1),g)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(s(1),g)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}([1,1],[1,g])=1;

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s(-1),g)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(s(-1),g)\overline{s}(g)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}([-1,1],[1,g])\overline{s}(g)=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(1,g)\overline{s}(g)=\overline{s}(g).

3.8.0.2
Step 2: the Hochschild–Serre sequence.

The Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence gives the exact sequence

0\to H^{1}(\mu_{2},\mathbb{T})\to H^{1}(\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{T})\to H^{1}(\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{T})^{\mu_{2}}\xrightarrow{d_{2}}H^{2}(\mu_{2},\mathbb{T})\to H^{2}(\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{T})_{1}\xrightarrow{p}H^{1}\!\bigl(\mu_{2},H^{1}(\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{T})\bigr).

Because
H^{2}(\mu_{2},\mathbb{T})=0
, the map
p
is injective. Thus

[\,\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}\,]
is trivial iff
p([\,\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}\,])=0
.

3.8.0.3
Step 3: evaluating
p([\,\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}\,])
.

For any
h\in\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, let us write
h=s(\epsilon_{h})g_{h}
, for
\epsilon_{h}\in\mu_{2}
,
g_{h}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Define a measurable function
\varkappa
of
\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
as

\varkappa(h)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s_{1}(\epsilon_{h}),g_{h})
. Let us define

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime\prime}=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}\circ\delta_{1}(\varkappa).

So the map
p
is just given by

p([\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}]):\epsilon\longmapsto(g\longrightarrow\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime\prime}(g,s(\epsilon))),\quad g\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\epsilon\in\mu_{2}.

Note that
p([\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}])(\epsilon)
is a character of
\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta&-\sin\theta\\
\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
g^{s(-1)}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos(-\theta)&-\sin(-\theta)\\
\sin(-\theta)&\cos(-\theta)\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime\prime}(g,s(-1))

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(g,s(-1))\varkappa(g)\varkappa(s(-1))\varkappa(gs(-1))^{-1}

(3.17)

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(g,s(-1))\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s(1),g)\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s(-1),1)\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}^{\prime}(s(-1),s(-1)^{-1}gs(-1))^{-1}

(3.18)

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(g,s(-1))\overline{s}(g)\overline{s}(g^{s(-1)})^{-1}

(3.19)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\overline{s}(g)\overline{s}(g^{s(-1)})^{-1}=e^{i\theta}&\textrm{ if }-\pi<\theta<\pi,\\
-1&\textrm{ if }\theta=-\pi,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=e^{i\theta}.

(3.22)

Note that there does not exist a character
f
of
\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that
e^{i\theta}=f(e^{i\theta})f^{-1}((e^{i\theta})^{s(-1)})=f(e^{2i\theta})
. Hence

p([\,\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}\,])\neq 0
, and therefore

[\,\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}\,]\neq 0
in
\operatorname{H}^{2}(\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{T})
.
∎

Corollary 3.24
.

The restrictions of both
[\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}]
and
[\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}]
to
\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
are non-trivial.

3.9.
The discrete subgroups

Lemma 3.25
.

The restriction of
[\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}]
to
\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
is non-trivial.

Proof.

Because
-I\in\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
commutes with
h_{-1}
, any trivialization would satisfy

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(-I,h_{-1})=f(-I)f(h_{-1})f((-I)h_{-1})^{-1}=f(-I)f(h_{-1})f(h_{-1}(-I))^{-1}=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},-I).

However, Lemma
3.20
gives

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(-I,h_{-1})=-1\neq 1=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},-I),

so no such function
f
can exist.
∎

Corollary 3.26
.

The restrictions of both
[\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}]
and
[\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}]
to
\Gamma_{\theta}^{\pm}
,
\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
are non-trivial.

3.10.
The projective group
\operatorname{PGL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})

Let
\operatorname{GL}_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{R})=\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\operatorname{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})=\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{R}^{\times}
,
\operatorname{PGL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})=\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
.
Then:

•

\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}=\mathbb{R}^{\times 2}
and
\mathbb{R}^{\times}/\mathbb{R}^{\times 2}\simeq\mu_{2}
.

•

There exists an exact sequence:
1\longrightarrow\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})/\{\pm 1\}\longrightarrow\operatorname{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dot{\lambda}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{\times}/\mathbb{R}^{\times 2}\longrightarrow 1.

•

There exists an exact sequence:
1\longrightarrow\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{\times}\longrightarrow 1.

•

There exists an exact sequence:
1\longrightarrow\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\operatorname{PGL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dot{\lambda}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{\times}/\mathbb{R}^{\times 2}\longrightarrow 1.

By Lemma
3.17
we set

\overline{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})=\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times},\qquad\widetilde{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})=\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times},\qquad\widehat{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})=\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times},

and obtain the exact sequences

1\to\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\overline{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dot{\overline{\lambda}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mu_{2}\to 1,

1\to\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\widetilde{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dot{\widetilde{\lambda}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mu_{2}\to 1,

1\to\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\widehat{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dot{\widehat{\lambda}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mu_{2}\to 1.

Fix
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}^{\times}
. Then
\mu_{2}\cong\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle
, and the map

\dot{\iota}_{r}\colon\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\to\operatorname{PGL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad-r\longmapsto\Bigl[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix}\Bigr],

is a group homomorphism. Consequently

\operatorname{PGL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\cong\bigl(\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\bigr)\ltimes\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

We extend the construction to the covering groups via the explicit lifts

\overline{\dot{\iota}}_{r}\colon\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\to\overline{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad-r\longmapsto\Bigl[\Bigl(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},1\Bigr)\Bigr],

\widetilde{\dot{\iota}}_{r}\colon\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\to\widetilde{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad-r\longmapsto\Bigl[\Bigl(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{e})}{4}}\Bigr)\Bigr],

\widehat{\dot{\iota}}_{r}\colon\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\to\widehat{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad-r\longmapsto\Bigl[\Bigl(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\Bigr)\Bigr].

Lemma 3.27
.

\overline{\dot{\iota}}_{r}
,
\widetilde{\dot{\iota}}_{r}
,
\widehat{\dot{\iota}}_{r}
all are group homomorphisms.

Proof.

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-r}\omega^{-1},h_{-r}\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(-r,\omega^{-1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}((\omega^{-1})^{h_{-r}},\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&-r\\
r^{-1}&0\end{pmatrix},\omega^{-1})=1.

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-r}\omega^{-1},h_{-r}\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle=\nu(-r,\omega^{-1})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}((\omega^{-1})^{h_{-r}},\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle=\nu(-r,\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Ex.}\ref {chap3example2}}}{{=}}(-1,-r)_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(-r,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=-e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{e})}{2}}=(e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{e})}{4}})^{2}.

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\nu_{z_{0}}(-r,\omega^{-1})\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}((\omega^{-1})^{h_{-r}},\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Ex.}\ref {exz0z}}}{{=}}\nu_{2}(-r,\omega^{-1})\tfrac{\overline{s}(\omega^{-1})}{\overline{s}((\omega^{-1})^{h_{-r}})}\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&-r\\
r^{-1}&0\end{pmatrix},\omega^{-1})

\displaystyle=1\cdot(-i)\cdot 1=(e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}})^{2}.

∎

Consequently we obtain semi-direct-product decompositions:

\overline{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\cong\bigl(\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\bigr)\ltimes\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\quad\widetilde{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\cong\bigl(\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\bigr)\ltimes\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\quad\widehat{\operatorname{PGL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\cong\bigl(\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\bigr)\ltimes\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

If
r\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}^{\times}
we may work over
\mathbb{Q}
and set

\overline{\operatorname{PGL}}^{\pm r}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})=\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\ltimes\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}),\quad\widetilde{\operatorname{PGL}}^{\pm r}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})=\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\ltimes\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}),\quad\widehat{\operatorname{PGL}}^{\pm r}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})=\langle-r\rangle/\langle r^{2}\rangle\ltimes\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}).

Let
h\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

•

If
\det h>0
, write

h=\sqrt{\det h}\;\bigl[\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\det h}}h\bigr]\quad\text{with}\quad\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\det h}}h\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

•

If
\det h<0
, write

h=\underbrace{\sqrt{-(\det h)r^{-1}}}_{h_{1}}\cdot\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
r&0\end{pmatrix}}_{h_{2}}\cdot\underbrace{\Big[\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{-(\det h)r^{-1}}}\begin{pmatrix}0&r^{-1}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}h\Big]}_{h_{3}}\quad\text{with}\quad h_{3}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Now consider
h\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. If
r\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}
and
\det h\in-r\mathbb{Q}^{\times 2}
, then in the above decomposition, all factors
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
, and
h_{3}
belong to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Let
\overline{h}=(h,t_{1})\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widetilde{h}=(h,t_{2})\in\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widehat{h}=(h,t_{3})\in\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

•

If
\det h>0
, write

\overline{h}=[\sqrt{\det h},1]\cdot\bigl[\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\det h}}h,t_{1}\bigr],\quad\widetilde{h}=[\sqrt{\det h},1]\cdot\bigl[\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\det h}}h,t_{2}\bigr],\quad\widehat{h}=[\sqrt{\det h},1]\cdot\bigl[\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\det h}}h,t_{3}\bigr].

•

If
\det h<0
, write:

\overline{h}=[h_{1},1]\cdot[h_{2},1]\cdot[h_{3},\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{3})^{-1}t_{1}],

\widetilde{h}=[h_{1},1]\cdot[h_{2},e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{e})}{4}}]\cdot[h_{3},e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{e})}{4}}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{3})^{-1}t_{2}],

\widehat{h}=[h_{1},1]\cdot[h_{2},e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}]\cdot[h_{3},e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\widehat{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2},h_{3})^{-1}t_{3}].

3.11.
Automorphic factor II

Consider the left action of
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
on
\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
by
h(z)=\tfrac{az+b}{cz+d}
, for
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
z\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
. This action is well-defined and transitive.
Restricting to the subgroup
\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, the stabilizer of
i
is just the group
\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and there exists a bijective map:

i:\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})/\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{\pm};[g]\longmapsto gi.

Note that
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})=P_{>0}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
P_{>0}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\cap\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})=1
.
So there exists a bijective map:

\displaystyle i^{\pm}:P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{\pm};g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&(\det g)a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\longmapsto ab\det g+ia^{2}\det g.

(3.23)

For
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, let us write
g=p_{g}k_{g}
, for
p_{g}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
and
k_{g}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then:

Lemma 3.28
.

p_{g}=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}&\det g\tfrac{bd+ac}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}\\
0&\det g\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}\end{pmatrix}
,
k_{g}=\det g\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{d}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}&-\tfrac{c}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}\\
\tfrac{c}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}&\tfrac{d}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}\end{pmatrix}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}\tfrac{d\det g}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}+(\tfrac{bd+ac}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}})\tfrac{c}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{d\det g}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}+\tfrac{cbd+ac^{2}}{c^{2}+d^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{dad-dbc+cbd+ac^{2}}{c^{2}+d^{2}}=a;

\displaystyle(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}})\tfrac{-c\det g}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}+(\tfrac{bd+ac}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}})\tfrac{d}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-c\det g}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}-\tfrac{bd^{2}+acd}{c^{2}+d^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-cad+bc^{2}+bd^{2}+acd}{c^{2}+d^{2}}=b.

∎

For
z\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
, let us write
p_{z}
for the corresponding element in
P_{>0}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
via the map
i^{\pm}
. Until the end of this subsection, we let
h=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
h_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{i}&b_{i}\\
c_{i}&d_{i}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
z\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
.
Define:

(1)

J(h,z)=cz+d
;

(2)

\alpha_{z}(h)=\tfrac{(cz+d)}{|cz+d|}
;

(3)

J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h,z)=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h,z)|^{1/2}
;

(4)

J_{1/2}(h,z)=J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h,z)\cdot\widetilde{s}(h)
;

(5)

J_{3/2}(h,z)=J_{1/2}(h,z)J(h,z)
.

The restriction of the above notions to
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
is comparable to that given in Section
2.6
above. It can be checked that
J(h_{1}h_{2},z)=J(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J(h_{2},z)
.

Lemma 3.29
.

(1)

\overline{s}(h)^{2}=\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)
.

(2)

[\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})]^{2}=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{1})\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{2})\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}
.

Proof.

1) Note that
\overline{s}(h)=\overline{s}(h_{\det h}h)
. So
[\overline{s}(h)]^{2}=[\overline{s}(h_{\det h}h)]^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref {eqalpha}}}{{=}}\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{\det h}h)=\tfrac{(\det h)ci+(\det h)d}{|(\det h)ci+(\det h)d|}=\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)
.

2)

Left side

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {Cz_0wid}}}{{=}}[\overline{s}(h_{1})]^{2}[\overline{s}(h_{2})]^{2}[\overline{s}(h_{1}h_{2})]^{-2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{1})\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{2})\alpha_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\text{Right side}.

∎

Lemma 3.30
.

(1)

J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)=J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h_{2},z)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

(2)

J_{1/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)=J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

(3)

J_{1/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}=J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2})^{-1}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

Proof.

1)

|J(h_{1}h_{2},z)|^{1/2}=|J(h_{1},h_{2}z)|^{1/2}|J(h_{2},z)|^{1/2};

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2}p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z}).

If
h_{i}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2}p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},p_{h_{2}p_{z}}k_{h_{2}p_{z}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},p_{h_{2}p_{z}}k_{h_{2}p_{z}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(p_{h_{2}p_{z}},k_{h_{2}p_{z}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},p_{h_{2}p_{z}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}p_{h_{2}p_{z}},k_{h_{2}p_{z}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},p_{h_{2}(z)})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1};

Right side

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},p_{h_{2}(z)})^{-1}\cdot|J(h_{1},z)|^{1/2}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h_{2},z)|^{1/2}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1}h_{2},p_{z})^{-1}|J(h_{1}h_{2},z)|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\text{Left side}.

In general, write
h_{i}=|\det(h_{i})|^{1/2}g_{i}
, with
g_{i}=|\det(h_{i})|^{-1/2}h_{i}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
.

J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h_{i},z)=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{i},p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(g_{i},z)|^{1/2}\cdot|\det(h_{i})|^{1/4}.

Right side

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{1},p_{h_{2}(z)})^{-1}\cdot|J(g_{1},z)|^{1/2}\cdot|\det(h_{1})|^{1/4}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{2},p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(g_{2},z)|^{1/2}\cdot|\det(h_{2})|^{1/4}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{1},p_{g_{2}(z)})^{-1}\cdot|J(g_{1},z)|^{1/2}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{2},p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(g_{2},z)|^{1/2}\cdot|\det(h_{1}h_{2})|^{1/4}\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=J^{\prime}_{1/2}(g_{1}g_{2},z)\cdot|\det(h_{1}h_{2})|^{1/4}=\text{Left side}.

2) It follows from Lemma
3.16
.

3) It follows from the equality (
3.11
).
∎

As a consequence, we have:

Lemma 3.31
.

(1)

J^{\prime}_{3/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)=J^{\prime}_{3/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J^{\prime}_{3/2}(h_{2},z)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

(2)

J_{3/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)=J_{3/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{3/2}(h_{2},z)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

(3)

J_{3/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}=J_{3/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{3/2}(h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2})^{-1}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

Lemma 3.32
.

J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h,z)^{2}=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)^{-1}J(h,z)
.

Proof.

Left side

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-2}\cdot|J(h,z)|

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)^{-1}\alpha_{z_{0}}(p_{z})^{-1}\alpha_{z_{0}}(hp_{z})\cdot|J(h,z)|

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)^{-1}|J(h,z)|\Big[\tfrac{J(p_{z},i)}{|J(p_{z},i)|}\Big]^{-1}\tfrac{J(hp_{z},i)}{|J(hp_{z},i)|}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)^{-1}|J(h,z)|\tfrac{J(h,z)}{|J(h,z)|}

\displaystyle=\text{Right side}.

∎

Lemma 3.33
.

[J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}]^{2}=\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)\cdot(cz+d)
.

Proof.

\text{Left side}=[J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h,z)\widetilde{s}(h)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}]^{2}=[J^{\prime}_{1/2}(h,z)\overline{s}(h)]^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {sgnalpha}}}{{=}}\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)^{-1}J(h,z)\cdot\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)\alpha_{z_{0}}(h)=\text{Right side}
.
∎

Lemma 3.34
.

(1)

For
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&\det(p)a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
,
J_{1/2}(p,z)=a^{-1/2}
.

(2)

For
k=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
J_{1/2}(k,z_{0})=\widetilde{s}(k)
and
J_{1/2}(k,\overline{z_{0}})=\widetilde{s}(k)(-bi+a)
.

Proof.

1)

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(p,z)

\displaystyle=J^{\prime}_{1/2}(p,z)\cdot\widetilde{s}(p)

\displaystyle=\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(p,p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(p,z)|^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{s}(p)

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(p,p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(p)^{-1}\overline{s}(p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(pp_{z})|J(p,z)|^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{s}(p)

\displaystyle=|J(p,z)|^{1/2}\cdot\overline{s}(p)m_{X^{\ast}}(p)

\displaystyle=|\det(p)a^{-1}|^{1/2}.

2) By Remark
2.22
,
J_{1/2}(k,z_{0})=\widetilde{s}(k)
. Then

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(h_{-1}kh_{-1},z_{0})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{-1},kh_{-1}z_{0})J_{1/2}(kh_{-1},z_{0})\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},kh_{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{-1},kh_{-1}z_{0})J_{1/2}(k,h_{-1}z_{0})J_{1/2}(h_{-1},z_{0})\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},kh_{-1})\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})m_{X^{\ast}}(kh_{-1})m_{X^{\ast}}(k)^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})m_{X^{\ast}}(k)^{-1}.

J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})=J_{1/2}(k^{-1},z_{0})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(k).

If
b\neq 0
, then
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(k,h_{-1})=1
,
J_{1/2}(k^{-1},z_{0})=\widetilde{s}(k^{-1})=m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})\overline{s}(k^{-1})=m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})\overline{s}(k)(-bi+a)
.
Therefore,

J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})=m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})\overline{s}(k)(-bi+a)m_{X^{\ast}}(k^{-1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(k)=\overline{s}(k)m_{X^{\ast}}(k)(-bi+a)=\widetilde{s}(k)(-bi+a).

If
b=0
,
a=-1
, then
k=-I
and

J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})=J_{1/2}(-I,z_{0})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(-I,h_{-1})^{-1}=-\widetilde{s}(-I).

If
b=0
,
a=1
, then
k=I
and

J_{1/2}(k,-z_{0})=J_{1/2}(I,z_{0})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(I,h_{-1})^{-1}=\widetilde{s}(I).

∎

Lemma 3.35
.

\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)\cdot(cz+d)}=J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}
.

Proof.

(1) Let
h\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. If
z\in\mathbb{H}
, then the result holds by Remark
2.27
.

If
z\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
, and
c\neq 0
, then

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,h_{-1})=1=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},hh_{-1})=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},h_{-1}p_{z})=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},hp_{z})=1;

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}},h_{-1}p_{z})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,h_{-1})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},hh_{-1})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}},h_{-1}p_{z})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,h_{-1})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(hh_{-1},h_{-1}p_{z})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},hp_{z})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},h_{-1}p_{z})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},hp_{z})=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z}).

\displaystyle\sqrt{cz+d}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{(-c)(-z)+d}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h^{h_{-1}},-z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(h^{h_{-1}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h^{h_{-1}},p_{-z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h^{h_{-1}},-z)|^{1/2}m_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(h^{h_{-1}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h^{h_{-1}},h_{-1}p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h^{h_{-1}},-z)|^{1/2}m_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h^{h_{-1}})\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h^{h_{-1}},h_{-1}p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h^{h_{-1}},-z)|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h^{h_{-1}})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h^{h_{-1}},h_{-1}p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(h^{h_{-1}})^{-1}\overline{s}(h_{-1}p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(h_{-1}hp_{z})

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(hp_{z})|J(h,z)|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\overline{s}(h)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h,z)|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}.

If
z\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
, and
c=0
,
a>0,d>0
, then
h\in P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
and

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(h)^{-1}\overline{s}(p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(hp_{z})=1.

Then:

J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}=\overline{s}(h)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h,z)|^{1/2}=d^{1/2}=\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}.

If
z\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
, and
c=0
,
a<0,d<0
, then
-h\in P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
. Then:

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}=-1,\quad\overline{s}(h)^{-1}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{2}},\quad\overline{s}(p_{z})^{-1}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{2}},\quad\overline{s}(hp_{z})=1;

\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(h)^{-1}\overline{s}(p_{z})^{-1}\overline{s}(hp_{z})=1;

J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}=\overline{s}(h)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h,p_{z})^{-1}\cdot|J(h,z)|^{1/2}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{2}}|d|^{1/2}=\sqrt{d}=\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}.

(2) If
h\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\det h>0
, then
h=|\det h|^{1/2}(|\det h|^{-1/2}h)
.

Right side

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(|\det h|^{1/2},|\det h|^{-1/2}hz)J_{1/2}(|\det h|^{-1/2}h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(|\det h|^{1/2},|\det h|^{-1/2}h)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(|\det h|^{1/2},|\det h|^{-1/2}hz)J_{1/2}(|\det h|^{-1/2}h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(|\det h|^{-1/2}h)^{-1}

\displaystyle=|\det h|^{1/4}\sqrt{|\det h|^{-1/2}\cdot(cz+d)}

\displaystyle=\text{Left side}.

(3) If
h\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\det h<0
, then
h=h_{-1}[h_{-1}h]
.

Right side

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{-1},h_{-1}hz)J_{1/2}(h_{-1}h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},h_{-1}h)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{-1},h_{-1}hz)J_{1/2}(h_{-1}h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1}h)^{-1}

\displaystyle=1^{-1/2}\cdot\sqrt{-cz-d}

\displaystyle=\text{Left side}.

∎

Let
h_{3}=h_{1}h_{2}
.

Lemma 3.36
.

\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h_{3})(c_{3}z+d_{3})}=\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h_{1})(c_{1}(h_{2}z)+d_{1})}\cdot\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h_{2})(c_{2}z+d_{2})}\cdot\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})
.

Proof.

Left side

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref {J121J1}}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2})^{-1}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{Right side}.

∎

3.12.
Automorphic factor plus

For
\overline{h}=(h,\epsilon)\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
or
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}^{\mu_{8}}(\mathbb{R})
, define:

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\epsilon^{-1}J_{1/2}(h,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h)^{-1}=\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)},

\displaystyle J_{3/2}(\overline{h},z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)\,J(h,z)=\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}(cz+d).

Lemma 3.37
.

For every
\overline{h}_{1},\overline{h}_{2}\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

(1)

J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{1}\overline{h}_{2},z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{1},h_{2}z)\,J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{2},z)
,

(2)

J_{3/2}(\overline{h}_{1}\overline{h}_{2},z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{h}_{1},h_{2}z)\,J_{3/2}(\overline{h}_{2},z)
.

Proof.

Note that
\overline{h}_{1}\overline{h}_{2}=[h_{1}h_{2},\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}]
.

\displaystyle 1)\quad\text{Left side}

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-1}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\epsilon_{2}^{-1}J_{1/2}(h_{1}h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {J121J1}}}{{=}}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-1}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\epsilon_{2}^{-1}J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1}h_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {relacto}(3)}}{{=}}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-1}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}\epsilon_{2}^{-1}J_{1/2}(h_{1},h_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(h_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(h_{2})^{-1}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{Right side}.

\displaystyle 2)\quad\text{Left side}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{1}\overline{h}_{2},z)J(h_{1}h_{2},z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{1},h_{2}z)\,J_{1/2}(\overline{h}_{2},z)J(h_{1},h_{2}z)J(h_{2},z)

\displaystyle=\text{Right side}.

∎

3.13.
The Weil-Deligne group

Recall the Weil group or the Weil-Deligne group
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
for
\mathbb{R}
from
[
17
]
or
[
33
]
. It can be seen as a subgroup of
\operatorname{SU}(2)
generated by

\{\begin{pmatrix}z&0\\
0&\overline{z}\end{pmatrix}\mid z\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}\},\qquad\qquad\omega_{\sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

We identify
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
as a subgroup of
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
. We decompose
\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
as
\langle h_{-1}\rangle\ltimes\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 3.38
.

There exists a group monomorphism
\iota
from
\overline{\operatorname{O}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
to
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
by sending
[h_{-1},i]
to
\omega_{\sigma}
,
[1,\epsilon]
to
\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon^{-1}&0\\
0&\overline{\epsilon^{-1}}\end{pmatrix}
and
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
to
\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{a+bi}&0\\
0&\overline{\sqrt{a+bi}}\end{pmatrix}
.

Proof.

1)
[h_{-1},i][h_{-1},i]=[1,-\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},h_{-1})]=[1,-\nu_{2}(-1,I)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(I,I)]=[1,-1]
. So the restriction of
\iota
to
\langle[h_{-1},i]\rangle
is a group morphism.

2) Note that the maps
f_{1}:\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C});\overline{g}\longmapsto J_{1/2}(\overline{g},z_{0})
and
f_{2}:\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C});[g,\epsilon]\longmapsto\overline{J_{1/2}(\overline{g},z_{0})}
both are group morphisms.

3)

\displaystyle[h_{-1},i][g,1][h_{-1},-i]

\displaystyle=[g^{h_{-1}},\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},g)\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1}g,h_{-1})]

\displaystyle=[g^{h_{-1}},\nu_{2}(1,1)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(1,g)\nu_{2}(-1,g)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g^{h_{-1}},1)]

\displaystyle=[g^{h_{-1}},\nu_{2}(-1,g)].

We write
u=a+bi=e^{i\tau_{u}}
with
-\pi\leq\tau_{u}<\pi
. If
b\neq 0
, we know that
\nu_{2}(-1,g)=1
. If
b=0
and
a=1
, then
\nu_{2}(-1,g)=1
. If
b=0
and
a=-1
, then
\nu_{2}(-1,g)=-1
.

J_{1/2}([g^{h_{-1}},\nu_{2}(-1,g)],z_{0})=\sqrt{a-bi}\nu_{2}(-1,g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}e^{-i\tfrac{\tau_{u}}{2}}&-\pi<\tau_{u}<\pi,\\
-e^{i\tfrac{\tau_{u}}{2}}&\tau_{u}=-\pi,\end{array}\right.=e^{-i\tfrac{\tau_{u}}{2}}=\overline{\sqrt{a+bi}}.

∎

By restriction, we also embed
\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
into
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
. In particular, on
\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, the embedding sends
[g,\epsilon]
to
\epsilon^{-1}\overline{s}(g)=\epsilon\overline{s}(g)
.

4.
Congruence subgroups

In this section, we write
p
for an odd prime number and assume
\psi=\psi_{0}
.

4.1.
Congruence subgroups of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

In
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, we consider the following subgroups:

\Gamma(N)=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}(\bmod N)\},

\Gamma(2N,N)=\{g\in\Gamma(N)\mid a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 2N),b\equiv c\equiv 0(\bmod N)\}\quad(\,N\textrm{ even}),

\Gamma(p,p)=\{g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid a\equiv d(\bmod p),b\equiv c\equiv 0(\bmod p)\},

\Gamma_{0}(N)=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
0&\ast\end{pmatrix}(\bmod N)\},

\Gamma^{0}(N)=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\ast&0\\
\ast&\ast\end{pmatrix}(\bmod N)\}.

Let us choose
c,d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
-pc+4d=1
. Then:

\begin{pmatrix}pc&d\\
-4p&-p\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}4&1\\
pc&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}c&d\\
-4&-p\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix};

\Gamma_{0}(4)\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\Gamma_{0}(4)\begin{pmatrix}c&d\\
-4&-p\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\Gamma_{0}(4)\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}4&1\\
pc&d\end{pmatrix}.

For elements in
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, we use the following notations:

g_{-p}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
p&0\end{pmatrix},g_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},r_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&x\end{pmatrix}\textrm{ for }1\leq x\leq p,r_{0}=\begin{pmatrix}4&1\\
pc&d\end{pmatrix}.

Then the following results can be checked:

(1)

g_{p}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}^{-1}=g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}
,
[g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}]\cap\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\Gamma_{0}(p)
,
g_{p}^{-1}\Gamma_{0}(p)g_{p}=\Gamma_{0}(p)
.

(2)

g_{-p}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})(g_{-p})^{-1}=(g_{-p})^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{-p}
,
[(g_{-p})^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{-p}]\cap\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\Gamma_{0}(p)
,
(g_{-p})^{-1}\Gamma_{0}(p)g_{-p}=\Gamma_{0}(p)
.

(3)

\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\mathop{\sqcup}_{0\leq x\leq p}\Gamma_{0}(p)r_{x}
,
g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}=\mathop{\sqcup}_{0\leq x\leq p}\Gamma_{0}(p)g_{p}^{-1}r_{x}g_{p}
and
(g_{-p})^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{-p}=\mathop{\sqcup}_{0\leq x\leq p}\Gamma_{0}(p)(g_{-p})^{-1}r_{x}g_{-p}
.

(4)

\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus[\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})]=\mathop{\sqcup}_{0\leq x\leq p}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}r_{x}
.

(5)

If
N
is odd,
\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid c\equiv 0(\bmod N),ab\equiv 0(\bmod 2),cd\equiv 0(\bmod 2)\}
.

(6)

If
N
is even,
\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mid c\equiv 0(\bmod N),a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 2),b\equiv 0(\bmod 2)\}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

\bigcap_{1\leq x\leq p}r_{x}^{-1}g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\,g_{p}r_{x}=r_{x}^{-1}\Gamma(p,p)r_{x}.

Proof.

Recall that

g_{p}r_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
0&p\end{pmatrix},\quad(g_{p}r_{x})^{-1}=\frac{1}{p}\begin{pmatrix}p&-x\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

A direct computation shows:

\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
0&p\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
0&p\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a-\dfrac{cx}{p}&ax+bp-\dfrac{cx^{2}}{p}-dx\\[6.0pt]
\dfrac{c}{p}&\dfrac{cx}{p}+d\end{pmatrix}.

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
and
g^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a^{\prime}&b^{\prime}\\
c^{\prime}&d^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}
be elements of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, and let
1\leq x\neq x^{\prime}\leq p
. Then:

(g_{p}r_{x})^{-1}g\,g_{p}r_{x}=(g_{p}r_{x^{\prime}})^{-1}g^{\prime}\,g_{p}r_{x^{\prime}}

if and only if the following two cases hold:

Case (1):
c\neq 0
.

The equality is equivalent to the system:

\begin{cases}a+d=a^{\prime}+d^{\prime},&\text{(1)}\\[2.0pt]
a-\dfrac{cx}{p}=a^{\prime}-\dfrac{c^{\prime}x^{\prime}}{p},&\text{(2)}\\[2.0pt]
c=c^{\prime},&\text{(3)}\\[2.0pt]
\dfrac{ad-1}{c}\in\mathbb{Z},&\text{(4)}\\[2.0pt]
\dfrac{a^{\prime}d^{\prime}-1}{c}\in\mathbb{Z},&\text{(5)}\\[2.0pt]
a,c,d,a^{\prime},d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}.&\text{(6)}\end{cases}

Case (2):
c=0
.

Then:

\begin{cases}a=d=a^{\prime}=d^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\},\\
b=b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}.\end{cases}

We now analyze
Case (1)
in detail. From (2) and (3), write
c=pc_{1}
. Then (2) becomes:

a-c_{1}x=a^{\prime}-c_{1}x^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad a^{\prime}=a-c_{1}(x-x^{\prime}).

From (1),
d^{\prime}=d+c_{1}(x-x^{\prime})
.
Now consider the expression:

\frac{a^{\prime}d^{\prime}-1}{c}=\frac{ad-1}{c}+\frac{\big[(a-d)-c_{1}(x-x^{\prime})\big](x-x^{\prime})}{p}.

Suppose
(g_{p}r_{x})^{-1}g\,g_{p}r_{x}\in\bigcap_{1\leq y\leq p}r_{y}^{-1}g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\,g_{p}r_{y}
.
Then for all
1\leq x^{\prime}\leq p
, the above expression must be an integer.
This implies:

\frac{\big[(a-d)-c_{1}(x-x^{\prime})\big](x-x^{\prime})}{p}\in\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{for all }x^{\prime}.

In particular, for varying
x^{\prime}
, this forces:

\begin{cases}a\equiv d\pmod{p},\\
p\mid c_{1}.\end{cases}

Conversely, suppose
a\equiv d\pmod{p}
and
p^{2}\mid c
.
Define:

\begin{cases}a^{\prime}=a-\dfrac{c}{p}(x-x^{\prime}),\\
c^{\prime}=c,\\
d^{\prime}=d+\dfrac{c}{p}(x-x^{\prime}),\\
b^{\prime}=b+\left[\dfrac{a-d}{p}-\dfrac{c(x-x^{\prime})}{p^{2}}\right](x-x^{\prime}).\end{cases}

Then one verifies that
g^{\prime}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and

(g_{p}r_{x})^{-1}g\,g_{p}r_{x}=(g_{p}r_{x^{\prime}})^{-1}g^{\prime}\,g_{p}r_{x^{\prime}}.

Finally, observe that

g_{p}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}g_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}d&-bp\\[2.0pt]
-\dfrac{c}{p}&a\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\bigcap_{1\leq x\leq p}r_{x}^{-1}g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\,g_{p}r_{x}=r_{x}^{-1}\big[\Gamma(p,p)\big]r_{x},

since the intersection is independent of the choice of
x\in\{1,\dots,p\}
.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

(1)

\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\Gamma_{0}(p)\sqcup\Gamma_{0}(p)s\Gamma_{0}(p)
, for any
s\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus\Gamma_{0}(p)
.

(2)

g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}=\Gamma_{0}(p)\sqcup\Gamma_{0}(p)s\Gamma_{0}(p)
, for any
s\in g_{p}^{-1}\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})g_{p}\setminus\Gamma_{0}(p)
.

Proof.

1)
\Gamma_{0}(p)r_{0}\Gamma_{0}(p)=\Gamma_{0}(p)
. For
1\leq x\leq p
, we have:

\Gamma_{0}(p)r_{x}\Gamma_{0}(p)=\Gamma_{0}(p)\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&x\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\Gamma_{0}(p)=\Gamma_{0}(p)\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\Gamma_{0}(p).

2) It is a consequence of Part (1).
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
N
be an odd positive integer number.

(1)

The canonical map
\iota:\bigl(\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)\backslash\Gamma_{0}(N)\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\theta}\backslash\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

is bijective.

(2)

The canonical map
\iota:\bigl(\Gamma(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)\backslash\Gamma(N)\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\theta}\backslash\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

is bijective.

Proof.

1) Write
G=\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}
.
For
x,y\in\Gamma_{0}(N)
, the equality
Gx=Gy
gives
xy^{-1}\in G\subseteq\Gamma_{\theta}
, hence
\Gamma_{\theta}x=\Gamma_{\theta}y
; thus
\iota
is well-defined.
Conversely, if
\iota(Gx)=\iota(Gy)
, then
\Gamma_{\theta}x=\Gamma_{\theta}y
, so
xy^{-1}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
.
Because
x,y\in\Gamma_{0}(p)
, we also have
xy^{-1}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)
, hence
xy^{-1}\in G
and
Gx=Gy
; therefore
\iota
is injective.

To see surjectivity, recall that
|\Gamma_{\theta}\backslash\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})|=3
.
The three cosets are represented by

\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-N&1\end{pmatrix}.

Each of these matrices lies in
\Gamma_{0}(N)
, so their
G
-cosets map under
\iota
to the three distinct cosets of
\Gamma_{\theta}
.
Consequently
\iota
is surjective and hence bijective.

2) The proof is analogous, using the coset representatives:

\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&N\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-N&1\end{pmatrix}.

∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
N
be an even positive integer number. Then
\Gamma_{0}(N)=\bigl(\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)\sqcup\bigl(\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)u(1)
.

Proof.

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{0}(N)
. Note that
2\nmid a
and
2\nmid d
. If
2\mid b
, then
g\in\bigl(\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)
. If
2\nmid b
, then
gu(-1)=\begin{pmatrix}a&b-a\\
c&d-c\end{pmatrix}
;
2\mid(b-a)
and
gu(-1)\in\bigl(\Gamma_{0}(N)\cap\Gamma_{\theta}\bigr)
.
∎

4.2.
One-dimensional representations of
\Gamma_{\theta},\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta},\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}

For
r\in\Gamma_{\theta}
, we define

\displaystyle\lambda(r)=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r).

(4.1)

Lemma 4.5
.

\lambda(r_{1})\lambda(r_{2})=\lambda(r_{1}r_{2})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})
, for
r_{i}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.19
(1),
\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(r_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1}r_{2})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})
. Consequently,

\displaystyle m_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1})\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(r_{2})\widetilde{\beta}(r_{2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1})m_{X^{\ast}}(r_{2})\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1}r_{2})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

∎

For
\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}
, we define:

\widetilde{\lambda}:\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T};\widetilde{r}\longmapsto t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r).

Lemma 4.6
.

\widetilde{\lambda}
is a character of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{s}=(s,t_{s})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. Then
\widetilde{r}\widetilde{s}=[rs,t_{r}t_{s}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(t_{r},t_{s})]
.

\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r}\widetilde{s})=t_{r}t_{s}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,s)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(rs)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref {spgamma12}}}{{=}}t_{r}t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(s)=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r})\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s}).

∎

Recall the isomorphism:

\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Im}(\iota)(\subseteq\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta});[r,\epsilon]\longmapsto[r,m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\epsilon].

Let
\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. Let us define:

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota:\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T};[r,\epsilon]\longmapsto\epsilon m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r).

(4.2)

Lemma 4.7
.

\overline{\lambda}(r,\epsilon)=\lambda(r)\epsilon
, for
(r,\epsilon)\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
.

Proof.

\overline{\lambda}(r,\epsilon)=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\epsilon\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)=\epsilon\lambda(r)
.
∎

Note:

\Gamma_{\theta}=\Gamma(2)\sqcup\Gamma(2)\omega.

If
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
, we let
\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)
denote the Kronecker symbol, and define
\left(\frac{0}{1}\right)=1
,
\left(\frac{0}{-1}\right)=-1
.
1
1

1

Our definition differs from Shimura’s
[
29
]
in that we take
\operatorname{Arg}:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\to[-\pi,\pi)
instead of
(-\pi,\pi]
in
[
29
]
.
Following
[
29
]
, let
\epsilon_{d}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4),\\
i&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

Lemma 4.8
.

\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d},&\textrm{ if }r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2),\\
\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-c,d)_{\mathbb{R}},&\textrm{ if }r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\omega.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

a) Let
r\in\Gamma(2)
.

(1)

If
c=0
,
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}(d))}
and
\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)=1
, then
\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}
.

(2 i)

If
c\neq 0
,
\operatorname{sgn}d>0
and
d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
, then
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
and
\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}
.

(2 ii)

If
c\neq 0
,
\operatorname{sgn}d<0
and
d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
, then
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=(-i)\left(\frac{2c}{-d}\right)e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
and

\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}i\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\operatorname{sgn}(c)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}.

(2 iii)

If
c\neq 0
,
\operatorname{sgn}d>0
and
d\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
, then
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=i\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
and

\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}(-i)\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}=(-i)\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}.

(2 iv)

If
c\neq 0
,
\operatorname{sgn}d<0
and
d\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
, then
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=\left(\frac{2c}{-d}\right)e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
and

\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\operatorname{sgn}(c)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}=(-i)\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}.

b)
m_{X^{\ast}}(\omega)=e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}
,
\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\omega)=1
,
\overline{\lambda}([\omega,1])=e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}
.

\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega)=(x(r),x(\omega))_{\mathbb{R}}\,(-x(r)x(\omega),x(r\omega))_{\mathbb{R}}=(-x(r)x(\omega),x(r\omega))_{\mathbb{R}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(-c,d)_{\mathbb{R}}&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0,\\
(-d,d)_{\mathbb{R}}=1&\textrm{ if }c=0.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}([r_{1}\omega,1])

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}([r_{1},1])\overline{\lambda}([\omega,1])\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},\omega)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{\lambda}([r_{1},1])\overline{\lambda}([\omega,1])\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},\omega)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon_{d}^{-1}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-c,d)_{\mathbb{R}}.

∎

Remark 4.9
.

The function defined above coincides with the multiplier system
\nu
appearing in
[
7
, Prop.2.3.3(b)]
.

Proof.

By direct inspection, the two expressions agree when
r\in\Gamma(2)
. It therefore suffices to verify the remaining case where
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\omega
. We factor
r
as
r=r_{1}\omega
, with

r_{1}=r\omega^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-b&a\\
-d&c\end{pmatrix}.

Consequently, we obtain:

\overline{\lambda}([r,1])=\left(\frac{-2d}{c}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{c}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(d,c)_{\mathbb{R}}=\left(\frac{2d}{c}\right)[\left(\frac{-1}{c}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{c}]e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(d,c)_{\mathbb{R}}=\left(\frac{2d}{c}\right)\epsilon_{c}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(c,d)_{\mathbb{R}},

which recovers precisely the formula given in
[
7
, Prop. 2.3.3(b)]
.
∎

4.3.
Two-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta},\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}

Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta},\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}
denote the corresponding covering subgroups of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}),\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})
respectively. Let us define:

\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}}\widetilde{\lambda},\qquad\quad\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}}\overline{\lambda}.

Lemma 4.10
.

Both
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
,
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}
are irreducible representations of two dimension.

Proof.

1) Take the element
r=\begin{pmatrix}3&4\\
2&3\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\subseteq\Gamma_{\theta}
and set
\widetilde{r}=[r,1]
. Then:

[h_{-1},1]^{-1}=[h_{-1},\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}([-1,1],[-1,1])^{-1}]=[h_{-1},1],

\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},r)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1}r,h_{-1})=\nu(1,1)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(1,r)\nu(-1,r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r^{h_{-1}},1)=\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=i,

[h_{-1},1]\widetilde{r}[h_{-1},1]=[h_{-1}rh_{-1},\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},r)\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1}r,h_{-1})]=[h_{-1}rh_{-1},i].

Consequently,

\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r})=\lambda\!\begin{pmatrix}3&4\\
2&3\end{pmatrix}=\Bigl(\frac{4}{3}\Bigr)\epsilon^{-1}_{3}=-i,

whereas

\widetilde{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\widetilde{r})=\widetilde{\lambda}\!\left([h_{-1},1]\widetilde{r}[h_{-1},1]\right)=\lambda\!\begin{pmatrix}3&-4\\
-2&3\end{pmatrix}i=\Bigl(\frac{-4}{3}\Bigr)\epsilon^{-1}_{3}i=-1.

Hence
\widetilde{\lambda}\neq\widetilde{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}
, proving that
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
is irreducible.

2) Recall the isomorphism
\overline{\mathrm{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\xrightarrow{\;\iota\;}\widetilde{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
from (
2.10
) and note that
\overline{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\leq\overline{\mathrm{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Because
\overline{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota
, we have
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}=\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}\circ\iota
, and the irreducibility of
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
immediately implies that of
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}
.
∎

4.4.
Finite-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

Recall:

u(n)=\begin{pmatrix}1&n\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad u_{-}(-n)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-n&1\end{pmatrix},\quad 1=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

Let
N
be an odd positive integer number.

Definition 4.11
.

(1)

In
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, define
M_{q_{1}}=u(N)
,
M_{q_{2}}=1
,
M_{q_{3}}=u_{-}(-N)
, and set
\mathcal{M}_{2}=\{\widetilde{M}_{q_{1}},\widetilde{M}_{q_{2}},\widetilde{M}_{q_{3}}\}
.

(2)

In
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, define
\widetilde{M}_{q_{1}}=[u(N),1],\widetilde{M}_{q_{2}}=[1,1],\widetilde{M}_{q_{3}}=[u_{-}(-N),1],
and set
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{2}=\{\widetilde{M}_{q_{1}},\widetilde{M}_{q_{2}},\widetilde{M}_{q_{3}}\}
.

(3)

In
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, define
\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[u(N),1],\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[1,1],\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[u_{-}(-N),1],
and set
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2}=\{\overline{M}_{q_{1}},\overline{M}_{q_{2}},\overline{M}_{q_{3}}\}
.

Then:

\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}\Gamma_{\theta}M_{q_{i}}=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}M_{q_{i}}\Gamma_{\theta}.

\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}\widetilde{M}_{q_{i}}=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}\widetilde{M}_{q_{i}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}.

\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}=\sqcup^{3}_{i=1}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}.

Definition 4.12
.

(1)

\widetilde{\gamma}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\widetilde{M}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

(2)

\overline{\gamma}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\lambda}^{-1},\overline{M}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

Proposition 4.13
.

(1)

\widetilde{\gamma}
is an irreducible representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

(2)

\overline{\gamma}
is an irreducible representation of
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

1) Let us write
G=\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
H=\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}
.
Then
G=H\sqcup[HsH]
, for any
s\notin H
. For such
s
, write
H_{s}=s^{-1}Hs
, and set
(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1})^{s}(\widetilde{r})=\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}(s\widetilde{r}s^{-1})
, for
\widetilde{r}\in H_{s}\cap H
. Then:

\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\gamma})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{H}(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{H}(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}\oplus\operatorname{Ind}_{H_{s}\cap H}^{H}(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1})^{s})

\simeq\mathbb{C}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{H_{s}\cap H}(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1})^{s})\simeq\mathbb{C}\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{H_{s}\cap H}(\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\lambda}^{s}).

In particular, we consider
s=u(1)
.

\widetilde{\lambda}:\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\longmapsto t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r),

\widetilde{\lambda}^{s}:\widetilde{r}^{s^{-1}}=(r^{s^{-1}},t_{r})\longmapsto t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r^{s^{-1}}).

Let us check the element
u_{-}(-2)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-2&1\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\subseteq\Gamma_{\theta}\subseteq\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Then

r=s^{-1}u_{-}(-2)s=\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-2&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}3&2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix}\in H_{s}\cap H.

\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r^{s^{-1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. \ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}},\quad\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. \ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

Hence
\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\gamma})\simeq\mathbb{C}
.

2)
Recall the isomorphism
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
from (
2.10
). It follows from that
\overline{\gamma}=\widetilde{\gamma}\circ\iota
, so the result holds.
∎

4.5.
Finite-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
II

For
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, write
g=rM_{q}
, for unique
r\in\Gamma_{\theta}
and
M_{q}\in\mathcal{M}_{2}
. Define the function:

f:\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\longmapsto\mu_{8};\quad g\longmapsto\widetilde{\beta}(r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q}).

We modify the cocycle
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}
by
f
to obtain a new cocycle:

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})f(g_{1})f(g_{2})f(g_{1}g_{2})^{-1}.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
r,r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
and
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Then
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=1=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})
.

Proof.

1) By Lem.
2.19
,
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})=1
.

2) If
g=M_{q}
, then

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q})f(rM_{q})^{-1}=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})\widetilde{\beta}(r)1\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})^{-1}=1.

3) For general
g
, write
g=r_{g}M_{q}
. Then:

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g}M_{q})=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g}M_{q})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{g},M_{q})=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rr_{g},M_{q})=1.

∎

Lemma 4.15
.

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,-)
defines a character of
\Gamma(2)
.

Proof.

Let
g_{1},g_{2},g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
r,r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma(2)
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rg_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rg_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g_{1}g_{2})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2}).

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1}r_{2})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(gr_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(gr_{1}g^{-1}g,r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{2}).

∎

Lemma 4.16
.

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r)=-1
for some
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and some
r\in\Gamma(4)
.

Proof.

Consider
r=u_{-}(-4)
and
g=M_{q}=u(N)
. Then:

M_{q}r=\begin{pmatrix}1&N\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&N\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&4N^{2}\\
-4&1+4N\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1&N\\
0&1\end{pmatrix};

f(M_{q})=1,\quad\quad f(r)=\widetilde{\beta}(r)=\widetilde{\beta}(u_{-}(-4))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. \ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}},

\displaystyle f(M_{q}r)

\displaystyle=f(\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&N\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix})=f(\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&4N^{2}\\
-4&1+4N\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1&N\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}(\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&4N^{2}\\
-4&1+4N\end{pmatrix})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&4N^{2}\\
-4&1+4N\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&N\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}(\begin{pmatrix}1-4N&4N^{2}\\
-4&1+4N\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. \ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}\big(\tfrac{-2}{1+4N}\big)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}=\big(\tfrac{-1}{1+4N}\big)\big(\tfrac{2}{1+4N}\big)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}=-e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(M_{q},r)f(M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q}r)^{-1}

\displaystyle=f(M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q}r)^{-1}=-1.

∎

4.6.
Finite-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})

Definition 4.17
.

(1)

\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\widetilde{M}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

(2)

\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\lambda}^{-1},\overline{M}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

Proposition 4.18
.

(1)

\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}
is an irreducible representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

(2)

\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}
is an irreducible representation of
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

(1) Note that
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}|_{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\simeq\widetilde{\gamma}\oplus\widetilde{\gamma}^{h_{-1}}
. Using the notations from the proof of Prop.
4.13
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\gamma}^{h_{-1}})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{H}\big(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},[\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}]^{h_{-1}}\big)\oplus\operatorname{Hom}_{H_{s}\cap H}\Big(\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\big([\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}]^{h_{-1}}\big)^{s}\Big)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{H_{s}\cap H}\Big(\widetilde{\lambda},[\widetilde{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}]^{s}\Big).

We now take
s=u_{-}(1)
. Then

[s,1][r,t_{r}][s^{-1},1]=[r^{s^{-1}},\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(s,r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(sr,s^{-1})t_{r}],

\widetilde{\lambda}:\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\mapsto t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r),

[\widetilde{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}]^{s}:\widetilde{r}^{s^{-1}}=(r^{s^{-1}},\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(s,r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(sr,s^{-1})t_{r})\mapsto\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(s,r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(sr,s^{-1})t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(h_{-1}r^{s^{-1}}h_{-1}).

Consider the element

r=s^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix}s=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2).

Then

\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(s,r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(sr,s^{-1})=e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}=1,

\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(h_{-1}r^{s^{-1}}h_{-1})=\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem.~\ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}\left[\left(\frac{-1}{1}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(2)}\right]^{-1}=e^{\frac{\pi i}{4}},

and

\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem.~\ref {chap8betacd}}}}{{=}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{1}\right)e^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(-2)}\right]^{-1}=e^{-\frac{\pi i}{4}}.

Hence
\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\gamma}^{h_{-1}})=0
, and so
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}
is irreducible.

(2) Recall the isomorphism
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\xrightarrow{\iota}\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
from (
2.10
). Since
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}=\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}\circ\iota
, the irreducibility of
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\pm}
implies that of
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}
.
∎

4.7.
Twisted by
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}

Let
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=(\mathfrak{w},t_{\mathfrak{w}})\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. Let
\overline{r}=(r,t_{r})=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. Let us define:

\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}:\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{T};\qquad\overline{r}\longmapsto\overline{\lambda}(\overline{s}).

Then
\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}
is a character of
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
.

Definition 4.19
.

\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}[\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}]^{-1},\overline{M}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}\mathbb{C}.

By Prop.
4.13
,
\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}
is an irreducible representation. Similarly, the vector space
\overline{M}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}
consists of the functions
f:\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that
f(\overline{r}\overline{g})=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})^{-1}f(\overline{g})
, for
\overline{r}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
and
\overline{g}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. Let
\overline{e}_{i}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}
denote the function of
\overline{M}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}
, supported on
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
and
\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{i}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}})=1
. Then
\overline{M}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}=\oplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{C}\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{i}
. Write

\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{g})(\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{1},\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{2},\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{3})=(\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{1},\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{2},\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{3})M^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{g}),

for some
3\times 3
-matrix
M^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{g})
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}:&\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}&\longrightarrow&M_{3}(\mathbb{C});\\
&\overline{g}&\longmapsto&M^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{g}),\end{array}

gives a matrix representation.

More precisely, we have:

•

(a1)

\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{s}=\overline{s}^{\prime}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}
, for some
\overline{s},\overline{s}^{\prime}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
iff
[\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}]\overline{r}=\overline{r}^{\prime}[\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}]
, for
\overline{r}=[\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}]
,
\overline{r}^{\prime}=[\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}]
.

(b1)

If the above condition holds, then
[\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})(\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{j})]([\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}])=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{\prime})^{-1}
, and
\operatorname{supp}[\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})](\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{j})=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}
. So
[\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})](\overline{e}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}_{j})=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\overline{e}^{\prime}_{i}
, and
[\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{-1})](\overline{e}^{\prime}_{i})=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{\prime})\overline{e}^{\prime}_{j}
.

•

(c1)

\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{r}}
, for some
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
iff
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=\overline{r}^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{r}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
, for the element
\overline{r}^{\prime}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
.

(d1)

If the above condition holds, then
[\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}})](\overline{e}^{\prime}_{r})=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{\prime})^{-1}\overline{e}^{\prime}_{i}
, and
\overline{\gamma}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}([\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{j}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}]^{-1})(\overline{e}^{\prime}_{i})=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{\prime})\overline{e}^{\prime}_{r}
.

4.8.
Two induced representations

In the next two subsections, we let
G
be a discrete group,
H\leq G
is a subgroup of a finite index
m
, and we fix once and for all a coset decomposition
G=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}H
.
Let
(\sigma,W)
be a representation of the subgroup
H
of finite dimension. Following
[
14
]
, there exist two induction from
H
to
G
.
Write
B=\mathbb{C}[H]
and
A=\mathbb{C}[G]
.

•

Hom-induction

\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}W=\{f:G\to W\mid f(hg)=\sigma(h)f(g),\;\forall h\in H,\,g\in G\}

with
G
-action
(g\cdot f)(x)=f(xg)
.

•

Tensor-induction

\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}W=A\otimes_{B}W,

where
A
is viewed as a right
B
-module and
G
acts by left multiplication on the first factor.

Lemma 4.20
.

There is an
A
-module isomorphism

\mathcal{F}:\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}W\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}W,\qquad f\otimes w\longmapsto F_{f\otimes w}

with

F_{f\otimes w}(g)=\sum_{h\in H}f(g^{-1}h)\,hw.

Proof.

\mathcal{F}
is well-defined: for
b\in H
,
[fb](g)=f(gb^{-1})
.

F_{fb\otimes w}(g)=\sum_{h\in H}[fb](g^{-1}h)\,hw=\sum_{h\in H}f(g^{-1}hb^{-1})\,hw=\sum_{h_{1}\in H}f(g^{-1}h_{1})\,h_{1}bw=F_{f\otimes bw}(g).

G
-equivariance:

F_{g_{0}(f\otimes w)}(g)=F_{g_{0}f\otimes w}(g)=\sum_{h\in H}f(g_{0}^{-1}g^{-1}h)\,hw=F_{f\otimes w}(gg_{0})=(g_{0}\cdot F_{f\otimes w})(g).

Finally,
\mathcal{F}
is bijective: an inverse is given by

\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}W\to A\otimes_{B}W,\qquad\varphi\longmapsto\sum_{x\in H\backslash G}x^{-1}\otimes\varphi(x).\qed

Write:

G=\sqcup_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}H=\sqcup_{i=1}^{m}Hx_{i}^{-1}.

Let
\{f_{j}\mid j=1,\cdots,n\}
be a basis of
W
. We choose the basis of
\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}W
as
\{e^{\prime}_{ij}=x_{i}\otimes f_{j}\mid 1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n\}
and the basis of
\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}W
as
\{e_{ij}\mid 1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n,\operatorname{supp}e_{ij}\subseteq Hx_{i}^{-1}\textrm{ and }e_{ij}(x_{i}^{-1})=f_{j}\}
. It can be checked that
\mathcal{F}(e_{ij}^{\prime})=e_{ij}
.

4.9.
Tensor induction

Let us first recall some results on the tensor induction and the transfer from
[
8
,
\wasyparagraph
13A]
and
[
28
, Chap. 7]
. For
g\in G
, write

gx_{i}=x_{\tau_{g}(i)}h_{g,x_{i}},\qquad\tau_{g}\in S_{m},h_{g,x_{i}}\in H.

(4.3)

The
transfer
is the map

\begin{array}[]{lccl}V_{G\to H}:&G&\longrightarrow&H^{ab}=\tfrac{H}{D(H)};\\
&g&\longmapsto&(\prod_{i=1}^{m}h_{g,x_{i}})\bmod D(H).\end{array}

Theorem 4.21
.

The transfer map
V_{G\to H}
is a group homomorphism and is independent of the choice of coset representatives
\{x_{i}\}
.

Proof.

See
[
28
, Theorem 7.1]
.
∎

Let
H\wr S_{m}=H^{m}\rtimes S_{m}
. Formula (
4.3
) yields a map:

\psi\colon G\longrightarrow H\wr S_{m},\qquad g\longmapsto\bigl([h_{g,x_{1}},\dots,h_{g,x_{m}}],\tau_{g}\bigr).

(4.4)

Lemma 4.22
.

\psi
is a group monomorphism. If another set of representatives
x_{i}^{\prime}=x_{i}h_{i}
is chosen. The corresponding map
\psi^{\prime}
satisfies

\psi^{\prime}(g)=s^{-1}\psi(g)s
, with
s=(h_{1},\cdots,h_{m})
.

Proof.

See
