[
8
, p.332, Lemma 13.3]
.
∎

Extend
\sigma^{\otimes m}
to
H\wr S_{m}
by letting
\tau\in S_{m}
act as

\tau(w_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes w_{m})=w_{\tau^{-1}(1)}\otimes\dots\otimes w_{\tau^{-1}(m)}.

The
tensor induction

\otimes\!\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma
is the representation of
G
obtained by restricting this extension via
\psi
. The lemma above shows that the equivalence class of
\otimes\!\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma
is well defined.

Proposition 4.23
.

If
\sigma
is a character(i.e.
\dim W=1
), then
\otimes\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma
is again a character and satisfies
\otimes\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma=\sigma\circ V_{G\to H}
.

Proof.

See
[
8
, Pro. 13.12]
.
∎

Following
[
8
,
\wasyparagraph
13A]
, we now give an explicit realization of the tensor induced representation
\otimes\!\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma
. Denote
\pi=\otimes\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma
. Let
V=(x_{1}\otimes W)\otimes\cdots\otimes(x_{m}\otimes W)
. For any
g\in G
, the generator
v=(x_{1}\otimes w_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(x_{m}\otimes w_{m})\in V
,

\displaystyle\pi(g)v

\displaystyle=(gx_{1}\otimes w_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(gx_{m}\otimes w_{m})

\displaystyle=(x_{\tau_{g}{(1)}}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{1}})w_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(x_{\tau_{g}{(m)}}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{m}})w_{m})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}x_{1}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(1)}})w_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{m}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(m)}})w_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(m)}.

Following Lemma
4.20
, we give another realization of this representation. For each coset representative
x_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,m
) and
w\in W
set

F_{x_{i}\otimes w}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}(x_{i}\otimes w)\in\operatorname{Ind}_{H}^{G}W,

the unique function supported on the coset
Hx_{i}^{-1}
and satisfying

F_{x_{i}\otimes w}(x_{i}^{-1})=w.

Let
V^{\prime}=\{\sum c_{w_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes w_{m}}F_{x_{1}\otimes w_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{x_{m}\otimes w_{m}}\}.
For
g\in G
, write
gx_{i}=x_{\tau_{g}(i)}h_{g,x_{i}}
(
h_{g,x_{i}}\in H
).
Then

x_{i}^{-1}g^{-1}=h_{g,x_{i}}^{-1}x_{\tau_{g}(i)}^{-1}.

Hence the left translate
{}^{g}F_{x_{i}\otimes w}\coloneqq g\cdot F_{x_{i}\otimes w}
is supported on

\operatorname{supp}{}^{g}F_{x_{i}\otimes w}\subseteq Hx_{i}^{-1}g^{-1}=Hx_{\tau_{g}(i)}^{-1},

and its value at the representative
x_{\tau_{g}(i)}^{-1}
is

{}^{g}F_{x_{i}\otimes w}(x_{\tau_{g}(i)}^{-1})=F_{x_{i}\otimes w}(x_{\tau_{g}(i)}^{-1}g)=F_{x_{i}\otimes w}(h_{g,x_{i}}x_{i}^{-1})=\sigma(h_{g,x_{i}})F_{x_{i}\otimes w}(x_{i}^{-1})=\sigma(h_{g,x_{i}})w.

We define the action of
G
on
V^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle\pi(g)F_{x_{1}\otimes w_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{x_{m}\otimes w_{m}}

\displaystyle=F_{x_{\tau_{g}{(1)}}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{1}})w_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{x_{\tau_{g}{(m)}}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{m}})w_{m}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}F_{x_{1}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(1)}})w_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(1)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{x_{m}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(m)}})w_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(m)}}.

4.10.
Example

Let the above
G=\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}
,
H=\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}
.
Let us define:

\sigma=[\widetilde{\lambda}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}}]^{-1},\qquad\delta=\otimes\operatorname{ind}_{H}^{G}\sigma.

By Prop.
4.23
,
\delta
is a character of
G
. Put

x_{1}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}},\quad x_{2}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{q_{2}}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}},\quad x_{3}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\widetilde{M}_{q_{3}}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}.

Recall the notations
\{\widetilde{e}^{\prime}_{1},\widetilde{e}^{\prime}_{2},\widetilde{e}^{\prime}_{3}\}
from Section
4.4
. Then
\widetilde{e}^{\prime}_{i}=F_{x_{i}\otimes 1}
.

\displaystyle\delta(g)F_{x_{1}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{2}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{3}\otimes 1}

\displaystyle=F_{x_{1}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(1)}})1}\otimes F_{x_{2}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(2)}})1}\otimes F_{x_{m}\otimes\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}^{-1}(m)}})1}

\displaystyle=F_{x_{1}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{2}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{3}\otimes 1}\sigma(V_{G\to H}(g)).

\displaystyle\delta(g)F_{x_{1}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{2}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{3}\otimes 1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\gamma}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}}(g)F_{x_{1}\otimes 1}\otimes\widetilde{\gamma}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}}(g)F_{x_{2}\otimes 1}\otimes\widetilde{\gamma}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{w}}}(g)F_{x_{3}\otimes 1}

\displaystyle=F_{x_{1}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{2}\otimes 1}\otimes F_{x_{3}\otimes 1}\sigma(V_{G\to H}(g)).

5.
Classical Weil representations

The center of
\operatorname{H}(W)
is precisely
\mathbb{R}
. Let
\psi=\psi_{0}^{\mathfrak{e}}
. According to the Stone-von Neumann’s theorem, there exists a unique unitary irreducible complex representation with central character
\psi
, up to unitary equivalence. Let us call it the Heisenberg representation, and denote it by
\pi_{\psi}
. The group
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
can then act on
\operatorname{H}(W)
and leave the center
\mathbb{R}
pointwise unchanged. Then the Heisenberg representation yields a projective representation of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, leading to an actual representation of a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-covering group over
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. This
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-covering group is commonly called the Metaplectic group, and the true representation is known as the Weil representation. This central covering can be reduced to an
8
-fold or
2
-fold covering over
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, as shown in
[
22
,
25
,
38
]
and others. Our main references are Rao
[
25
]
, Kudla
[
15
]
, and Lion–Vergne
[
18
]
.

5.1.
Schrödinger model

Note that
X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}
is a closed subgroup of
\operatorname{H}(W)
. Let
\psi_{X^{\ast}}
denote the one–dimensional unitary representation of
X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}
obtained by extending
\psi
trivially along
X^{\ast}
. Define

\pi_{\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\psi_{X^{\ast}},V_{\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\mathbb{C}.

Then
\pi_{\psi}
is the Heisenberg representation of
\operatorname{H}(W)
with central character
\psi
. By Weil’s theorem it extends uniquely to a unitary representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
, provided the central subgroup
\mu_{8}
of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
acts trivially; uniqueness follows because
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
is perfect.

With respect to the self-dual Haar measure
d\mu(y)
on
\mathbb{R}
determined by
\psi
, the Heisenberg representation
\pi_{\psi}
acts on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
by the following formulas, valid for all
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
:

\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}([x,0]\cdot[x^{\ast},0]\cdot[0,k])f(y)=\psi(k+\langle x+y,x^{\ast}\rangle)f(x+y),

(5.1)

\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(u(b))f(y)=\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,yb\rangle)f(y),

(5.2)

\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h(a))f(y)=|\det(a)|^{1/2}f(ya),

(5.3)

\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}([\omega,t])f(y)=\int_{X^{\ast}}t\psi(\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)f(y^{\ast}\omega^{-1})d\mu(y^{\ast}),

(5.4)

where

u(b)=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad h(a)=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\omega=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Moreover, for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})\,\pi_{\psi}(g_{1}g_{2}),

where
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
is Perrin–Rao’s
2
-cocycle attached to
(\psi,X^{\ast})
; see
[
25
,
22
]
.

5.2.
The Lattice model

Let
L\subset W
be a lattice (cf.
[
18
, p. 138]
). Define its orthogonal complement with respect to
\psi
by

L^{\perp}=\bigl\{w\in W\bigm|\psi\!\bigl(\langle w,l\rangle\bigr)=1\text{ for all }l\in L\bigr\}.

A lattice
L
is called
self-dual
(with respect to
\psi
) if
L=L^{\perp}
.
Write
\operatorname{H}(L)=L\times\mathbb{R}
for the corresponding subgroup of
\operatorname{H}(W)
and set

\pi^{\prime}_{\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\psi_{L},

where
\psi_{L}
denotes the extension of
\psi
to
\operatorname{H}(L)
.
Then
\pi^{\prime}_{\psi}
is a Heisenberg representation of
\operatorname{H}(W)
with central character
\psi
.

Example 5.1
.

Take
\psi=\psi_{0}
and
L=\mathbb{Z}e\oplus\mathbb{Z}e^{\ast}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(L)
be the space of measurable functions
f\colon W\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying

(i)

f(l+w)=\psi\!\bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle x_{l},x_{l}^{\ast}\rangle-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle l,w\rangle\bigr)f(w)

for all
l=x_{l}+x_{l}^{\ast}\in L=(L\cap X)\oplus(L\cap X^{\ast})

and almost all
w\in W
;

(ii)

\displaystyle\int_{L\backslash W}|f(w)|^{2}\,dw<+\infty
,

with the natural
W
-invariant measure on
L\backslash W
(from the self-dual measure on
W
with respect to
\psi
).
Then
\pi^{\prime}_{\psi}
is realized on
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}(L)
by

\pi^{\prime}_{\psi}\bigl[(w^{\prime},t)\bigr]f(w)=\psi\!\bigl(t+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle\bigr)f(w+w^{\prime}),\qquad w,w^{\prime}\in W,\;t\in\mathbb{R}.

5.3.
The Fock model

In this section we fix
\psi=\psi_{0}
. Let
V=\mathbb{C}
and equip it with the Hermitian form as follows:

(z,z^{\prime})_{V}=\overline{z}z^{\prime},\quad\quad z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}.

Let
\operatorname{H}(V)=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{R}
be the group of elements
(v,t)
, with the
multiplication law given by

(z,t)(z^{\prime},t^{\prime})=(v+v^{\prime},t+t^{\prime}+\tfrac{\operatorname{Im}(z,z^{\prime})_{V}}{2}),\quad\quad z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.

Then there exists a group isomorphism:

\displaystyle\phi\colon\operatorname{H}(V)\longrightarrow\operatorname{H}(W),\qquad(a+ib,t)\mapsto(ae+be^{\ast},t).

(5.5)

We now recall the Fock model of the Heisenberg representation.

Example 5.2

(Fock model)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{F}
be the Fock space of entire functions
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying

\|f\|^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{C}}|f(z)|^{2}e^{-\pi|z|^{2}}\,d(z)<+\infty.

The Heisenberg representation of
\operatorname{H}(V)
is realized on
\mathcal{H}_{F}
by

\pi_{F}[(z^{\prime},t)]f(z)=\psi_{0}(t)\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}|z^{\prime}|^{2}-\pi z\overline{z^{\prime}}}f(z+z^{\prime}),\qquad z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C},\ t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

See
[
12
, p. 43, (1.71)]
.
∎

Following Satake–Takase
[
26
,
27
,
31
,
32
]
we attach the following objects to each
z=P_{z}+iQ_{z}\in\mathbb{H}
.

•

Complex conjugate on
\mathbb{H}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{z}:=-\overline{z}
.

•

Measures on
V
:

\displaystyle d_{z}(v):=Q_{z}^{-1}\,d(v)=d_{\widehat{z}}(v)
.

•

Gaussian factor:

\displaystyle q_{z}(v):=e^{-\pi iz^{-1}v^{2}}
.

•

Kernel functions:

\kappa(z^{\prime},v^{\prime};z,v):=e^{\pi i(z^{\prime}-\overline{z})^{-1}(v^{\prime}-\overline{v})^{2}},\qquad\kappa_{z}(v^{\prime},v):=\kappa(z,v^{\prime};z,v)=e^{\frac{\pi}{2}Q_{z}^{-1}(v^{\prime}-\overline{v})^{2}}.

•

2-cocycle:

\displaystyle\alpha_{z}(g_{1},g_{2}):=\epsilon\!\bigl(g_{1}^{h_{-1}};z,g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(z)\bigr)
,

(g_{i}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}))
.

•

Action of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
on
\mathbb{H}\times V
:

[g,h]\cdot[z,v]:=\Bigl[g(z),\,(v+xz+x^{\ast})J(g,z)^{-1}\Bigr],\qquad h=(x,x^{\ast};t)\in\operatorname{H}(W).

•

Automorphy factor

\eta\colon[\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)]\times[\mathbb{H}\times V]\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
:

(a)

For
h=[x,x^{\ast};t]\in\operatorname{H}(W)
:

\eta(h;[z,v]):=e^{2\pi i\bigl[t+\tfrac{1}{2}(xz+x^{\ast})x+vx\bigr]}.

(b)

For
g=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
:

\eta(g;[z,v]):=e^{2\pi i\bigl[-\tfrac{1}{2}(cz+d)^{-1}cv^{2}\bigr]}.

(c)

Mixed case:

\eta\bigl([g,h];[z,v]\bigr):=\eta\!\bigl(h^{g^{-1}};[g(z),vJ(g,z)^{-1}]\bigr)\;\eta(g;[z,v]),\qquad(g,h)=(1,h^{g^{-1}})(g,0).

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\widetilde{g}_{1}=[g_{1},h_{1}]
,
\widetilde{g}_{2}=[g_{2},h_{2}]\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
and
\widetilde{z}=[z,v]\in\mathbb{H}\times V
we have

\eta\!\bigl(\widetilde{g}_{1}\widetilde{g}_{2},\widetilde{z}\bigr)=\eta\!\bigl(\widetilde{g}_{1},\widetilde{g}_{2}(\widetilde{z})\bigr)\,\eta\!\bigl(\widetilde{g}_{2},\widetilde{z}\bigr).

Proof.

See
[
27
, p. 395]
or
[
31
, p. 121]
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

For
\widetilde{g}=[g,h]\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
and
\widetilde{z}=[z,v]
,
\widetilde{z}^{\prime}=[z^{\prime},v^{\prime}]\in\mathbb{H}\times V
we have

\kappa\!\bigl(\widetilde{g}(\widetilde{z});\widetilde{g}(\widetilde{z}^{\prime})\bigr)=\eta\!\bigl(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{z}\bigr)\,\kappa(\widetilde{z};\widetilde{z}^{\prime})\,\overline{\eta\!\bigl(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{z}^{\prime}\bigr)}.

Proof.

See
[
27
, p. 396]
or
[
31
, p. 121]
.
∎

Example 5.5

(Generalized Fock model)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}
be the space of entire functions
f\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
satisfying

\|f\|^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{C}}|f(v)|^{2}\,\kappa_{z}(v,v)\,d_{z}(v)<\infty.

The Heisenberg representation of
\operatorname{H}(W)
is realized on
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}
by

\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(h)f(v)=\psi_{0}(t)\,e^{-2\pi i\!\left[\frac{1}{2}(x\overline{z}+x^{*})\,x+vx\right]}f\!\bigl(v+x\overline{z}+x^{*}\bigr),

where
h=(w,t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
with
w=x+x^{*}\in W
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

See
[
31
, pp. 125–126]
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

Take
z=\widehat{z}=z_{0}=i
. Following
[
26
, p.397]
, there exists a unitary equivalence
\mathcal{A}
from
\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}
to
\mathcal{H}_{F}
, defined by

\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{F};f\longmapsto\phi(v)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}f(-vi).

Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}
is an
\operatorname{H}(W)
-intertwining operator.

Proof.

1)
v=v_{x}+iv_{y}
,
w=-vi=v_{y}-v_{x}i
,
w_{x}=v_{y},w_{y}=-v_{x}
.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mid\phi(v)\mid^{2}e^{-\pi|v|^{2}}d(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mid e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}f(-vi)\mid^{2}e^{-\pi|v|^{2}}d(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi v_{x}^{2}}\mid f(-vi)\mid^{2}d(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w=-vi}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi w_{y}^{2}}\mid f(w)\mid^{2}d(w)<+\infty.

2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(h)f)(v)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}[\pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(h)f](-vi)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}\psi_{0}(t)e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(-xi+x^{*})x-vix]}f(-vi-xi+x^{\ast});

\displaystyle\pi_{F,\psi}(h)\mathcal{A}(f)(v)=\psi_{0}(t)e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}(x^{2}+x^{\ast 2})-\pi v(x-x^{\ast}i)}\mathcal{A}(f)(v+x+x^{\ast}i)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}(v+x+x^{\ast}i)^{2}}\psi_{0}(t)e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}(x^{2}+x^{\ast 2})-\pi v(x-x^{\ast}i)}f(-vi-xi+x^{\ast})

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}e^{-2\pi vx}\psi_{0}(t)e^{-\pi(x^{2}+ix^{\ast}x)}f(-vi-xi+x^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}(\pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi_{0}}(h)f)(v).

∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For
\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{H}
, there exists a unitary equivalence operator
U_{\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}}}
mapping from
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}
to
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}}
, defined by

U_{\widehat{z^{\prime}},\widehat{z}}:\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}};\phi\longmapsto f(v^{\prime})=\gamma(\widehat{z^{\prime}},\widehat{z})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime};\widehat{z},v)^{-1}\phi(v)\kappa_{\widehat{z}}(v,v)d_{\widehat{z}}(v).

Furthermore,
U_{\widehat{z^{\prime}},\widehat{z}}
is an
\operatorname{H}(W)
-intertwining operator.

Proof.

See
[
31
, p.125]
or
[
26
, Lemma 3]
.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

For
z,z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{H}
,
U_{z,z^{\prime}}\circ U_{z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}}=U_{z,z^{\prime\prime}}
.

Proof.

See
[
26
, pp.399-400]
.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

For every
g=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
the map

\displaystyle T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}\colon\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{F_{g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}},

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto\eta\!\bigl((g^{h_{-1}})^{-1},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}),v\bigr)\,\phi\!\bigl(vJ(g^{h_{-1}},\widehat{z})\bigr)=e^{-\pi i(c\overline{z}+d)cv^{2}}\phi\!\bigl(v(c\overline{z}+d)\bigr)

is unitary. Moreover, for every
h=(x,x^{\ast};t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
the diagram

\begin{CD}\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}@>{T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}}>{}>\mathcal{H}_{F_{g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}}\\
@V{\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(h)}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{F_{g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}}(h^{g^{-1}})}V\\
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}@>{}>{T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}}>\mathcal{H}_{F_{g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}}\end{CD}

commutes.

Proof.

1)

g^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}d&-b\\
-c&a\end{pmatrix},\quad g^{h_{-1}}=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
-c&d\end{pmatrix},\quad(g^{h_{-1}})^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}d&b\\
c&a\end{pmatrix},

g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})=(-a\overline{z}-b)(c\overline{z}+d)^{-1},J(g^{h_{-1}},\widehat{z})=c\overline{z}+d;

\displaystyle\eta((g^{h_{-1}})^{-1};g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}),v)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\langle vg^{h_{-1}},vJ((g^{h_{-1}})^{-1},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}))^{-1}\rangle\Big]}

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\Big[\tfrac{1}{2}\langle v(-c),vJ(g^{h_{-1}},\widehat{z})\rangle\Big]}

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\Big[-\tfrac{1}{2}v(\overline{z}+d)cv^{2}\Big]}.

2) Write
z_{1}=\widehat{z}
,
z_{2}=g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})
,
h=(x,x^{\ast};t)
,
g_{1}=g^{h_{-1}}
,
h_{1}=h^{h_{-1}}
,
h^{g^{-1}}=(x_{1},x_{1}^{\ast};t)
,
w_{1}=(x_{1},x_{1}^{\ast})
. Then:

h^{g^{-1}}=h^{h_{-1}g_{1}^{-1}h_{-1}}=h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}h_{-1}};

(h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}})^{-1}=[h^{g^{-1}h_{-1}}]^{-1}=[x_{1},-x_{1}^{\ast};-t]^{-1}=[-x_{1},x_{1}^{\ast};t];

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{z_{2}}}(h^{g^{-1}})\circ T^{z_{1}}_{g_{1}}f(v)

\displaystyle=\eta((h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}})^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v)[T^{z_{1}}_{g_{1}}f](v+w_{1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\eta\big((h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}})^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big)\eta\big(g_{1}^{-1},g_{1}(z_{1}),v+w_{1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix}\big)f\bigg([v+w_{1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix}]J(g_{1},z_{1})\bigg);

\displaystyle T^{z_{1}}_{g_{1}}\circ\pi_{F_{z_{1}}}(h)f(v)

\displaystyle=\eta(g_{1}^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v)[\pi_{F_{z_{1}}}(h)f](vJ(g_{1},z_{1}))

\displaystyle=\eta(g_{1}^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v)\eta((h^{h_{-1}})^{-1};z_{1},vJ(g_{1},z_{1}))f(vJ(g_{1},z_{1})+w\begin{pmatrix}-z_{1}\\
1\end{pmatrix});

i)

\displaystyle(w_{1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix})J(g_{1},z_{1})

\displaystyle=wg^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix}J(g_{1},z_{1})

\displaystyle=wh_{-1}g_{1}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-g_{1}(z_{1})\\
-1\end{pmatrix}J(g_{1},z_{1})

\displaystyle=w\begin{pmatrix}-z_{1}\\
1\end{pmatrix};

ii)

\displaystyle\eta([g_{1},h^{h_{-1}}]^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v)

\displaystyle=\eta\big([1,h^{h_{-1}}]^{-1}[g_{1},0]^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big)

\displaystyle=\eta\Big((h^{h_{-1}})^{-1};z_{1},vJ\big(g_{1}^{-1},g_{1}(z_{1})\big)^{-1}\Big)\eta\big(g_{1}^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big)

\displaystyle=\eta\big((h^{h_{-1}})^{-1};z_{1},vJ(g_{1},z_{1})\big)\eta\big(g_{1}^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big);

\displaystyle\eta([g_{1},h^{h_{-1}}]^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v)

\displaystyle=\eta\big([g_{1},0]^{-1}[1,h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}}]^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big)

\displaystyle=\eta\big(g_{1}^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v+(-x_{1},x_{1}^{\ast})\cdot\begin{pmatrix}g_{1}(z_{1})\\
1\end{pmatrix}\big)\eta\big((h_{1}^{g_{1}^{-1}})^{-1};g_{1}(z_{1}),v\big).

∎

Lemma 5.10
.

(1)

T^{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}}=T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}g_{2}^{h_{-1}}}
.

(2)

For
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
U_{g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z^{\prime}}),g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}=\epsilon(g^{h_{-1}};\widehat{z^{\prime}},\widehat{z})T^{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}\circ U_{\widehat{z^{\prime}},\widehat{z}}
.

Proof.

See
[
31
, p.125]
.
∎

The Heisenberg representation
\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}
of
\operatorname{H}(W)
from Example
5.5
extends to a projective representation of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
on
\mathcal{H}_{F_{\widehat{z}}}
by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(h)f(v)=\eta((h^{h_{-1}})^{-1},\widehat{z},v)f(v+x\overline{z}+x^{\ast}),\\
\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g)f(v)=U_{\widehat{z},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}f(v),\end{array}\right.

(5.6)

for
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
h=(x,x^{\ast};t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
.

Lemma 5.11
.

(1)

The formulas (
5.6
) are well-defined.

(2)

For
g_{1},g_{2}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g_{1})\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g_{2})=\alpha_{\widehat{z}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}\,\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g_{1}g_{2}).

Proof.

[g,h]=[g,0]\cdot[1,h]=[1,h^{g^{-1}}]\cdot[g,0].

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g)\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}},\psi}(h)

\displaystyle=U_{\widehat{z},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}\circ\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}},\psi}(h)

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\widehat{z}_{1}=g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}}U_{\widehat{z},\widehat{z}_{1}}\circ\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}_{1}}}(h^{g^{-1}})\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(h^{g^{-1}})\circ U_{\widehat{z},\widehat{z}_{1}}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(h^{g^{-1}})\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g).

By the above lemma,

U_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}),g_{1}^{h_{-1}}g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}}=\epsilon(g_{1}^{h_{-1}};\widehat{z},g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}))T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}}\circ U_{\widehat{z},g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})};

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g_{1})\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}},\psi}(g_{2})

\displaystyle=U_{\widehat{z},g_{1}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}}\circ U_{\widehat{z},g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon(g_{1}^{h_{-1}};\widehat{z},g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}))^{-1}U_{\widehat{z},g_{1}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ U_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z}),g_{1}^{h_{-1}}g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}_{g_{1}^{h_{-1}}}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g_{2}^{h_{-1}}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\widehat{z}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}\pi_{F_{\widehat{z}}}(g_{1}g_{2}).

∎

Remark 5.12
.

When
z=\widehat{z}=z_{0}
, the identities

\alpha_{z_{0}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}=\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}(g_{1}^{h_{-1}},g_{2}^{h_{-1}})^{-1}=\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}(g_{1},g_{2})

hold, and the extended formulas (
5.6
) give the representation

\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}
of
\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
.

Example 5.13
.

Take
z=z_{0}=i
. Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C})
. Then
g^{h_{-1}}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
-b&a\end{pmatrix}
.
Additionally, the representation
\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)
acting on a function
f(v)
is defined as:

\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f(v)=e^{-\pi i(-bi+a)bv^{2}}f(v(-bi+a)).

(5.7)

(1)

If we take
f(v)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}
, then
f(v)\in\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}
. Moreover,
\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f(v)=f(v)
.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi v_{y}^{2}}\mid f(v)\mid^{2}d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi v_{y}^{2}}e^{-\pi(v_{x}^{2}-v_{y}^{2})}d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi(v_{x}^{2}+v_{y}^{2})}dv_{x}dv_{y}<+\infty.

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f(v)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i(-bi+a)bv^{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}(-bi+a)^{2}v^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi iabv^{2}-\pi b^{2}v^{2}}e^{\tfrac{\pi}{2}(-a^{2}+b^{2}+2abi)v^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}=f(v).

(2)

If we take
g(v)=ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}
for
v\in V
, then
g(v)\in\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}
.

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)g(v)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i(-bi+a)bv^{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}(-bi+a)^{2}v^{2}}(iv(-bi+a))

\displaystyle=g(v)(-bi+a).

Example 5.14
.

Take
z=\widehat{z}=z_{0}=i
. Let us give the explicit actions of
u(b),h(a)
, for
u(b)=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
,
h(a)=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

1) If
g=h(a)
, then
g^{h_{-1}}=h(a)
,
\widehat{z^{\prime}}=g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})=\widehat{z}a^{2}=ia^{2}
. Write
v=v_{x}+iv_{y}
and
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime}_{x}+iv^{\prime}_{y}
.

\displaystyle\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\big(\tfrac{\widehat{z}-\overline{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}}{2i}\big)^{-1/2}\cdot(\operatorname{Im}\widehat{z})^{1/4}\cdot(\operatorname{Im}\widehat{z^{\prime}})^{1/4}

\displaystyle=\big(\tfrac{1+a^{2}}{2}\big)^{-1/2}\cdot|a|^{1/2};

\displaystyle\kappa_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime}a,v^{\prime}a)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\pi v^{\prime}_{y}a[a^{2}]^{-1}av^{\prime}_{y}}

\displaystyle=e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime});

\displaystyle d_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime})=

\displaystyle a^{-2}d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime});

\displaystyle\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime}a)^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\cdot(\widehat{z}-\overline{\widehat{z^{\prime}}})^{-1}\cdot(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}.

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{z}}(g)f(v)

\displaystyle=U_{\widehat{z},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}f(v)

\displaystyle=\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\big[T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}f\big](v^{\prime\prime})\kappa_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})d_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime\prime})^{-1}f(v^{\prime\prime}a^{-1})\kappa_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime\prime})d_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}\cdot a^{-1}}}{{=}}|a|^{2}\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime}a)^{-1}f(v^{\prime})\kappa_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime}a,v^{\prime}a)d_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}\big(\tfrac{2}{1+a^{2}}\big)^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}f(v^{\prime})e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime}).

2) If
g=u(b)
, then
g^{h_{-1}}=u(-b)
,
\widehat{z^{\prime}}=g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})=i-b
.

\displaystyle\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\big(\tfrac{\widehat{z}-\overline{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}}{2i}\big)^{-1/2}\cdot(\operatorname{Im}\widehat{z})^{1/4}\cdot(\operatorname{Im}\widehat{z^{\prime}})^{1/4}

\displaystyle=\big(\tfrac{2i+b}{2i}\big)^{-1/2}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tfrac{b}{2}i}}.

\displaystyle\kappa_{\widehat{z^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})=\kappa_{z}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v^{\prime}=v^{\prime}_{x}+iv^{\prime}_{y}}}{{=}}e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}};

\displaystyle\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime})^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i(\widehat{z}-\overline{\widehat{z^{\prime}}})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i(2i+b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}};

\displaystyle\pi_{F_{z}}(g)f(v)=U_{\widehat{z},g^{h_{-1}}(\widehat{z})}\circ T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}f(v)

\displaystyle=\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime})^{-1}\big[T^{\widehat{z}}_{g^{h_{-1}}}f\big](v^{\prime})\kappa_{z}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})d_{z}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\gamma(\widehat{z},\widehat{z^{\prime}})\int_{\mathbb{C}}\kappa(\widehat{z},v;\widehat{z^{\prime}},v^{\prime})^{-1}f(v^{\prime})\kappa_{z}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})d_{z}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tfrac{b}{2}i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi i(2i+b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}f(v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y}.

6.
Extended Weil representations:
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
case

In this section, we will generate the Weil representation to
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

6.1.

Recall that
\pi_{\psi}
is Weil representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
associated to
\psi
, and
\omega_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}|_{\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}
. Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
act on
\operatorname{H}(W)
via
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 6.1
.

\omega_{\psi}\simeq\omega_{\psi^{h^{2}}}
, for any
h\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}
, and
\omega_{\psi}\ncong\omega_{\psi^{-1}}
.

Proof.

We closely follow the argument of
[
20
, p. 36]
in the
p
-adic setting.
Let
\widetilde{h}=[h,1]\in\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widetilde{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and write
w_{t}=(w,t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
.
Then

\pi_{\psi}\!\bigl(w_{t}^{\widetilde{h}}\bigr)=\pi_{\psi}\!\bigl(wh,\,t\lambda_{h}\bigr)\simeq\pi_{\psi^{\lambda_{h}}}\!(w_{t}),

whence

\pi_{\psi}\!\bigl([\widetilde{g},w_{t}]^{\widetilde{h}}\bigr)=\pi_{\psi}\!\bigl([\widetilde{g}^{\widetilde{h}},\,(wh,t\lambda_{h})]\bigr)\simeq\pi_{\psi^{\lambda_{h}}}\!\bigl([\widetilde{g},w_{t}]\bigr).

Taking
h\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\times}
gives
\widetilde{g}^{\widetilde{h}}=\widetilde{g}
and consequently

\omega_{\psi}(\widetilde{g})=\pi_{\psi}(\widetilde{g})\simeq\pi_{\psi^{h^{2}}}(\widetilde{g})=\omega_{\psi^{h^{2}}}(\widetilde{g}).

The last relation can also be derived from the character computations for
\omega_{\psi}
and
\omega_{\psi^{-1}}
given in
[
34
, Thm. 1C]
and
[
1
]
.
∎

Let

\Pi_{\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}^{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}\pi_{\psi},\quad V^{\pm}_{\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}^{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}V_{\psi},\qquad\Omega_{\psi}=\operatorname{Res}_{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})}^{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}\Pi_{\psi}.

By Clifford–Mackey theory,

\Omega_{\psi}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\,\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})}\omega_{\psi}.

Lemma 6.2
.

The restriction of
\Omega_{\psi}
to
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
decomposes as the direct sum of the two Weil representations

\omega_{\psi}\oplus\omega_{\psi^{-1}}
.

Consequently,
\Omega_{\psi}
is independent of the choice of
\psi
; we denote it simply by
\Omega
and call it the Weil representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
. As a representation of
\operatorname{H}(W)
one has

\Pi_{\psi}\simeq\pi_{\psi}\oplus\pi_{\psi^{-1}},

which is reducible.
Set
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)=F_{2}\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
. Then
\Pi_{\psi}
is an irreducible representation of
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
. Observe that
\mathbb{R}
is a normal subgroup of
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
and sits in the exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)\longrightarrow F_{2}\ltimes W\longrightarrow 1.

6.2.
The Schrödinger model

Retain the notations of Section
5.1
. Let us consider
F\in V^{\pm}_{\psi}
. Then

F([\epsilon,h])=F([1,h^{s(\epsilon)}][\epsilon,0])=\pi_{\psi}(h^{s(\epsilon)})F([\epsilon,0]).

We identify
F([\epsilon,0])(x)=f([\epsilon,x])
, for
x\in X
. The representation
\Pi_{\psi}
of
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)
can be realized on

L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)\cong L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{R})
. To distinguish the different models, we write it by
\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}
. It is given by the following formulas:

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[(x,0)\cdot(x^{\ast},0)\cdot(0,k)]f([\epsilon,y])=\psi(\epsilon k+\epsilon(x+y)x^{\ast})f([\epsilon,x+y]),

(6.1)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[h_{-1}]f([\epsilon,y])=f([-\epsilon,y]),

(6.2)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[u(b)]f([\epsilon,y])=\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon by^{2})f([\epsilon,y]),

(6.3)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[h(a)]f([\epsilon,y])=|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,ya]),

(6.4)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[\omega]f([\epsilon,y])=\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(\epsilon yy^{\ast})f([\epsilon,-y^{\ast}])d\mu(y^{\ast}).

(6.5)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[\operatorname{diag}(r,r)]f([\epsilon,y])=f([\epsilon,y]).

(6.6)

where
x,y,x^{\ast},y^{\ast},k\in\mathbb{R}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{>0}
,
t\in\mu_{8}
,
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
, and

h_{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},h(a)=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix},u(b)=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\omega=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\operatorname{diag}(r,r)=\begin{pmatrix}r&0\\
0&r\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Moreover, under such actions, for
h_{1},h_{2}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{1})\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{2})=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{1}h_{2}).

Let us verify these formulas:

1)

(x,0)\cdot(x^{\ast},0)\cdot(0,k)=(x,x^{\ast};k+\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\in\operatorname{H}(W);

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[(x,0)\cdot(x^{\ast},0)\cdot(0,k)]f(\epsilon,y)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}[(x,0)\cdot(x^{\ast},0)\cdot(0,k)]F([\epsilon,0])(y)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0]\cdot[1,(x,x^{\ast};k+\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})])(y)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,(x,x^{\ast};k+\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})])(y)

\displaystyle=F([1,(x,x^{\ast};k+\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})^{s(\epsilon)}][\epsilon,0])(y)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}((x,x^{\ast};k+\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})^{s(\epsilon)})F([\epsilon,0])(y)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0])((y,0)\cdot(x,\epsilon x^{\ast};\epsilon k+\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0])((x+y,\epsilon x^{\ast};\tfrac{\langle y,\epsilon x^{\ast}\rangle}{2}+\epsilon k+\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0])((\epsilon x^{\ast};\epsilon k+\epsilon\langle x+y,x^{\ast}\rangle)(x+y,0)

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon k+\epsilon\langle x+y,x^{\ast}\rangle)F([\epsilon,0])(x+y,0)

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon k+\epsilon(x+y)x^{\ast})f([\epsilon,x+y]).

Let:

([\epsilon,1],1),([1,g],1)\in\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}).

\displaystyle([\epsilon,1],1)([1,g],1)

\displaystyle=([\epsilon,g],\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}([\epsilon,1],[1,g]))

\displaystyle=([\epsilon,g],1)

\displaystyle=\Big([1,g^{s(\epsilon)}],\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}([1,g^{s(\epsilon)}],[\epsilon,1])^{-1}\Big)\Big([\epsilon,1],1\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big([1,g^{s(\epsilon)}],\nu(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g,1)^{-1}\Big)\Big([\epsilon,1],1\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big([1,g^{s(\epsilon)}],\nu(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\Big)\Big([\epsilon,1],1\Big).

2) If
g=u(b)
, then
\nu(1,g)=1=\nu(-1,g)=\nu(-1,g^{s(-1)})
.

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,y])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)F([\epsilon,0])(y)

(6.7)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0]\cdot[g,0])(y)

(6.8)

\displaystyle=F(\Big[\big([\epsilon,g],\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}([\epsilon,1],[1,g])\big),0\Big])(y)

(6.9)

\displaystyle=F(\Big(\big([1,g^{s(\epsilon)}],\nu(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\big),0\Big)\Big(\big([\epsilon,1],1\big),0\Big))(y)

(6.10)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}(g^{s(\epsilon)})\nu(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}F(\Big(\big([\epsilon,1],1\big),0\Big))(y)

(6.11)

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,yb\rangle)F([\epsilon,0])(y)

(6.12)

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{by^{2}}{2})f([\epsilon,y]).

(6.13)

3) If
g=h(a)
, then
\nu(1,g)=1
,
\nu(-1,g^{s(\epsilon)})=\nu(-1,g)=(a,-1)_{\mathbb{R}}
.

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,y])

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}(g^{s(\epsilon)})\nu(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})F([\epsilon,0])(y)

(6.14)

\displaystyle=(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}|a|^{1/2}F([\epsilon,0])(ya)

(6.15)

\displaystyle=(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}|a|^{1/2}f([\epsilon,ya]).

(6.16)

4) If
g=\omega
, then
\nu(1,g)=1
,
\nu(-1,g)=\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=e^{\tfrac{(\operatorname{sgn}\mathfrak{e})\pi i}{2}}
,
\nu(-1,g^{s(-1)})=e^{-\tfrac{(\operatorname{sgn}\mathfrak{e})\pi i}{2}}
.

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f([1,y])

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}(g)\nu(1,g)^{-1}F([1,0])(y)

(6.17)

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)F([1,0])(y^{\ast}\omega^{-1})d\mu(y^{\ast})

(6.18)

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(yy^{\ast})f([1,-y^{\ast}])d\mu(y^{\ast}).

(6.19)

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f([-1,y])

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}(g^{s(-1)})\nu(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}F([-1,0])(y)

(6.20)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\psi}(-g)\nu(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}F([-1,0])(y)

(6.21)

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}}\nu(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}\psi(\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)F([-1,0])(-y^{\ast}\omega^{-1})d\mu(y^{\ast})

(6.22)

\displaystyle=\nu(-1,g)\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(yy^{\ast})f([-1,y^{\ast}])d\mu(y^{\ast})

(6.23)

\displaystyle=\nu(-1,g)\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(-yy^{\ast})f([-1,-y^{\ast}])d\mu(y^{\ast}).

(6.24)

6.3.
The Lattice model

Retain notations from Section
5.2
. Then
\Pi_{\psi}\simeq\Pi_{L,\psi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)}\psi_{L}
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}^{\pm}(L)
be the set of measurable functions
f:\mu_{2}\times W\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that

(i)

f(\epsilon,l+w)=\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2}-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2})f(\epsilon,w)
, for all
l=x_{l}+x_{l}^{\ast}\in L=(X\cap L)\oplus(X^{\ast}\cap L)
, almost all
w\in W
,

(ii)

\int_{L\setminus W}||f(\epsilon,w)||^{2}dw<+\infty
,

where
w\in W
,
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
, and
dw
is a
W
-right invariant measure on
L\setminus W
. Then
\Pi_{L,\psi}
can be realized on
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}^{\pm}(L)
by the following formulas:

\Pi_{L,\psi}([1,w^{\prime}_{t}])f(\epsilon,w)=\psi(\epsilon t+\epsilon\tfrac{\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle}{2})f(\epsilon,w+w^{\prime}),

(6.25)

\Pi_{L,\psi}([-1,0])f(\epsilon,w)=f(-\epsilon,x-x^{\ast}),

(6.26)

for
w=x+x^{\ast},w^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+x^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\in W
,
w^{\prime}_{t}=(w^{\prime},t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
,
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

6.4.
The Fock model

Retain notations from Section
5.3
, and consider
z=z_{0}=i
. Let:

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}^{\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}\pi_{F_{z_{0}}},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}^{\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}(W)}\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}.

Let
F\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}
. Then

F([\epsilon,h])=F([1,h^{s(\epsilon)}][\epsilon,0])=\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h^{s(\epsilon)})F([\epsilon,0]).

We identify
F([\epsilon,0])(v)=f([\epsilon,v])
. So
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}
can be taken as the space of holomorphic functions
f
on
\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{C}
such that

\|f\|^{2}=\Big[\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mid f([1,v])\mid^{2}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)d_{z_{0}}(v)+\int_{\mathbb{C}}\mid f([-1,v])\mid^{2}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)d_{z_{0}}(v)\Big]^{1/2}<+\infty.

The representation
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}
of
\widehat{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\ltimes\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
can be realized on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}
by the following formulas:

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h)f([\epsilon,v])=\psi_{0}(\epsilon t)e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{*})x+vx]}f([\epsilon,v+x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{\ast}]),

(6.27)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[h_{-1}]f([\epsilon,v])=f([-\epsilon,v]),

(6.28)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[u]f([1,v])=e^{-\pi i\overline{u}\operatorname{Im}(u)v^{2}}f([1,v\overline{u}]),

(6.29)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[u]f([-1,v])=ue^{-\pi iu\operatorname{Im}(\overline{u})v^{2}}f([-1,vu]),

(6.30)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[u(b)]f([\epsilon,v])=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\epsilon\tfrac{b}{2}i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi i(2i+\epsilon b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}f([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y},

(6.31)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[h(a)]f([\epsilon,v])=(\epsilon,a)_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\tfrac{2|a|}{1+a^{2}}\big)^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}f([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y},

(6.32)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}[\operatorname{diag}(r,r)]f([\epsilon,v])=f([\epsilon,v]),

(6.33)

where
w=x+x^{\ast}\in W
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
h=(w,t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
,
r>0
,
u(b)=\begin{pmatrix}1&b\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad h(a)=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\omega=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and
u=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
is identified to
u=bi+a
. Let us verify these formulas:

1)

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h)f([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([1,h])F([\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0][1,h])(v)

\displaystyle=F([1,h^{s(\epsilon)}][\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h^{s(\epsilon)})F([\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=\psi_{0}(\epsilon t)\,e^{-2\pi i\!\left[\frac{1}{2}(x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{*})\,x+vx\right]}F([\epsilon,0])\!\bigl(v+x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\psi_{0}(\epsilon t)\,e^{-2\pi i\!\left[\frac{1}{2}(x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{*})\,x+vx\right]}f(\epsilon,v+x\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon x^{*}]).

2)

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([-1,0])F([\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0][-1,0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([-\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=f([-\epsilon,v]).

Let:

((\epsilon,1),1),((1,g),1)\in\widehat{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R}).

\displaystyle((\epsilon,1),1)((1,g),1)

\displaystyle=((\epsilon,g),\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}((\epsilon,1),(1,g)))

\displaystyle=((\epsilon,g),1)

\displaystyle=\Big((1,g^{s(\epsilon)}),\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}((1,g^{s(\epsilon)}),(\epsilon,1))^{-1}\Big)\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big((1,g^{s(\epsilon)}),\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\widehat{c}_{z_{0}}(g,1)^{-1}\Big)\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big((1,g^{s(\epsilon)}),\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\Big)\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big).

3) If
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
corresponding to
u=a+bi\in\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C})
, we have:
\nu_{z_{0}}(1,g)=1
.

\nu_{2}(-1,g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}-1&\textrm{ if }g=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\\
1&\textrm{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.\qquad\qquad\tfrac{\overline{s}(g^{h_{-1}})}{\overline{s}(g)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\textrm{ if }g=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\\
a-bi&\textrm{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.

\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{h_{-1}})=\nu_{2}(-1,g^{h_{-1}})\tfrac{\overline{s}(g^{h_{-1}})}{\overline{s}(g)}=a-bi.

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([g,0])F([\epsilon,0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([\epsilon,0][g,0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([((\epsilon,g),1),0])(v)

\displaystyle=F([\Big((1,g^{s(\epsilon)}),\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g)^{-1}\Big)\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big),0])(v)

\displaystyle=\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(1,g^{s(\epsilon)})F([\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big),0])(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {unitact})}}{{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}e^{-\pi i(-bi+a)bv^{2}}f([1,v(-bi+a)]),&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
(bi+a)e^{\pi i(bi+a)bv^{2}}f([-1,v(bi+a)]),&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

4) If
g=u(b)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\nu_{z_{0}}(1,g)=1
and

\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g)=\nu_{2}(-1,g)\tfrac{\overline{s}(g)}{\overline{s}(g^{h_{-1}})}=\nu_{2}(-1,g)=1=\nu_{2}(-1,g^{s(-1)}).

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(1,g^{s(\epsilon)})F([\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big),0])(v).

By Example
5.14
, we have:

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([1,v])=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\tfrac{b}{2}i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi i(2i+b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}f([1,v^{\prime}])dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y}

(6.34)

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([-1,v])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\tfrac{b}{2}i}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi i(2i-b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}f([-1,v^{\prime}])dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y}.

(6.35)

5) If
g=h(a)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\nu_{z_{0}}(1,g)=1
and
\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{s(-1)})=\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g)=\nu_{2}(-1,g)\tfrac{\overline{s}(g)}{\overline{s}(g^{h_{-1}})}=\nu_{2}(-1,g)=(-1,a)_{\mathbb{R}}
.

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(1,g^{s(\epsilon)})F([\Big((\epsilon,1),1\Big),0])(v).

By Example
5.14
, we have:

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([1,v])=|a|^{1/2}\big(\tfrac{2}{1+a^{2}}\big)^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}f([1,v^{\prime}])e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y};

(6.36)

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f([-1,v])

\displaystyle=(-1,a)_{\mathbb{R}}|a|^{1/2}\big(\tfrac{2}{1+a^{2}}\big)^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}a)^{2}}f([-1,v^{\prime}])e^{-2\pi(v^{\prime}_{y})^{2}}dv^{\prime}_{x}dv^{\prime}_{y}.

(6.37)

Example 6.3
.

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, corresponding to
u=bi+a\in\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C})
. Let:

•

f^{1/2}_{+}([\epsilon,v])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}},&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
0,&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

•

f^{1/2}_{-}([\epsilon,v])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0,&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}},&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

•

f^{3/2}_{+}([\epsilon,v])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\tfrac{1}{2}ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}},&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
0,&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

•

f^{3/2}_{-}([\epsilon,v])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0,&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
\tfrac{1}{2}ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}},&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

Then:

(1)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{+}=f^{1/2}_{+}
,
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{+}=\overline{u}f^{3/2}_{+}
,
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{-}=uf^{1/2}_{-}
,
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{-}=u^{2}f^{3/2}_{-}
.

(2)

\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{1/2}_{+}=f^{1/2}_{-}
and
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{3/2}_{+}=f^{3/2}_{-}
.

Proof.

Let
f_{1}(v)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}
and
f_{2}(v)=\tfrac{1}{2}ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}
.

\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{h_{-1}})^{-1}=u.

1)

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{+}([1,v])

\displaystyle=\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f_{1}(v)=f_{1}(v)=f^{1/2}_{+}([1,v]).

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{-}([-1,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([-1,1])\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g^{h_{-1}})\nu(-1,g^{h_{-1}})^{-1}f_{1}([1,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([-1,1])uf_{1}([1,v])=uf^{1/2}_{-}([-1,v]).

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{+}([1,v])

\displaystyle=\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f_{2}(v)=\overline{u}f_{2}(v)=\overline{u}f^{3/2}_{+}([1,v]).

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{-}([-1,v])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([-1,1])\pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g^{h_{-1}})uf_{2}(v)=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}([-1,1])u^{2}f_{2}(v)=u^{2}f^{3/2}_{-}([-1,v]).

2) It follows from Eq.
6.29
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{D}^{+}=\operatorname{Span}\{f^{1/2}_{+},f^{1/2}_{-}\}
and
\mathcal{D}^{-}=\operatorname{Span}\{f^{3/2}_{+},f^{3/2}_{-}\}
. Let
\delta^{+}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}(1\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})
and
\delta^{-}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}(\overline{u}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

The actions of
\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}))
on
\mathcal{D}^{+}
and
\mathcal{D}^{-}
are isomorphic to
\delta^{+}
and
\delta^{-}
, respectively.

Proof.

\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})=\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\sqcup\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\widehat{h}_{-1}
, for
\widehat{h}_{-1}=[h_{-1},1]
. Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widehat{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
u=bi+a
.

a) Let
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
denote the complex functions on
\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that (1)
\operatorname{supp}e_{1}=\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\operatorname{supp}e_{2}=\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\widehat{h}_{-1}
, (2)
e_{1}(1)=1=e_{2}(\widehat{h}_{-1})
, (3)
e_{1}(\widehat{g})=\epsilon=e_{2}(\widehat{g}\widehat{h}_{-1})
. Let
V^{+}
be the vector space spanned by
e_{1},e_{2}
. The representation
\delta^{+}
can be realized on
V^{+}
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map from
\mathcal{D}^{+}
to
V^{+}
by sending
f^{1/2}_{+}
to
e_{1}
and
f^{1/2}_{-}
to
e_{2}
. Let us check that it indeed defines an intertwining operator.

\displaystyle\delta^{+}(g)e_{2}(\widehat{h}_{-1})

\displaystyle=e_{2}(\widehat{h}_{-1}[g,1])

\displaystyle=e_{2}([g^{h_{-1}},\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{-1},g)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g^{h_{-1}},h_{-1})^{-1}]\widehat{h}_{-1})

\displaystyle=e_{2}([g^{h_{-1}},\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}]\widehat{h}_{-1})=\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}=u;

\delta^{+}(g)e_{1}=e_{1},\delta^{+}(g)e_{2}=\nu_{z_{0}}(\epsilon,g^{s(\epsilon)})^{-1}e_{2},\delta^{+}(h_{-1})e_{1}=e_{2},\delta^{+}(h_{-1})e_{2}=e_{1};

\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{+})=\mathcal{A}(f^{1/2}_{+})=e_{1}=\delta^{+}(g)e_{1};

\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{1/2}_{-})=\mathcal{A}(uf^{1/2}_{-})=ue_{2}=\delta^{+}(g)e_{2};

\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{1/2}_{+})=\mathcal{A}(f^{1/2}_{-})=e_{2}=\delta^{+}(h_{-1})e_{1};

\mathcal{A}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{1/2}_{-})=\mathcal{A}(f^{1/2}_{+})=e_{1}=\delta^{+}(h_{-1})e_{2}.

b) Let
e_{3}
and
e_{4}
denote the complex functions on
\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that (1)
\operatorname{supp}e_{3}=\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\operatorname{supp}e_{4}=\widehat{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\widehat{h}_{-1}
, (2)
e_{3}(1)=1=e_{4}(\widehat{h}_{-1})
, (3)
e_{3}(\widehat{g})=\epsilon\overline{u}=e_{4}(\widehat{g}\widehat{h}_{-1})
. Let
V^{-}
be the vector space spanned by
e_{3},e_{4}
. The representation
\delta^{-}
can be realized on
V^{-}
. Let
\mathcal{B}
be the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map from
\mathcal{D}^{-}
to
V^{-}
by sending
f^{3/2}_{+}
to
e_{3}
and
f^{3/2}_{-}
to
e_{4}
. Let us check that it indeed defines an intertwining operator.

\displaystyle\delta^{-}(g)e_{4}(\widehat{h}_{-1})

\displaystyle=e_{4}(\widehat{h}_{-1}[g,1])

\displaystyle=e_{4}([g^{h_{-1}},\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(h_{-1},g)\widehat{C}_{z_{0}}(g^{h_{-1}},h_{-1})^{-1}]\widehat{h}_{-1})

\displaystyle=e_{4}([g^{h_{-1}},\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}]\widehat{h}_{-1})=u\nu_{z_{0}}(-1,g^{s(-1)})^{-1}=u^{2};

\delta^{-}(g)e_{3}=\overline{u}e_{3},\delta^{-}(g)e_{4}=u^{2}e_{4},\delta^{-}(h_{-1})e_{3}=e_{4},\delta^{-}(h_{-1})e_{4}=e_{3};

\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{+})=\mathcal{B}(\overline{u}f^{3/2}_{+})=\overline{u}e_{3}=\delta^{-}(g)e_{3};

\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)f^{3/2}_{-})=\mathcal{B}(u^{2}f^{3/2}_{-})=u^{2}e_{4}=\delta^{-}(g)e_{4};

\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{3/2}_{+})=\mathcal{B}(f^{3/2}_{-})=e_{4}=\delta^{-}(h_{-1})e_{3};

\mathcal{B}(\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})f^{3/2}_{-})=\mathcal{B}(f^{3/2}_{+})=e_{3}=\delta^{-}(h_{-1})e_{4}.

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Both
\delta^{+}
and
\delta^{-}
are irreducible representations.

Proof.

(\widehat{h}_{-1})^{-1}\widehat{g}\widehat{h}_{-1}=[g^{h_{-1}},u]
. Hence:

(1\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})^{\widehat{h}_{-1}}(\widehat{g})=(1\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})([g^{h_{-1}},u])=u\neq(1\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})(\widehat{g}),

(\overline{u}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})^{\widehat{h}_{-1}}(\widehat{g})=(\overline{u}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})([g^{h_{-1}},u])=u^{2}\neq(\overline{u}\cdot 1_{\mathbb{T}})(\widehat{g}).

∎

7.
Intertwining operators for
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
case: Schrödinger model to Lattice model

In this section,
\psi=\psi_{0}
and
z=z_{0}
.

7.1.
Lattice

(cf.
[
9
]
,
[
10
]
)
Let
w_{1}=ae_{1}+be_{1}^{\ast}
,
w_{2}=ce_{1}+de_{1}^{\ast}
be two elements of
W
. Then
\mathbb{Z}w_{1}+\mathbb{Z}w_{2}
forms a lattice in
W
iff
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
are
\mathbb{R}
-linear independence iff
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Moreover, it is a
\psi
-self dual lattice iff
\langle w_{1},w_{2}\rangle=1
or
-1
iff
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
denote the set of all
\psi
-self dual lattices in
W
. We can define a right
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
-action on
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{L}_{\psi}\times\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})&\longrightarrow&\mathcal{L}_{\psi};\\
(L=\mathbb{Z}w_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}w_{2},g)&\longmapsto&Lg=\mathbb{Z}(w_{1}g)\oplus\mathbb{Z}(w_{2}g).\end{array}

Let
L_{0}=\mathbb{Z}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}e_{1}^{\ast}\in\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
. Then:

•

\mathcal{L}_{\psi}=\{L_{0}g\mid g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\}
.

•

L_{0}g=L_{0}
iff
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})
.

•

There exists a bijection:
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\psi};g\longmapsto L_{0}g
.

7.2.
Lagrangian Grassmanian

(cf.
[
9
]
)
By Section
5.3
, we can identity
\mathbb{C}
with
W
by
a+ib\longrightarrow ae_{1}+be_{1}^{\ast}
, and define
\langle z,z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}=\operatorname{Im}(\overline{z}z^{\prime})
. A Lagrangian plane of
(\mathbb{C},\langle,\rangle_{\mathbb{C}})
is a set
\mathbb{R}z
, for some
z\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Let
\varLambda
denote the set of all Lagrangians in
\mathbb{C}
. Then there exists a
left
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action on
\Lambda
given as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\Lambda&\longrightarrow&\Lambda;\\
(t,\mathbb{R}z)&\longmapsto&t(\mathbb{R}z)=\mathbb{R}t(z).\end{array}

Then
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
acts transitively on
\Lambda
, and there exists a bijective map:

\mathbb{C}^{\times}/\mathbb{R}^{\times}\longrightarrow\Lambda;z\longmapsto\mathbb{R}z.

Note that
\mathbb{C}^{\times}/\mathbb{R}^{\times}\simeq\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C})/\mu_{2}
.

7.3.
Two models

Let
\Pi_{\psi}
denote the Heisenberg representation of
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
defined in Sections
6.2
, and
6.3
.
It can be realized on

\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(Y)=\operatorname{Ind}_{Y\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)}\psi_{Y}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(L)=\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)}\psi_{L},

for any
Y\in\Lambda
and
L\in\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
, where
\psi_{Y}
and
\psi_{L}
extend the character
\psi
on
\mathbb{R}
. In this section, we only consider the following case:

•

Y^{\ast}=X^{\ast}
,
Y=X
, and
L=\mathbb{Z}e_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}e_{1}^{\ast}
.

Note that the actions of
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(X^{\ast})
and
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(L)
are given by the formulas (
6.1
) (
6.2
) and (
6.25
) (
6.26
) respectively. By
[
38
, pp.164-165]
, or
[
18
, pp.142-145]
, there exists a pair of explicit isomorphisms between
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)
and
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(L)
, given as follows:

\begin{split}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})([\epsilon,w])&=\sum_{l\in L/L\cap X^{\ast}}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,w+l])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2}+\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,w+l])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2}),\end{split}

(7.1)

\begin{split}\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)([\epsilon,y])&=\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f([1,(\dot{y}^{\ast},0)]\cdot[\epsilon,(y,0)])d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},y\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,y+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])d\dot{y}^{\ast},\end{split}

(7.2)

for
w=x+x^{\ast}\in W
,
y\in X
,
f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(X^{\ast})
with
f^{\prime}|_{\mu_{2}\times X}\in S(\mu_{2}\times X)\subseteq L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)
,
f\in L^{1}(W)\cap\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(L)
.

Lemma 7.1
.

\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}
and
\theta_{X^{\ast},L}
define a pair of inverse intertwining operators between
\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)}\psi_{L}
and
\operatorname{Ind}_{X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)}\psi_{X^{\ast}}
.

Proof.

Let
g=(\epsilon^{\prime},w^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
, with
w^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+x^{\ast^{\prime}}\in X\oplus X^{\ast}
.

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f^{\prime})([\epsilon,w])

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,x+l][\epsilon^{\prime},w^{\prime},t^{\prime}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},x+l+w^{\prime},t^{\prime}+\tfrac{\langle x+l,w^{\prime}\rangle}{2}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}t^{\prime}+\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\langle x+l,w^{\prime}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\tfrac{\langle x^{\prime},w^{\prime}\rangle}{2})f^{\prime}([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},x+l+x^{\prime}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}t^{\prime}+\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\langle x,w^{\prime}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\tfrac{\langle x^{\prime},w^{\prime}\rangle}{2})f^{\prime}([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},x+l+x^{\prime}])\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\langle l,\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast}+x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\tfrac{\langle x,\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}t^{\prime}+\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}[\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}-\tfrac{\langle x^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast}\rangle}{2}])f^{\prime}([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},x+l+x^{\prime}])\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\langle l,\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast}+x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\tfrac{\langle x+x^{\prime},x^{\ast^{\prime}}+\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}t^{\prime}+\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}\tfrac{\langle w^{s(\epsilon^{\prime})},w^{\prime}\rangle}{2})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},w^{s(\epsilon^{\prime})}+w^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})([\epsilon,w][\epsilon^{\prime},w^{\prime},t^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)[\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})]([\epsilon,w]).

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f)([\epsilon,y])

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f([1,(\dot{y}^{\ast},0)]\cdot[\epsilon,(y,0)]\cdot[\epsilon^{\prime},(w^{\prime},t^{\prime})])d\dot{y}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f([1,(\dot{y}^{\ast},0)]\cdot[\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},(y+w^{\prime},t^{\prime}+\tfrac{\langle y,w^{\prime}\rangle}{2})])d\dot{y}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f\Big([1,(\dot{y}^{\ast},0)]\cdot[1,(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}x^{\ast^{\prime}},\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}(t^{\prime}+\langle y,x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle+\tfrac{\langle x^{\prime},x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}))]\cdot[\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},y+x^{\prime}]\Big)d\dot{y}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}(t^{\prime}+\langle y,x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle+\tfrac{\langle x^{\prime},x^{\ast^{\prime}}\rangle}{2}))\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)([\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime},y+x^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)([\epsilon,y]).

∎

For
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
, we can define

\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})]=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)].

(7.3)

Under such action, for
g_{1},g_{2}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
,

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g_{1})[\Pi_{L,\psi}(g_{2})f]&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g_{1})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}]([\Pi_{L,\psi}(g_{2})f])\\
&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}([\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g_{1})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g_{2})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)]))\\
&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g_{1})\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g_{2})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)]\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})\Pi_{L,\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})f.\end{split}

(7.4)

7.4.
Explicit elements I

Let us consider
g\in\operatorname{Sp}^{\pm}(L)
. Under the basis
\{e_{1},e_{1}^{\ast}\}
of
\mathcal{L}
,
\operatorname{Sp}^{\pm}(L)\simeq\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let
w=x+x^{\ast}\in W
.

Case
1
:
g=u(b)\in\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
, and
2\mid b
.

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f^{\prime}]([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle(x+l),\epsilon(x+l)b\rangle)f^{\prime}([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle(x+l),\epsilon(x+l)b\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,x+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,x+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x^{\ast}+xb\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}+xb\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=f([\epsilon,wg])=f([\epsilon,w]g).\end{split}

Case
2
:
g=h(a)\in\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
,
a=\pm 1
.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f^{\prime}]([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,(x+l)a])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},(x+l)a\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,xa+la+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},(x+l)a\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,xa+la+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle la,wa\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle xa,x^{\ast}a\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},xa+l\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,xa+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle l,wa\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle xa,x^{\ast}a\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f(\epsilon,wa)=(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,w]g).\end{split}

(7.5)

Case
3
:
g=h_{-1}\in\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
.

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f^{\prime}]([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}([-\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})f([-\epsilon,x+l-\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})f([-\epsilon,x+l-\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(-\epsilon\langle l,-x^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,-x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=f([-\epsilon,x-x^{\ast}])\\
&=f([\epsilon,w]g).\end{split}

(7.6)

Case
4
:
g=\omega\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

\nu(-1,g)=(1,-1)_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=i,\quad\quad\nu(-1,g)^{-1}=-i.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f^{\prime}]([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}\nu(\epsilon,g)f^{\prime}([\epsilon,y^{\ast}\omega^{-1}])\psi(\langle\epsilon(x+l),y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dy^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}\nu(\epsilon,g)f^{\prime}([\epsilon,y^{\ast}\omega^{-1}])\psi(\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x^{\ast}+y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dy^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,y^{\ast}\omega^{-1}+\epsilon\dot{z}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{z}^{\ast},y^{\ast}\omega^{-1}\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x^{\ast}+y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dy^{\ast}d\dot{z}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l^{\ast}\in L\cap X^{\ast}}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,(-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast})\omega^{-1}+\epsilon\dot{z}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{z}^{\ast},(-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast})\omega^{-1}\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle x,-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{z}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,-x^{\ast}\omega^{-1}-l+\epsilon\dot{z}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{z}^{\ast},-x^{\ast}\omega^{-1}-l\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle x,-x^{\ast}+l\omega^{-1}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{z}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,x^{\ast}\omega+l+\epsilon\dot{z}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{z}^{\ast},x^{\ast}\omega+l\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x\omega\rangle)\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{z}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,x^{\ast}\omega+l+\epsilon\dot{z}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{z}^{\ast},x^{\ast}\omega+l\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x\omega\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x^{\ast}\omega,x\omega\rangle}{2})d\dot{z}^{\ast}\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,wg])=\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,w]g).\end{split}

(7.7)

Case
5
:
g=\omega^{-1}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])&=\Pi_{L,\psi}(-\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega,-1)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)\Big(\Pi_{L,\psi}(-1)f\Big)([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\Big(\Pi_{L,\psi}(-1)f\Big)([\epsilon,w]\omega)\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega)(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,w]g)\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,g)f([\epsilon,w]g).\end{split}

(7.8)

Case
6
:
g=u_{-}(c)=\omega^{-1}u(-c)\omega\in\Gamma^{\pm}(2)
with
2\mid c
.

\nu(-1,\omega)=i,\qquad\nu(-1,\omega^{-1})=(-1,-1)_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=-i.

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])&=\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1}u(-c)\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},u(-c))^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(-c))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(-c))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\nu(\epsilon,\omega)f([\epsilon,wg])\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}f([\epsilon,wg])\\
&=e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}f([\epsilon,wg]).\end{split}

(7.9)

7.5.
Explicit elements II

We focus on the other elements

g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\setminus\Gamma(2)
. Let
w=xe+x^{\ast}e^{\ast}\in W
.

Case 7:
g=u(b)=u(1)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle(x+l),\epsilon(x+l)b\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,x+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle l,lb\rangle)f([\epsilon,x+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x^{\ast}+xb)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}+xb\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\psi(-\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x)\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f([\epsilon,x+l+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},x+l\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle l,x^{\ast}+xb+\tfrac{1}{2}e_{i}^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}+xb+\tfrac{1}{2}e_{i}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\psi(-\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x)f([\epsilon,x+x^{\ast}+xb+\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\ast}])\\
&=\psi(-\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x)f([\epsilon,wg+\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\ast}])\\
&=\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x)f([\epsilon,wg-\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\ast}]).\end{split}

Case 8:
g=u_{-}(-1)=\omega^{-1}u(1)\omega\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
w=(x,x^{\ast})
,
w\omega^{-1}=(x,x^{\ast})\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}=(-x^{\ast},x)
.

\begin{split}\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])&=\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1}u(1)\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},u(1))^{-1}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(1),\omega)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(1))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(1),\omega)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(1))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(1),\omega)^{-1}[\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(1))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)]f([\epsilon,w\omega^{-1}])\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(1),\omega)^{-1}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x^{\ast})[\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)]f([\epsilon,w\omega^{-1}u(1)+\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\ast}])\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(1),\omega)^{-1}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x^{\ast})f([\epsilon,wg+\tfrac{1}{2}e])\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\\
&=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x^{\ast})f([\epsilon,wg+\tfrac{1}{2}e])\\
&=m_{X^{\ast}}(g)^{-1}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon x^{\ast})f([\epsilon,wg+\tfrac{1}{2}e]).\end{split}

Case 9:
g=h(a)=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1}{n}&0\\
0&n\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
a=\tfrac{1}{n}
.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})([\epsilon,x+l])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([\epsilon,(x+l)a])\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}|a|^{1/2}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},(x+l)a\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,xa+la+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}|a|^{1/2}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},(x+l)a\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,xa+la+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle la,wa^{-1}\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle xa^{-1},x^{\ast}a\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}|a|^{1/2}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},\tfrac{1}{n}x+l+\tfrac{k}{n}\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,\tfrac{1}{n}x+l+\tfrac{k}{n}+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])(\tfrac{1}{n},\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\epsilon\langle l+\tfrac{k}{n},nw\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\tfrac{1}{n}x,nx^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\end{split}

\begin{split}&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}|a|^{1/2}\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\dot{y}^{\ast},\tfrac{1}{n}x+l+\tfrac{k}{n}\rangle}{2})f([\epsilon,\tfrac{1}{n}x+l+\tfrac{k}{n}+\epsilon\dot{y}^{\ast}])\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\tfrac{k}{n},nw\rangle}{2})\psi(\epsilon\langle l,nw\rangle)\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{\langle\tfrac{1}{n}x+\tfrac{k}{n},nx^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=|n|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f([\epsilon,w]g+\tfrac{k}{n}e)\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx^{\ast}).\end{split}

Case 10:
g=h(m)=\begin{pmatrix}m&0\\
0&\tfrac{1}{m}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, for
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then
g=\omega^{-1}h(m^{-1})\omega
.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f(\epsilon,w)\\
&=\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1}h(m^{-1})\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},h(m^{-1})\omega)^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h(m^{-1}),\omega)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(m^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(m^{-1}))\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w\omega^{-1}])\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})|m|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\omega)f([\epsilon,w]\omega^{-1}h(m^{-1})+\tfrac{k}{m}e)\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx)\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\nu(\epsilon,\omega)|m|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}f([\epsilon,w]g-\tfrac{k}{m}e^{\ast})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx)\\
&=|m|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}f([\epsilon,w]g-\tfrac{k}{m}e^{\ast})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx).\end{split}

(7.10)

Case 11:
g=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{m}{n}&0\\
0&\tfrac{n}{m}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, for
m,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
(n,m)=1
.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h(m),h(n^{-1}))^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(m))\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(n^{-1}))f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=|m|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Big[\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(n^{-1}))f\Big]([\epsilon,w]h(m)-\tfrac{k}{m}e^{\ast})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx)\\
&=|mn|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{n}f([\epsilon,w]g-\tfrac{kn}{m}e^{\ast}+\tfrac{l}{n}e)\psi\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon l(\tfrac{x^{\ast}}{m}-\tfrac{k}{m})\Big)\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx)\\
&=|mn|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{l=1}^{n}f([\epsilon,w]g-\tfrac{k}{m}e^{\ast}+\tfrac{l}{n}e)\psi\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon\tfrac{l}{m}x^{\ast}+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon\tfrac{k}{n}x-\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon\tfrac{kl}{nm}\Big).\end{split}

(7.11)

Case 12:
g=u(\tfrac{2q}{N})=\begin{pmatrix}1&\tfrac{2q}{N}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, for
(q,N)=1
and a positive integer number
N
.

u(\tfrac{2q}{N})=h(N^{-1})u(2qN)h(N).

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h(N^{-1}),u(2qN)h(N))^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(u(2qN),h(N))^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N^{-1}))\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(2qN))\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N))f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N^{-1}))\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(2qN))\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N))f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=|N|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Pi_{L,\psi}(u(2qN))\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N))f([\epsilon,w]h(N^{-1})+\tfrac{k}{N}e)\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx^{\ast})\\
&=|N|^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Pi_{L,\psi}(h(N))f\Big([\epsilon,w]h(N^{-1})u(2qN)+\tfrac{k}{N}e+2qke^{\ast}\Big)\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx^{\ast})\\
&=|N|^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}f([\epsilon,w]h(N^{-1})u(2qN)h(N)+ke+2qk\tfrac{1}{N}e^{\ast}-\tfrac{l}{N}e^{\ast})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon l\tfrac{x}{N})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon l\tfrac{k}{N})\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon kx^{\ast})\\
&=|N|^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\sum_{l=1}^{N}f([\epsilon,w]g+\tfrac{2qk}{N}e^{\ast}-\tfrac{l}{N}e^{\ast})\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{qkx}{N})\psi(-\epsilon\tfrac{qk^{2}}{N})\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{lx}{2N})\psi(\epsilon\tfrac{lk}{N}).\end{split}

(7.12)

Case 13:
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
. In this case, we choose positive rational numbers
\tfrac{m_{i}}{n_{i}}
approximating
a
. Note that the restriction of
\Pi_{L,\psi}
on
P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
is continuous. (cf.
[
6
]
)

\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])=\lim_{i}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\tfrac{m_{i}}{n_{i}})f([\epsilon,w]).

(7.13)

Case 14:
g_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, for a positive prime
p
. Let
\sqrt{p}=\lim_{i}\tfrac{n_{i}}{m_{i}}
, for coprime natural number pair
(n_{i},m_{i})
. Then:

g=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{p}&0\\
0&\sqrt{p}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{p}&0\\
0&\sqrt{p}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

\begin{split}&\Pi_{L,\psi}(g)f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{p}&0\\
0&\sqrt{p}^{-1}\end{pmatrix})^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})\Pi_{L,\psi}(\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{p}&0\\
0&\sqrt{p}^{-1}\end{pmatrix})f([\epsilon,w])\\
&=\nu(\epsilon,\omega^{-1})\Pi_{L,\psi}(\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{p}&0\\
0&\sqrt{p}^{-1}\end{pmatrix})f([\epsilon,w]\omega^{-1}).\end{split}

(7.14)

7.6.
Application

For
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(L)
, we write
f(1,w)=f_{1}(w)
and
f(-1,w)=f_{2}(w)
. Let:

\Gamma(4,2)=\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\mid a\equiv 1\equiv d(\bmod 4),b\equiv 0\equiv c(\bmod 2)\}.

Then
\Gamma_{\theta}=\langle\omega\rangle\Gamma(4,2)\simeq\langle\omega\rangle\ltimes\Gamma(4,2)
.

7.6.1.

Note that every element of
\Gamma_{\theta}
can be expressed as

r
or
r\omega
, for some
r\in\Gamma(2)
. By the above cases (1)—(6), we have:

\Pi_{L,\psi}(r)f_{1}(w)=\epsilon_{r}f_{1}(wr).

(7.15)

for some
\epsilon_{r}\in\mu_{8}
. Hence:

Lemma 7.2
.

The restriction of the cocycle
[\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}]
to the subgroup
\Gamma_{\theta}
is trivial, with an explicit trivialization

\epsilon:\Gamma_{\theta}\to\mu_{8};r\longmapsto\epsilon_{r},

such that
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\epsilon(r_{1})\epsilon(r_{2})\epsilon(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}
, for all
r_{i}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

\widetilde{\beta}(r)\epsilon_{r}=1
, for all
r\in\Gamma_{\theta}
.

Proof.

For any
r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
, we have the following relationship:

\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})=\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(r_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(r_{1}r_{2})=\epsilon_{r_{1}}\epsilon_{r_{1}}\epsilon_{r_{1}r_{2}}^{-1}.

(7.16)

Thus,
the map
r\to\widetilde{\beta}(r)\epsilon_{r}
defines a character of
\Gamma_{\theta}
. Since
\Gamma_{\theta}
is generated by
u(2)
,
u(-2)
and
\omega
, the result follows.
∎

Let
\widetilde{r}=[r,\epsilon]\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. According to Eq. (
7.15
), we have:

\Pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{r})f_{1}(w)=\pi_{\psi}(\widetilde{r})f_{1}(w)=\epsilon\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}f_{1}(wr)=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r})f_{1}(wr).

(7.17)

7.6.2.

Let us consider
\Gamma_{\theta}^{\pm}=\langle\omega\rangle\Gamma(2)^{\pm}
.
Let
r\in\Gamma_{\theta}
. By the above Cases
1-6
,
\Pi_{L,\psi}(r)f_{2}(w)=c_{r}f_{2}(wr)
. So
c_{r}
differs from
\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}
by a character
\chi_{\omega}
.
By Cases
1
and
6
, we known that
\chi_{\omega}(u(2))=1=\chi_{\omega}(u_{-}(2))
. By Case
4
,
\chi_{\omega}(\omega)=\nu(-1,\omega)=\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}=i
. Hence
