c_{r}=\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}\chi_{\omega}(r)
, with
(\chi_{\omega})|_{\Gamma(4,2)}=1
and
\chi_{\omega}(\omega)=i
. Let:

\lambda^{\epsilon}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=1,\\
\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}\chi_{\omega}(r)&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

Then:

\Pi_{L,\psi}(r)f([\epsilon,w])=\lambda^{\epsilon}(r)f([\epsilon,wr]).

It is known that
\Pi_{L,\psi}(h_{-1})f([\epsilon,w])=f([-\epsilon,wh_{-1}])
. For any
r\in\Gamma_{\theta}^{\pm}
, let us write
r=h_{\det(r)}(h_{\det(r)}r)
. Then:

\displaystyle\Pi_{L,\psi}(r)f([\epsilon,w])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{L,\psi}(h_{\det(r)}(h_{\det(r)}r))f([\epsilon,w])

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{\det(r)},h_{\det(r)}r)^{-1}\Pi_{L,\psi}(h_{\det(r)}r)f([\epsilon\det(r),wh_{\det(r)}])

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\epsilon\det(r)}(h_{\det(r)}r)f([\epsilon\det(r),wh_{\det(r)}h_{\det(r)}r])

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\epsilon\det(r)}(h_{\det(r)}r)f([\epsilon,w]r).

Lemma 7.4
.

For
\overline{r}=[r,t]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
,
\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})=\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})\chi_{\omega}(r)
.

Proof.

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1},r)\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{-1}r,h_{-1})=\nu_{2}(1,1)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(1,r)\nu_{2}(-1,r)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r^{h_{-1}},1)=\nu_{2}(-1,r),

[h_{-1},1]\overline{r}[h_{-1},t]=[h_{-1}rh_{-1},\nu_{2}(-1,r)t].

So
\overline{r}^{h_{-1}}=[h_{-1}rh_{-1},\nu_{2}(-1,r)t]
.
By Lemma
4.8
, for
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(4,2)
, we have:

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})=\left(\frac{-2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}t=\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}t=\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r}).

For
r=\omega
,

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})=-\epsilon^{-1}_{-1}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}t=ie^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}t,\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})=\epsilon^{-1}_{1}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}t=e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}t.

∎

8.
Intertwining operators for
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
case: Schrödinger model to Fock model

In this section we fix
\psi=\psi_{0}
and
z=z_{0}
.
By
[
31
,
\wasyparagraph
3]
or
[
26
, p. 401]
there is a unitary equivalence

\mathcal{H}(X^{*})\simeq L^{2}(X)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\mathcal{H}_{F_{z_{0}}}

of
\operatorname{H}(W)
-representations.
We extend it to a unitary equivalence

L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}

of
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
-representations, give two explicit maps, and include full proofs for completeness.
Identify
W=X\oplus X^{*}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}
and set
q_{z_{0}}(v)=e^{-\pi v^{2}}
.

•

\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}:\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}
.

\begin{split}\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)([\epsilon,v])&=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}f([\epsilon,x])dx.\end{split}

(8.1)

•

\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}:\mathcal{H}^{\pm}_{F_{z_{0}}}\longrightarrow L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times X)
.

\begin{split}\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)([\epsilon,x])&=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}\phi([\epsilon,v])dv_{x}dv_{y}.\end{split}

(8.2)

Let us verify that
\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}
,
\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}
both are
\operatorname{H}^{\pm}(W)
-intertwining operators. Let
h=(y,y^{\ast};t)\in\operatorname{H}(W)
and
h_{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

1)

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h)f]([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h)f]([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}\psi(\epsilon t+\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle+\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)f([\epsilon,x+y])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}(x-y)^{2}+(x-y)v\big)}\psi(\epsilon t+\epsilon\langle x-y,y^{\ast}\rangle+\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t-\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}(x-y)^{2}+(x-y)v\big)\psi(\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle)f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t-\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}yy^{\ast}+\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}-yv)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-xz_{0}y+xv+\epsilon xy^{\ast}\big)f([\epsilon,x])dx;

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}f]([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon t)e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(y\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon y^{*})y+vy]}[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}f]([\epsilon,v+y\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon y^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon t)e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(y\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon y^{*})y+vy]}2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+x(v+y\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon y^{\ast})\big)}f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t+\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon y^{*}y-vy)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+x(v+y\overline{z}_{0}+\epsilon y^{\ast})\big)}f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f]([\epsilon,v]).

2)

\displaystyle\kappa_{z_{0}}(v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast},v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v=v_{x}+iv_{y}}}{{=}}e^{-2\pi(v_{y}+y)(v_{y}+y)}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)e^{2\pi i(-\overline{v}y+vy+iy^{2})};

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}([\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h)\phi])([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)[\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h)\phi]([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)\psi(\epsilon t)e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(y\overline{z_{0}}+\epsilon y^{*})y+vy]}\phi([\epsilon,v+y\overline{z_{0}}+\epsilon y^{\ast}])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t)\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x(\overline{v}-yz-\epsilon y^{\ast})\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast},v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast})e^{-2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(y\overline{z_{0}}+\epsilon y^{*})y+(v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast})y]}\phi([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t)\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x(\overline{v}-yz-\epsilon y^{\ast})\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast},v-y\overline{z_{0}}-\epsilon y^{\ast})e^{-2\pi i[-\frac{1}{2}(y\overline{z_{0}}+\epsilon y^{*})y+vy]}\phi([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t)\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x(\overline{v}-yz_{0}-\epsilon y^{\ast})\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)e^{2\pi i[\frac{1}{2}(yz_{0}+\epsilon y^{*})y-\overline{v}y]}\phi([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v);

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h)[\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)]([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon t+\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle+\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)[\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)]([\epsilon,x+y])

\displaystyle=\psi(\epsilon t+\epsilon\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle+\epsilon\tfrac{1}{2}\langle y,y^{\ast}\rangle)\int_{\mathbb{C}}2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}\langle x+y,(x+y)z_{0}\rangle-\langle x+y,\overline{v}\rangle\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)\phi([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\psi(\epsilon t)\int_{\mathbb{C}}\psi\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+x(yz_{0}+\epsilon y^{\ast}-\overline{v})\big)e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}y(\epsilon y^{\ast})+\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}-y\overline{v}\big)}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)\phi([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}([\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h)\phi])([\epsilon,x]).

3)

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{-1})f]([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{-1})f]([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}f([-\epsilon,x])dx;

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)]([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)]([-\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}f([-\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}[\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{-1})f]([\epsilon,v]).

4)

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}([\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})\phi])([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)[\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})\phi]([\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}\kappa_{z_{0}}(v,v)\phi([-\epsilon,v])d_{z_{0}}(v);

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{-1})[\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)]([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)([-\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}\phi([-\epsilon,v])dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}([\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(h_{-1})\phi])([\epsilon,x]).

Let us verify that
\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}
and
\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}
are indeed inverses of one another. It suffices to show the following:

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}\Big[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)\Big]([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}\Big[\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)\Big]([\epsilon,v])dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}+yv\big)}f([\epsilon,y])dyd_{z_{0}}(v)

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}\big)}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}\big)}f([\epsilon,y])\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi ix\overline{v}}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{2\pi i\big(yv\big)}d_{z_{0}}(v)\bigg]dy

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}\big)}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}y^{2}\big)}f([\epsilon,y])\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{-2\pi\big((x+y)v_{y}\big)}e^{2\pi i\big((-x+y)v_{x}\big)}d_{z_{0}}(v)\bigg]dy

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\pi(xy-\tfrac{1}{2}x^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}y^{2})}f([\epsilon,y])dy\cdot\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-2\pi(\tfrac{1}{2}x+\tfrac{1}{2}y+v_{y})(\tfrac{1}{2}x+\tfrac{1}{2}y+v_{y})}e^{2\pi i\big((-x+y)v_{x}\big)}d_{z_{0}}(v)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\pi(xy-\tfrac{1}{2}x^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}y^{2})}f([\epsilon,y])dy\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg[e^{2\pi i\big((-x+y)v_{x}\big)}dv_{x}\bigg]

\displaystyle=f(x).

Proposition 8.1
.

For every
g\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,

\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}\!\bigl(\Pi_{X^{*},\psi}(g)f\bigr)=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\Pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(g)\!\bigl(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}(f)\bigr).

8.1.
Proof of Proposition
8.1

The identity is already known for
g=h_{-1}
and
g=\operatorname{diag}(r,r)
(
r>0
), so we may restrict to
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.
Recall that
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
is generated by
u(b)
,
h(a)
and
\omega
, and that Lemma
2.14
gives
\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}=1
for these generators.
Below we verify the identity for these generators; consequently

\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}\!\bigl(\pi_{X^{*},\psi}(g)f\bigr)=c_{g}\,\pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(g)\!\bigl(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}(f)\bigr)\quad\text{for all }g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

with some constants
c_{g}
.
Comparing both sides on
g_{1}g_{2}
yields

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}\!\bigl(\pi_{X^{*},\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})f\bigr)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{*}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}\!\bigl(\pi_{X^{*},\psi}(g_{1})\pi_{X^{*},\psi}(g_{2})f\bigr)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{*}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}c_{g_{1}}c_{g_{2}}\alpha_{z_{0}}(g_{1},g_{2})\pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})\!\bigl(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{*}}(f)\bigr),

so

\alpha_{z_{0}}(g_{1},g_{2})^{-1}=\widetilde{c}_{X^{*}}(g_{1},g_{2})\,c_{g_{1}g_{2}}\,c_{g_{2}}^{-1}c_{g_{1}}^{-1}.

Since these constants
c_{g}
are uniquely determined by this equality, Lemma
2.13
and Remark
5.12
force
c_{g}=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}
. Let
f=\big(\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(\phi)\big)
, for
\phi\in S(\mathbb{R})\subseteq L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

8.1.1.

Let
g=u(b)
.

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f\big)([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon bx^{2})f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon bx^{2}\big)}f([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon bx^{2}\big)}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v^{\prime}}\big]}k_{\widehat{z_{0}}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{\widehat{z_{0}}}(v^{\prime})dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon bx^{2}\big)}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v^{\prime}}\big]}dx\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi\big((2-\epsilon z_{0}b)x^{2}\big)}e^{2\pi ix\big(v-\overline{v^{\prime}}\big)}dx\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\textrm{ \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Fo}{}{}, App.A, Thm.1]} }}}2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[(2-\epsilon z_{0}b)^{-1/2}e^{-\pi((2-\epsilon z_{0}b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2})}\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{2i+\epsilon b}{2i})^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[e^{-\pi i\big[(2i+\epsilon b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}\big]}\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime}).

By Eq. (
6.31
), we have:

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)(\phi)([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\epsilon\tfrac{b}{2}i}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-\pi i(2i+\epsilon b)^{-1}(v-\overline{v^{\prime}})^{2}}k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([\epsilon,v]).

8.1.2.

Let
g=h(a)
.

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f\big)([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}|a|^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,xa])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}a^{2}x^{2}-xa\overline{v^{\prime}}\big]}k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi(v^{\prime})d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi(1+a^{2})x^{2}}e^{2\pi i\big(x(v-a\overline{v^{\prime}})\big)}dx\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\textrm{ \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Fo}{}{}, App.A, Thm.1]} }}}2^{\tfrac{1}{2}}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[(1+a^{2})^{-1/2}e^{-\pi\big((1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v}^{\prime}a)^{2}\big)}\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{2|a|}{1+a^{2}})^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\Big[e^{-\pi\big((1+a^{2})^{-1}(v-\overline{v}^{\prime}a)^{2}\big)}\Big]k_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime},v^{\prime})\phi([\epsilon,v^{\prime}])d_{z_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\textrm{Eq. \ref {z_05} }}}\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)(\phi)([\epsilon,v])=\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)\big(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)\big)([\epsilon,v]).

8.1.3.

Let
g=\omega
.

\displaystyle\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([\epsilon,v])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)f\big)([\epsilon,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xv\big)}dx\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nu(\epsilon,\omega)e^{2\pi i\epsilon xy}f([\epsilon,-y])dy

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,-y])dy\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+x(v+\epsilon y)\big)}dx\Big]

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\textrm{ \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Fo}{}{}, App.A, Thm.1]} }}}2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}f([\epsilon,-y])\Big[e^{-\pi(v+\epsilon y)^{2}}\Big]dy;

By Eq.
6.29
and
6.30
, we have:

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)\big(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)\big)([1,v])

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i(-i)v^{2}}\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)([1,v(-i)])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-xiv\big)}f([1,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{\pi\big(-x^{2}+2xv\big)}f([1,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{\pi\big(-y^{2}-2yv\big)}f([1,-y])dy

\displaystyle=\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([1,v]).

\displaystyle\Pi_{F_{z_{0}}}(g)\big(\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)\big)([-1,v])

\displaystyle=ie^{-\pi v^{2}}\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}(f)([-1,vi])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}i\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{2\pi i\big(\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}+xiv\big)}f([-1,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}i\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{\pi\big(-x^{2}-2xv\big)}f([-1,x])dx

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}i\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi v^{2}}e^{\pi\big(-y^{2}+2yv\big)}f([-1,-y])dy

\displaystyle=\theta_{F_{z_{0}},X^{\ast}}\big(\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)(f)\big)([-1,v]).

8.2.

Retain the notations from Example
6.3
.

1)

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(f^{1/2}_{+})([1,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}e^{-\pi x^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[-x(v_{x}-iv_{y})\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=2^{1/4}e^{-\pi x^{2}}.

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(f^{1/2}_{-})([-1,x])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=i\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}e^{-\pi x^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[-x(v_{x}-iv_{y})\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}ve^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=i\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 2^{1/4}e^{-\pi x^{2}}(-2ix)=2^{1/4}xe^{-\pi x^{2}}.

2)

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(f^{3/2}_{+})([1,x])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot 2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}ive^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=2^{1/4}xe^{-\pi x^{2}}.

\displaystyle\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(f^{3/2}_{-})([-1,x])

\displaystyle=2^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{C}}e^{2\pi i\big[\tfrac{1}{2}z_{0}x^{2}-x\overline{v}\big]}e^{-2\pi(v_{y})^{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{2}v^{2}}dv_{x}dv_{y}

\displaystyle=2^{1/4}e^{-\pi x^{2}}.

Definition 8.2
.

Let

A^{+}([\epsilon,x])=\begin{cases}\,e^{-\pi x^{2}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1,\end{cases}

A^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\begin{cases}0&\epsilon=1,\\
\,e^{-\pi x^{2}}&\epsilon=-1,\end{cases}

B^{+}([\epsilon,x])=\begin{cases}\,xe^{-\pi x^{2}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1,\end{cases}

B^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\begin{cases}0&\epsilon=1,\\
\,xe^{-\pi x^{2}}&\epsilon=-1.\end{cases}

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and
u=a+bi\in\operatorname{U}(\mathbb{C})
.

(1)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A^{+}=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}A^{+}
,
\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A^{-}=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}uA^{-}
.

(2)

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B^{+}=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\overline{u}B^{+}
,
\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B^{-}=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}u^{2}B^{-}
.

Proof.

By the above 1) and 2),
\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(2^{-1/4}f^{1/2}_{\pm})=A^{\pm}
, and
\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(2^{-1/4}f^{3/2}_{\pm})=B^{\pm}
.

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A^{+}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(2^{-1/4}f^{1/2}_{+})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}\Big(2^{-1/4}\Pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(g)f^{1/2}_{+}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Ex. \ref {exm2}}}}{{=}}\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(2^{-1/4}f^{1/2}_{+})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}A^{+};

\displaystyle\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B^{+}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}(2^{-1/4}f^{3/2}_{+})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}\Big(2^{-1/4}\Pi_{F_{z_{0}},\psi}(g)f^{3/2}_{+}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Ex. \ref {exm2}}}}{{=}}\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\theta_{X^{\ast},F_{z_{0}}}\Big(2^{-1/4}(-bi+a)f^{3/2}_{+}\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}(-bi+a)B^{+}.

The remaining equalities follow in the same fashion.
∎

Following Lemma
6.5
, for
[g,\epsilon]\in\widetilde{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we define:

\widetilde{\delta}^{+}:[g,\epsilon]\to\epsilon\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\delta^{+}(g),

\widetilde{\delta}^{-}:[g,\epsilon]\to\epsilon\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}\delta^{-}(g).

Lemma 8.4
.

\widetilde{\delta}^{+}
and
\widetilde{\delta}^{-}
both are irreducible representations of
\widetilde{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

Recall the isomorphism from (
2.12
):

\iota^{\prime}:\widetilde{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\to\widehat{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R});[g,\epsilon]\longmapsto[g,\epsilon\widetilde{s}(g)^{-1}].

Then
\widetilde{\delta}^{+}=\delta^{+}\circ\iota^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\delta}^{-}=\delta^{-}\circ\iota^{\prime}
. So both representations are irreducible.
∎

We also denote:

\mathcal{D}^{+}=\operatorname{Span}\{A^{+},A^{-}\},\quad\mathcal{D}^{-}=\operatorname{Span}\{B^{+},B^{-}\}.

For any functions
A\in\mathcal{D}^{+}
,
B\in\mathcal{D}^{-}
and
g\in\operatorname{O}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}([g,\epsilon])A=\widetilde{\delta}^{+}([g,\epsilon])A,\quad\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}([g,\epsilon])B=\widetilde{\delta}^{-}([g,\epsilon])B.

Similarly, for
[g,\epsilon]\in\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we define:

\overline{\delta}^{+}:[g,\epsilon]\to\epsilon\overline{s}(g)^{-1}\delta^{+}(g),

\overline{\delta}^{-}:[g,\epsilon]\to\epsilon\overline{s}(g)^{-1}\delta^{-}(g).

Then
\overline{\delta}^{+}
and
\overline{\delta}^{-}
both are irreducible representations. Moreover,
\overline{s}^{-1}:[g,\epsilon]\to\epsilon\overline{s}(g)^{-1}
defines a character.

\overline{\delta}^{+}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}(\overline{s}^{-1}\cdot 1_{\mu_{2}}),\quad\overline{\delta}^{-}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}(\overline{s}^{-1}\overline{u}\cdot 1_{\mu_{2}}).

(8.3)

9.
Siegel modular forms associated to Weil representations:
\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
&
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})

Unless otherwise stated, the following conventions are used throughout this section:

•

z\in\mathbb{H}
and
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
;

•

g_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{i}&b_{i}\\
c_{i}&d_{i}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
for
i=1,2
;

•

the character
\psi
is fixed to be
\psi_{0}
.

•

N
is a positive integer number, and
\chi
is a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
N
. We say
\chi
even if
\chi(-1)=1
and odd if
\chi(-1)=-1
. In particular,
N=1
,
\chi\equiv 1
.

We recall the functions
A^{\pm}
and
B^{\pm}
from Lemma
8.3
; as we work exclusively with the Weil representation of
\widetilde{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we abbreviate
A(x)=A^{+}([1,x])
and
B(x)=B^{+}([1,x])
for
x\in X\simeq\mathbb{R}
.
Let us define the Gauss sum:

G(n,\overline{\chi})=\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)e^{\tfrac{2\pi ikn}{N}}=\sum_{k\bmod N,(k,N)=1}\overline{\chi}(k)e^{\tfrac{2\pi ikn}{N}}.

The following result is known:

Lemma 9.1
.

(1)

|G(1,\overline{\chi})|=\sqrt{N}
.

(2)

G(n,\overline{\chi})=\chi(n)G(1,\overline{\chi})
.

9.1.
P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})

Let
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
and write
z_{p}=p(i)=ba+ia^{2}\in\mathbb{H}
.
By (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) we have

\widetilde{s}(p)=1;

\displaystyle\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A(x)

\displaystyle=\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h(a)u(a^{-1}b))A(x)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(u(a^{-1}b))A(xa)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{-1}ba^{2}x^{2})A(xa)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}e^{\pi ibax^{2}}\cdot e^{-\pi a^{2}x^{2}}

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}e^{i\pi z_{p}x^{2}};

\displaystyle\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B(x)

\displaystyle=\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h(a)u(a^{-1}b))B(x)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(u(a^{-1}b))B(xa)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{-1}ba^{2}x^{2})B(xa)

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}xae^{i\pi z_{p}x^{2}};

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})=\epsilon(p;z_{0},z_{0})\cdot|J(p,z_{0})|^{1/2}=|a|^{-1/2};

\displaystyle J_{3/2}(p,z_{0})=J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})J(p,z_{0})=|a|^{-1/2}a^{-1};

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A(x)=e^{i\pi xz_{p}x}.

\displaystyle J_{3/2}(p,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B(x)=e^{i\pi xz_{p}x}x.

9.2.
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

Write
z_{g}=g(i)=x_{g}+iy_{g}\in\mathbb{H}
,
g=p_{g}k_{g}
(cf. Eq.
2.6
),
z_{g}=z_{p_{g}}
. Then:

p_{g}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{y_{g}}&x_{g}(\sqrt{y_{g}})^{-1}\\
0&(\sqrt{y_{g}})^{-1}\end{pmatrix},k_{g}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{y_{g}}^{-1}a-(\sqrt{y_{g}})^{-1}x_{g}c&\sqrt{y_{g}}^{-1}b-(\sqrt{y_{g}})^{-1}x_{g}d\\
\sqrt{y_{g}}c&\sqrt{y_{g}}d\end{pmatrix}.

J_{1/2}(p_{g}k_{g},z_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref {J121J1}}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(p_{g},k_{g}(z_{0}))J_{1/2}(k_{g},z_{0})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(p_{g},k_{g})=J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\widetilde{s}(k_{g}).

J_{3/2}(p_{g}k_{g},z_{0})=J_{1/2}(p_{g}k_{g},z_{0})(cz_{0}+d)=(cz_{0}+d)J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\widetilde{s}(k_{g}).

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A(x)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\widetilde{s}(k_{g})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(p_{g},k_{g})^{-1}\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(k_{g})A(x)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\widetilde{s}(k_{g})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(k_{g})A(x)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref {ABM}}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A(x)

\displaystyle=e^{i\pi xz_{g}x}.

\displaystyle J_{3/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B(x)

\displaystyle=(cz_{0}+d)J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\widetilde{s}(k_{g})\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(p_{g},k_{g})^{-1}\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(k_{g})B(x)

\displaystyle=(cz_{0}+d)J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\widetilde{s}(k_{g})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(k_{g})B(x)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref {ABM}}}{{=}}(cz_{0}+d)J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})[(-\sqrt{y_{g}}cz_{0}+\sqrt{y_{g}}d)B](x)

\displaystyle=(cz_{0}+d)e^{i\pi xz_{g}x}(-\sqrt{y_{g}}cz_{0}+\sqrt{y_{g}}d)\sqrt{y_{g}}x

\displaystyle=e^{i\pi xz_{g}x}(cz_{0}+d)(-cz_{0}+d)y_{g}x

\displaystyle=e^{i\pi xz_{g}x}x

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B(x).

9.3.
\Gamma_{\theta}

Let
f\in\mathcal{H}(X^{*})
. Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
.

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)(0)=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f(l)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f(n).

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\overline{\chi}(k)f(l)\psi(\tfrac{kl}{N})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f(n)\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N}).

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)(cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)(\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\chi(d)\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\overline{\chi}(kd)f(l)\psi(\tfrac{kdl}{N})

\displaystyle=\chi(d)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f(n)\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N}).

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}(g)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}.

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(g)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)A(n)\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\Big(\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n).

\displaystyle\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(g)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{1/2}(g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)B(n)\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\Big(\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)

Note that
\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(g)
and
\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(g)
only depend on
z_{g}=g(z_{0})
.

Definition 9.2
.

If
\chi
is even, define

\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi zn^{2}}
,

and if
\chi
is odd, define

\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,n\,e^{i\pi n^{2}z}
.

Let us consider the explicit action of
\Gamma_{\theta}
on
\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)
and
\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)
.

1) For
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
, we have:

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}(rg)

\displaystyle=\theta^{1/2}(rp_{g})

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(rp_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(rp_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,p_{g})=1}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\pi_{L,\psi}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chapeq8})}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(r,z_{g})\epsilon_{r}\theta^{1/2}(g)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{cz_{g}+d}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\epsilon_{r}\theta^{1/2}(g)

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)\sqrt{cz_{g}+d}\cdot\theta^{1/2}(g)

\displaystyle=\lambda(r)\sqrt{cz_{g}+d}\cdot\theta^{1/2}(g).

Note that
[h(N^{-1})\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})h(N)]\cap\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})=\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
.

2) Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
cN^{2}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
.

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(rg)

\displaystyle=\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(rp_{g})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(rg,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(rg)A\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,p_{g})=1}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\pi_{L,\psi}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chapeq8})}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\chi(d)\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(n)\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\chi(d)\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(g)

\displaystyle=\chi(d)\lambda(r)\sqrt{cN^{2}z_{g}+d}\cdot\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(g).

\displaystyle\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(rg)

\displaystyle=\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(rp_{g})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(rg,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(rg)B\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,p_{g})=1}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{3/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\pi_{L,\psi}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chapeq8})}}{{=}}J_{3/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\chi(d)\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(n)\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N})

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(r,p_{g}(z_{0}))\epsilon_{r}\chi(d)\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(g)

\displaystyle=\chi(d)\lambda(r)\sqrt{cN^{2}z_{g}+d}(cN^{2}z_{g}+d)\cdot\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(g).

We conclude:

Theorem 9.3
.

For
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

(1)

\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(rz)=\chi(d)\lambda(r)\sqrt{cz+d}\,\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)
.

(2)

\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(rz)=\chi(d)\lambda(r)\sqrt{cz+d}(cz+d)\,\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)
.

Note that
\chi
defines a character of
\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})/\Gamma_{1}(N^{2})
.

9.4.
\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}

We let
\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
act on
\mathbb{H}
through its projection to
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 9.4
.

The map
\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}:\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\to\mathbb{T}
defined by

[r,\epsilon]\longmapsto\lambda(r)\chi(r)\epsilon

is a character.

Proof.

Recall the character
\overline{\lambda}
from Lemma
4.7
. The character
\chi
can also be viewed as a character of
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. Then
\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}=\overline{\lambda}\otimes\chi
.
∎

According to Theorem
9.3
, we have:

Theorem 9.5
.

For
\overline{r}=[r,\epsilon]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

(1)

\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)
.

(2)

\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)
.

9.5.
Twisted by
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}

Let
\mathfrak{w}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, and let
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=(\mathfrak{w},t_{\mathfrak{w}})\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. For
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, let
\overline{r}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. We define a character on this conjugated subgroup by

\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}):=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s}).

Definition 9.6
.

Define the slash operators for the theta functions as follows:

(1)

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z):=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)
;

(2)

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z):=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)
.

Proposition 9.7
.

For
\overline{r}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
, we have:

(1)

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)
.

(2)

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)
.

Proof.

Write
\overline{r}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
.

(1)

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r},z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z).

\displaystyle[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z).

(2)

\displaystyle J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r},z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z).

\displaystyle[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r})[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z).

∎

Let
t
now be a positive integer, and let
\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
. We consider the associated element
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
.
Then:

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{1/4}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{1/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)e^{\pi iztn^{2}}

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{3/4}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{3/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)ne^{\pi iztn^{2}}.

Definition 9.8
.

•

\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(tN^{2},t^{-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
.

•

\theta^{1/2}_{\chi,t}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)e^{\pi iztn^{2}}
.

•

\theta^{3/2}_{\chi,t}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)ne^{\pi iztn^{2}}
.

Proposition 9.9
.

For
\overline{r}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta,t}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(tN^{2},t^{-1})
, we have:

(1)

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi,t}](\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi,t}](z)
.

(2)

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi,t}](\overline{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi,t}](z)
.

9.6.
Induction to
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
:
N
odd

Recall notations from Def.
4.11
. By Lemma
4.3
, we have:

\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})=\sqcup_{i=1}^{3}[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{M}_{q_{i}},

\overline{\Gamma}(N^{2})=\sqcup_{i=1}^{3}[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}(N^{2})]\overline{M}_{q_{i}}.

Definition 9.10
.

\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}[\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}]^{-1},\overline{M}_{\chi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}\mathbb{C}.

Lemma 9.11
.

\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}
is an irreducible representation.

Proof.

If
N=1
, the result holds by Prop.
4.13
(2). Assume
N>1
. Note that
\chi
is a character of
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. So

\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}\simeq\chi^{-1}\Big(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}[\overline{\lambda}]^{-1}\Big)\simeq\chi^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}.

Note that
\overline{\gamma}
has three dimension. By Clifford theory,
\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}
is irreducible, so is
\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}
.
∎

For our purposes, we give an explicit analysis of the representation
\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}
. The vector space
\overline{M}_{\chi}
consists of the functions
f:\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that
f(\overline{r}\overline{g})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})^{-1}f(\overline{g})
, for
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
and
\overline{g}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. Let
\overline{e}_{i}
denote the function of
\overline{M}_{\chi}
, supported on
\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
and
\overline{e}_{i}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}})=1
. Then
\overline{M}=\oplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{C}\overline{e}_{i}
. Write

\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{g})(\overline{e}_{1},\overline{e}_{2},\overline{e}_{3})=(\overline{e}_{1},\overline{e}_{2},\overline{e}_{3})M(\overline{g}),

for some
3\times 3
-matrix
M(\overline{g})
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}:&\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})&\longrightarrow&M_{3}(\mathbb{C});\\
&\overline{g}&\longmapsto&M(\overline{g}),\end{array}

gives a matrix representation. More precisely, we have:

•

(a)

\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{r}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}
, for some
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
iff
\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{r}=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{j}}
.

(b)

If the above condition holds, then
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r})(\overline{e}_{j})](\overline{M}_{q_{i}})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})^{-1}
, and
\operatorname{supp}[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r})](\overline{e}_{j})=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
. So
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r})](\overline{e}_{j})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})^{-1}\overline{e}_{i}
, and
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})](\overline{e}_{i})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\overline{e}_{j}
.

•

(c)

\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{r}}
, for some
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
iff
[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{i}}]\overline{M}_{q_{j}}=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{r}}
.

(d)

If the above condition holds, then
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}})(\overline{e}_{r})](\overline{M}_{q_{i}})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})^{-1}
, and
\operatorname{supp}\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}})(\overline{e}_{r})=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
. So
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}})](\overline{e}_{r})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})^{-1}\overline{e}_{i}
, and
[\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}}^{-1})](\overline{e}_{i})=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\overline{e}_{r}
.

We now define:

\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{1\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z),[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big),

\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{1\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z),[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big).

9.6.1.

Let
\overline{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. Let
z=z_{g}\in\mathbb{H}
. Write
\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{r}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}
, for some
\overline{M}_{q_{j}}
,
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
.

\displaystyle[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}}}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{j}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{j}}}(z).

9.6.2.

Let
\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
. Write
\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{r}}
, for some
\overline{M}_{q_{r}}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
,
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
.

\displaystyle[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}}}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}}\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{r}}z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\overline{M}_{q_{j}}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{r}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{r}},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{r}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{j}},z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s})[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{r}}}(z).

Theorem 9.12
.

Let
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. Then:

(1)

\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

(2)

\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

Proof.

1a) If
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, then by the results of Section
9.6.1
and the above (a)(b), the equality holds.

1b) If
\overline{r}=\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
, by the results of Section
9.6.2
and the above (c)(d), the equality holds.

1c) If
\overline{r}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
, for some
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, then

\displaystyle\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{i}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{i}}z)\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}}z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{s}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{i}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}},z)\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}}^{-1})\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{s}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

The proof of Part (2) is similar.
∎

9.7.
Induction to
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
:
N
even

In this case, let
\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[u(1),1]
and
\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[1,1]
. By Lemma
4.4
, we have:

\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})=\sqcup_{i=1}^{2}[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{M}_{q_{i}},\qquad[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\unlhd\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2}).

Definition 9.13
.

\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}[\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}]^{-1},\overline{M}_{\chi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}\mathbb{C}.

Lemma 9.14
.

(1)

If
N\equiv 2(\bmod 4)
, then
\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}
is an irreducible representation of dimension
2
.

(2)

If
N\equiv 0(\bmod 4)
, then
\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}
is a reducible representation of dimension
2
with two different irreducible components.

Proof.

Let
\overline{r}=[\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix},\epsilon]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})\subseteq\overline{\Gamma}(2)
. Then

\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[u(-1)ru(1),\epsilon]=[\begin{pmatrix}a-c&a+b-c-d\\
c&c+d\end{pmatrix},\epsilon],

\chi(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})=\chi(\overline{r}),

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})=\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{2c}{d}\Bigr)\epsilon_{d}^{-1},\qquad\overline{\lambda}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})=\epsilon\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)\epsilon_{c+d}^{-1}.

Since
N^{2}\mid c
,
4\mid c
and
\epsilon_{c+d}^{-1}=\epsilon_{d}^{-1}
. To prove the result, it suffices to check whether
\left(\tfrac{2c}{d}\right)
and
\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)
are equal or not.

(1)

If
N\equiv 2(\bmod 4)
, then
N^{2}\equiv 4(\bmod 8)
. We choose:

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-1-2N^{2}&2\\
N^{2}&-1\end{pmatrix}.

Then:

(\tfrac{2c}{d})=(\tfrac{2N^{2}}{-1})=1,\quad\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{2N^{2}}{N^{2}-1}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{2}{N^{2}-1}\Bigr)=-1.

(2)

If
N\equiv 0(\bmod 4)
, then
N^{2}\equiv 0(\bmod 16)
. Write
c=N^{2}r=[\pm 2^{k}]r_{1}
with an odd positive number
r_{1}
. If
c=0
,
\left(\tfrac{2c}{d}\right)=\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)
. In the following, we assume that
c\neq 0
. Then

\Bigl(\tfrac{2c}{d}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{2c}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{\pm 2^{k}}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{r_{1}}{(c+d)d}\Bigr).

(i)

If
(c+d)d>0
, then
(c+d)d\equiv cd+d^{2}\equiv d^{2}\equiv 1(\bmod 8)
. So
\Bigl(\frac{2}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=1
.

(ii)

If
(c+d)d<0
, then
-(c+d)d\equiv-cd-d^{2}\equiv-d^{2}\equiv-1(\bmod 8)
. So
\Bigl(\frac{2}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{2}{-1}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{2}{-(c+d)d}\Bigr)=1
.

(iii)

If
(c+d)d>0
, then
(c+d)d\equiv cd+d^{2}\equiv d^{2}\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
. So
\Bigl(\frac{-1}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=1
.

(iv)

If
(c+d)d<0
, then
-(c+d)d\equiv-cd-d^{2}\equiv-d^{2}\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
. So
\Bigl(\frac{-1}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{-1}{-1}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{-1}{-(c+d)d}\Bigr)=(-1)(-1)=1
.

(v)

If
(c+d)d>0
, then
\Bigl(\frac{r_{1}}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{(c+d)d}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{((c+d)d-1)}{2}}=\Bigl(\frac{(c+d)d}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{((c+d)d-1)}{2}}=\Bigl(\frac{d^{2}}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{((c+d)d-1)}{2}}=(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{((c+d)d-1)}{2}}=1
.

(vi)

If
(c+d)d<0
, then
\Bigl(\frac{r_{1}}{(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{r_{1}}{-(c+d)d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{-(c+d)d}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{(-(c+d)d-1)}{2}}=\Bigl(\frac{-d^{2}}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{(-(c+d)d-1)}{2}}=\Bigl(\frac{-1}{r_{1}}\Bigr)(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{(-d^{2}-1)}{2}}=(-1)^{\tfrac{(r_{1}-1)}{2}\tfrac{(-d^{2}+1)}{2}}=1
.

Hence,
\Bigl(\tfrac{2c}{d}\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)=1
and
\Bigl(\tfrac{2c}{d}\Bigr)=\Bigl(\frac{2c}{c+d}\Bigr)
.

∎

We now define:

\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{1\times 2}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\Big),

\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{1\times 2}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\Big).

Analogue of the odd case, we have:

Theorem 9.15
.

Let
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
. Then:

(1)

\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

(2)

\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

9.8.
Tensor induction to
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})

Let us give the following definitions:

•

If
N
is odd,

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z).

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z).

•

If
N
is even,

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z).

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z).

Theorem 9.16
.

Let
g\in G=\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
,
H=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
.

(1)

If
N
is odd, then
[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(gz)=J_{\kappa/2}(g,z)^{3}[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(V_{G\to H}(g))
, for
\kappa=1
or
3
.

(2)

If
N
is even, then
[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(gz)=J_{\kappa/2}(g,z)^{2}[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\otimes}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(V_{G\to H}(g))
, for
\kappa=1
or
3
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Section
4.10
. Retain the notations from Sect.
4.9
. In this case,
x_{i}^{-1}=\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
,
\sigma=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}
. Write

g^{-1}\overline{M}^{-1}_{q_{i}}=\overline{M}^{-1}_{q_{\tau_{g}(i)}}h^{-1}_{g,x_{\tau_{g}(i)}},\quad\overline{M}_{q_{i}}g=h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}(i)}}\overline{M}_{q_{\tau_{g}(i)}},\quad\quad h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}(i)}}\in H.

1) By Sections
9.6.1
–
9.6.2

[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}}}(gz)=J_{\kappa/2}(g,z)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{\tau_{g}(i)}}}(z)\sigma(h_{g,x_{\tau_{g}(i)}}).

Hence:

\displaystyle[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(gz)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(gz)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(gz)

\displaystyle=J_{\kappa/2}(g,z)^{3}[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\sigma^{-1}(V_{G\to H}(g^{-1}))

\displaystyle=J_{\kappa/2}(g,z)^{3}[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)[\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\sigma(V_{G\to H}(g)).

2) Similarly.
∎

10.
Siegel modular forms associated to Weil representations:
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
&
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})

Unless otherwise stated, the following conventions are used throughout this section:

•

z\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
and
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
;

•

g_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{i}&b_{i}\\
c_{i}&d_{i}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
for
i=1,2
;

•

the character
\psi
is fixed to be
\psi_{0}
;

•

N
is a positive integer number, and
\chi
is a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
N
;

•

Define a Galois action of
\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R})=\langle\sigma\rangle
on
\mathbb{C}
by

z\mapsto z^{\sigma}
.

10.1.
Weil representations of
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

Note that

\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{GL}}^{8}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),\quad\quad\overline{\operatorname{GL}}^{8}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\simeq}}\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R});[h,\epsilon]\longmapsto[h,m_{X^{\ast}}(h)\epsilon].

We modify the Weil representation from the
8
-fold covering group
\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
to the two-fold covering groups
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

via the above embedding map. We denote Weil representations of
\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
by
\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}
and
\overline{\Pi}_{L,\psi}
. They differ from
\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}
and
\Pi_{L,\psi}
by the factors
m_{X^{\ast}}(h)
. For simplicity, we will take these factors in account without repeating the corresponding results.

10.2.
Automorphic factors

We recall
\overline{\delta}^{+}
and
\overline{\delta}^{-}
from (
8.3
). By Lemma
3.38
, there exists a group embedding from

\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
to
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
. We extend
\overline{\delta}^{+}
and
\overline{\delta}^{-}
to
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
z^{k}\overline{z}^{l}
denote the character of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
by sending
z
to
z^{k}\overline{z}^{l}
. Now define:

(\overline{\delta}^{+}=\operatorname{Ind}^{\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}z^{-1},\mathcal{D}^{+}=\operatorname{Ind}^{\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\mathbb{C}),\qquad(\overline{\delta}^{-}=\operatorname{Ind}^{\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}z^{-3},\mathcal{D}^{-}=\operatorname{Ind}^{\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\mathbb{C}).

Recall
\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{R}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\cup\omega_{\sigma}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Note that
\mathcal{D}^{+}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2};f\to\begin{bmatrix}f(1)\\
f(\omega_{\sigma})\end{bmatrix}
. For any
t\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,

\overline{\delta}^{+}(t)f(1)=f(t)=t^{-1}f(1),\quad\overline{\delta}^{+}(t)f(\omega_{\sigma})=f(\omega_{\sigma}t)=f(\overline{t}\omega_{\sigma})=\overline{t}^{-1}f(\omega_{\sigma}).

So the restriction of
\overline{\delta}^{+}
to
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is isomorphic to the representation:

z\to\begin{pmatrix}z^{-1}&0\\
0&\overline{z}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Similarly, the restriction of
\overline{\delta}^{-}
to
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is isomorphic to the representation:

z\to\begin{pmatrix}z^{-3}&0\\
0&\overline{z}^{-3}\end{pmatrix}.

For
\overline{h}\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, let us define:

(1)

J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{h},z)=(\overline{\delta}^{+})^{-1}(J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z))=\begin{pmatrix}J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)&0\\
0&\overline{J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)}\end{pmatrix}\\
=\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}&0\\
0&\overline{\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}}\end{pmatrix}
;

(2)

J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{h},z)=(\overline{\delta}^{-})^{-1}(J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z))=\begin{pmatrix}J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)^{3}&0\\
0&\overline{J_{1/2}(\overline{h},z)}^{3}\end{pmatrix}

=\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}[\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)]&0\\
0&\epsilon^{-1}\overline{\sqrt{\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(cz+d)}}[\operatorname{sgn}(\det h)(c\overline{z}+d)]\end{pmatrix}
.

Lemma 10.1
.

J_{\delta^{\pm}}(\overline{h}_{1}\overline{h}_{2},z)=J_{\delta^{\pm}}(\overline{h}_{1},\overline{h}_{2}z)J_{\delta^{\pm}}(\overline{h}_{2},z)
, for
\overline{h}_{i}\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.37
.
∎

10.3.
\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

We recall the functions
A^{\pm}
,
B^{\pm}
from Lemma
8.3
. For
\overline{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\overline{\delta}^{+}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix},\quad\quad\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\overline{\delta}^{-}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix},

where

•

\overline{\delta}^{+}:[g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix},\epsilon]\to\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{ai+b}^{-1}&0\\
0&\epsilon^{-1}\overline{\sqrt{ai+b}}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
and
[g=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},1]\to\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
,

•

\overline{\delta}^{-}:[g=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
b&a\end{pmatrix},\epsilon]\to\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{ai+b}^{-3}&0\\
0&\epsilon^{-1}\overline{\sqrt{ai+b}}^{-3}\end{pmatrix}
and
[g=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},1]\to\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
.

For
\overline{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix},\quad J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}.

For
\overline{g}=[h_{-1},1]\in\overline{\operatorname{O}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{-}\\
A^{+}\end{bmatrix},\quad J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}B^{-}\\
B^{+}\end{bmatrix}.

10.4.
P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})

Let
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&\det(p)a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
(
a>0
) and write
z_{p}=p(i)=\det(p)ba+i\det(p)a^{2}\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
.
By (
6.2
)–(
6.4
), we have:

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h_{\det(p)}h(a)u(a^{-1}b))f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(h(a)u(a^{-1}b))f([\det(p)\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}(a,\det(p)\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\Pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(u(a^{-1}b))f([\det(p)\epsilon,xa])

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}(a,\det(p)\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\det(p)\epsilon a^{-1}b(xa)^{2})f([\det(p)\epsilon,xa])

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}(a,\det(p)\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\det(p)\epsilon abx^{2})f([\det(p)\epsilon,xa])

\displaystyle=|a|^{1/2}(a,\det(p)\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\pi i\det(p)\epsilon abx^{2}}f([\det(p)\epsilon,xa])

\displaystyle=a^{1/2}e^{\pi i\det(p)\epsilon abx^{2}}f([\det(p)\epsilon,xa]).

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {jpz}}}{{=}}|a|^{-1/2}=a^{-1/2};

\displaystyle[J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})]^{3}=a^{-3/2};

If
\det(p)>0
, then:

J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A^{+}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\epsilon=1,\\
e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

[J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})]^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B^{+}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

[J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})]^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\epsilon=1,\\
xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

If
\det(p)<0
, then:

J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A^{+}([\epsilon,x])=e^{-\pi i\epsilon abx^{2}}A^{+}([-\epsilon,xa])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\epsilon=1,\\
e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

J_{1/2}(p,z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)A^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

[J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})]^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B^{+}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}0&\epsilon=1,\\
xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&\epsilon=-1,\end{array}\right.

[J_{1/2}(p,\pm z_{0})]^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p)B^{-}([\epsilon,x])=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&\epsilon=1,\\
0&\epsilon=-1.\end{array}\right.

We conclude:

•

For
\overline{p}=[p,1]\in P_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{p},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{p})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}([1,x])&A^{+}([-1,x])\\
A^{-}([1,x])&A^{-}([-1,x])\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&0\\
0&e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix},

J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{p},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{p})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}([1,x])&B^{+}([1,x])\\
B^{-}([1,x])&B^{-}([-1,x])\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&0\\
0&xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix}.

•

For
\overline{h}_{-1}=[h_{-1},1]
, we have:

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{h}_{-1}\overline{p},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{h}_{-1}\overline{p})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}([1,x])&A^{+}([-1,x])\\
A^{-}([1,x])&A^{-}([-1,x])\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}\\
e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&0\\
0&e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix}\overline{\delta}^{+}(\overline{h}_{-1}),

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{h}_{-1}\overline{p},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{h}_{-1}\overline{p})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}([1,x])&B^{+}([-1,x])\\
B^{-}([1,x])&B^{-}([-1,x])\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}\\
xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{p}}&0\\
0&xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix}\overline{\delta}^{-}(\overline{h}_{-1}).

10.5.
\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

Write
z_{g}=g(i)=x_{g}+iy_{g}\in\mathbb{H}^{\pm}
,
g=p_{g}k_{g}
(cf. Lem.
3.28
),
z_{g}=z_{p_{g}}
. Then:

p_{g}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)}&(\det g)x_{g}(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}\\
0&(\det g)(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}\end{pmatrix};

k_{g}=\begin{pmatrix}(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}a-(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}x_{g}c&(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}b-(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})^{-1}x_{g}d\\
(\det g)(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})c&(\det g)(\sqrt{y_{g}(\det g)})d\end{pmatrix}.

For
\overline{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we write
\overline{g}=p_{g}\overline{k}_{g}
with
p_{g}\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
,
\overline{k}_{g}\in\overline{\operatorname{SO}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(1)

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{k}_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{k}_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\begin{bmatrix}e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{g}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)>0,\\
\begin{bmatrix}0\\
e^{\pi ix^{2}z_{g}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{k}_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{k}_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)>0,\\
\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{g}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.

(2)

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{-}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{k}_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{k}_{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{-}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,x])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\begin{bmatrix}xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{g}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)>0,\\
\begin{bmatrix}0\\
xe^{\pi ix^{2}z_{g}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{-}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{k}_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{k}_{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{-}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,x])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{p}^{\sigma}}\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)>0,\\
\begin{bmatrix}xe^{-\pi ix^{2}z_{g}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.

10.6.
\Gamma_{\theta}^{\pm}

Let
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}(X^{\ast})
,
g\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\overline{g}=[g,\epsilon]\in\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)([\epsilon,0])=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f([\epsilon,l])=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f([\epsilon,n]).

(10.1)

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)([\epsilon,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f([\epsilon,n])\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N}).

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f)([\epsilon,cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\chi(d)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f([\epsilon,n])\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N}).

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,n])\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z^{\sigma}_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,n])\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z^{\sigma}_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,3/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,3/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{\delta^{-}}(g,z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(g)\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([1,n])\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z^{\sigma}_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}B^{+}\\
B^{-}\end{bmatrix}([-1,n])\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{-i\pi z^{\sigma}_{g}n^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0,\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{-i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

Note that
\theta^{\pm,1/2}_{\epsilon,\chi}(\overline{g})
,
\theta^{\pm,3/2}_{\epsilon,\chi}(\overline{g})
only depend on
z_{g}=g(z_{0})
,
z_{g}^{\sigma}=g(-z_{0})
.

Definition 10.2
.

(1)

If
\chi
is even, define:

(a)

\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi zn^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
0&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0,\end{array}\right.

\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,e^{-i\pi zn^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0.\end{array}\right.

(b)

\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)e^{i\pi z^{\sigma}n^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0,\end{array}\right.

\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)e^{-i\pi z^{\sigma}n^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
0&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0.\end{array}\right.

(2)

If
\chi
is odd, define:

(a)

\theta^{+,3/2}_{1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,ne^{i\pi zn^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
0&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0,\end{array}\right.

\theta^{+,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,ne^{-i\pi zn^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0.\end{array}\right.

(b)

\theta^{-,3/2}_{1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)ne^{i\pi z^{\sigma}n^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0,\end{array}\right.

\theta^{-,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)ne^{-i\pi z^{\sigma}n^{2}}&\operatorname{Im}(z)>0,\\
0&\operatorname{Im}(z)<0.\end{array}\right.

(3)

(a)
