\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(z)&\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(z)&\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)\end{bmatrix}
.

(b)

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,3/2}_{1,\chi}(z)&\theta^{+,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)\\
\theta^{-,3/2}_{1,\chi}(z)&\theta^{-,3/2}_{-1,\chi}(z)\end{bmatrix}
.

Similar to Theorems
9.3

9.5
, the following result holds:

Lemma 10.3
.

For
\overline{r}=[r,\epsilon]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

(1)

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})&0\\
0&\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})\end{bmatrix}
.

(2)

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(z)\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})&0\\
0&\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})\end{bmatrix}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
9.3
. To be easily checked, we give a detailed proof for the first statement.
Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
cN^{2}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
.

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(r\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(r\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(rp_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(rp_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chapeq8})}}{{=}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\chi(d)\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,n])\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\chi(d)\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(r,z_{g})\chi(d)\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}\lambda(r).

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(r\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(r\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(rp_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(rp_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chapeq8})}}{{=}}\tfrac{\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\chi_{\omega}(r)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)}{G(1,\chi)}J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\chi_{\omega}(r)\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,cNke+\tfrac{kd}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\chi(d)\chi_{\omega}(r)\tfrac{\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(p_{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,n])\overline{\chi}(kd)\psi(\tfrac{kdn}{N})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(r,p_{g}z_{0})\epsilon_{r}m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\chi_{\omega}(r)\chi(d)\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {hminustoh}}}{{=}}J_{\delta^{+}}(r,z_{g})\chi(d)\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(\overline{g})\end{bmatrix}\lambda(r^{h_{-1}}).

∎

For
r=h_{-1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm,1/2}_{1,\chi}(rz_{g})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(rz_{g})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{\delta^{+}}(r\overline{g},z_{0})\overline{\Pi}_{X^{\ast},\psi}(r\overline{g})\begin{bmatrix}A^{+}\\
A^{-}\end{bmatrix}\Big)([-1,\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}])

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)>0,\\
0&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cr}0&\det(g)>0,\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&\det(g)<0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.

If
\operatorname{Im}(z)>0
,

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(rz)=\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(rz)&\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(rz)\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(rz)&\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(rz)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)\\
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)&0\end{bmatrix}=\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

(10.2)

If
\operatorname{Im}(z)<0
,

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(rz)=\begin{bmatrix}\theta^{+,1/2}_{1,\chi}(rz)&\theta^{+,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(rz)\\
\theta^{-,1/2}_{1,\chi}(rz)&\theta^{-,1/2}_{-1,\chi}(rz)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-i\pi z_{g}n^{2}}\chi(n)&0\\
0&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}^{\sigma}n^{2}}\chi(n)\end{bmatrix}=\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

(10.3)

Similarly, we have:

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(rz)=\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(z)\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Definition 10.4
.

\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{-1}.

Lemma 10.5
.

\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}
is an irreducible representation of two dimension.

Proof.

Recall the character
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}
from Lemma
4.10
. The character
\chi
can also be viewed as a character of
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
. Then
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}=[\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}]^{-1}\otimes\chi^{-1}
.
∎

Note that
[\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})]=[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]\cup[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]h_{-1}
. Let
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
denote the complex functions on
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
such that (1)
\operatorname{supp}e_{1}=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
and
\operatorname{supp}e_{2}=[\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})]h_{-1}
, (2)
e_{1}(1)=1=e_{2}(h_{-1})
. Let
V
be the vector space spanned by
e_{1},e_{2}
. The representation
\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}
can be realized on
V
. For any
f\in V
, we have:

\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(h_{-1})f(1)=f(h_{-1}),\quad\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(h_{-1})f(h_{-1})=f(1).

For any
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
,

\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r})f(1)=[\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}]^{-1}(\overline{r})f(1),\quad\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r})f(h_{-1})=[\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}]^{-1}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})f(h_{-1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(h_{-1})(e_{1},e_{2})

\displaystyle=(e_{1},e_{2})\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix},

(10.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r})(e_{1},e_{2})

\displaystyle=(e_{1},e_{2})\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r})^{-1}&0\\
0&\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{h_{-1}})^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

(10.5)

Similar to Theorem
9.12
, we have:

Theorem 10.6
.

For
\overline{r}=[r,\epsilon]\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

(1)

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

(2)

\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

Proof.

1) a) If
\overline{r}=\overline{r}_{0}\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})
, the result follows from Lemma
10.3
and (
10.5
).

b) If
\overline{r}=h_{-1}
, then
J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)=I_{2}
and the result follows from (
10.2
), (
10.3
)and (
10.4
).

c) For the general
\overline{r}=h_{-1}\overline{r}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(h_{-1}\overline{r}_{0}z)

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(h_{-1},\overline{r}_{0}z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}_{0}z)\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(h_{-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(h_{-1},\overline{r}_{0}z)J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r}_{0},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}_{0}^{-1})\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(h_{-1}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

2) The proof of Part (2) is similar.
∎

10.7.
Twisted by
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}

Let
\mathfrak{w}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, and let
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=(\mathfrak{w},t_{\mathfrak{w}})\in\overline{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. For
\overline{s}\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
, let
\overline{r}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. We define a character on this conjugated subgroup by

\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}):=\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s}).

Definition 10.7
.

Define the slash operators for the theta functions as follows:

(1)

[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z):=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)
;

(2)

[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z):=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{3/4}J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)
.

Proposition 10.8
.

For
\overline{r}\in\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
, we have:

(1)

[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

(2)

[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{r},z)[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

Proof.

The proof parallels that of Proposition
10.8
. For completeness, we provide the proof of statement (1); the case of (2) is analogous.
Write
\overline{r}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
.

\displaystyle J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r},z)

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z).

\displaystyle[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)

\displaystyle=\det(\mathfrak{w})^{1/4}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}(\overline{s}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},\overline{r}z)^{-1}J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{s},\overline{\mathfrak{w}}z)J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

∎

We extend the results of Sections
9.6
and
9.7
to the group
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
.

•

Follow the notions form Section
9.6
. If
N
is odd, we define:

\Theta^{\pm,1/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{2\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),\cdots,[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big),

\Theta^{\pm,3/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{2\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),\cdots,[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big).

•

Follow the notions form Section
9.7
. If
N
is even, we define:

\Theta^{\pm,1/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{2\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\Big),

\Theta^{\pm,3/2}_{\chi}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{2\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\Big).

Definition 10.9
.

\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}[\overline{\lambda}^{\pm}_{\chi}].

Lemma 10.10
.

(1)

If
N
is odd, then
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}
is an irreducible representation.

(2)

(a)

If
N\equiv 2(\bmod 4)
, then
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}
is an irreducible representation.

(b)

If
N\equiv 0(\bmod 4)
, then
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}
is a reducible representation with two different irreducible components.

Proof.

1) If
N=1
, the result holds by Prop.
4.18
(2). Assume
N>1
. Note that
\chi
is a character of
\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
. So

\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}\overline{\lambda}_{\chi}^{-1}\simeq\chi^{-1}\Big(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}\cap\overline{\Gamma}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}[\overline{\lambda}]^{-1}\Big)\simeq\chi^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}.

Note that
\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}
has three dimension. By Clifford theory,
\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}
is irreducible, so is
\operatorname{Res}_{\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})}^{\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}
.

2)
N
is even. Retains the notations from the proof of Lem.
9.14
.

a) If take
\overline{r}=[r,1]
with
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-1-2N^{2}&2\\
N^{2}&-1\end{pmatrix}
, then:

h_{-1}\overline{r}h_{-1}^{-1}=[\begin{pmatrix}-1-2N^{2}&-2\\
-N^{2}&-1\end{pmatrix},\nu_{2}(-1,r)]=[\begin{pmatrix}-1-2N^{2}&-2\\
-N^{2}&-1\end{pmatrix},1].

So

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})=\Bigl(\frac{2N^{2}}{-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{-1}^{-1}\neq\overline{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\overline{r})=\Bigl(\frac{-2N^{2}}{-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{-1}^{-1},

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})=\Bigl(\frac{2N^{2}}{N^{2}-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{N^{2}-1}^{-1}\neq\overline{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})=\Bigl(\frac{-2N^{2}}{N^{2}-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{N^{2}-1}^{-1}.

If take
\overline{r}_{1}=[r_{1},1]
with
r_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-1-4N^{2}&2\\
2N^{2}&-1\end{pmatrix}
, then:

\overline{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\overline{r}_{1})=\Bigl(\frac{-4N^{2}}{-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{-1}^{-1}=-\epsilon_{-1}^{-1}\neq\overline{\lambda}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}_{1}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})=\Bigl(\frac{4N^{2}}{2N^{2}-1}\Bigr)\epsilon_{2N^{2}-1}^{-1}=\epsilon_{-1}^{-1}.

So the result of Part(a) holds.

b) We take the same
\overline{r}
as above. Then

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})\neq\overline{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\overline{r}),\quad\overline{\lambda}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}})\neq\overline{\lambda}^{h_{-1}}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}}^{-1}\overline{r}\,\overline{M}_{q_{1}}).

So the result of Part(a) holds.
∎

Analogue of Theorem
9.12
, we have:

Theorem 10.11
.

Let
\overline{r}\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{0}(N^{2})
.

(1)

\Theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{+}}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{\pm,\delta^{+}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

(2)

\Theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(\overline{r}z)=J_{\delta^{-}}(\overline{r},z)\Theta^{\pm,\delta^{-}}_{\chi}(z)\overline{\gamma}^{\pm}_{\chi}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

11.
Examples

11.1.
Example 1 to Sect.
9.5

\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}2&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]
. Recall
\omega
and
h(a)
from Introduction.
Note:

\Gamma_{\theta}=\Gamma(2)\sqcup\Gamma(2)\omega,\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}=\Gamma_{0}(4),\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\omega\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\tfrac{1}{2}\\
2&0\end{pmatrix}=\omega h(2),\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{\theta}\mathfrak{w}=\Gamma_{0}(4)\sqcup\Gamma_{0}(4)\omega h(2).

Definition 11.1
.

(1)

\theta(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{2\pi izn^{2}}
.

(2)

\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{\theta}\mathfrak{w}=\Gamma_{0}(4)\sqcup\Gamma_{0}(4)\omega h(2)
.

(3)

\nu_{\theta,2}(r)=\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})
, for
r\in\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}
.

(4)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}(4)^{\kappa}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\{[\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\lambda(r)^{-1}]\mid r\in\Gamma_{\theta}\}=\{[r,\nu_{\theta,2}(r)^{-1}]\mid r\in\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}\}
.

Lemma 11.2
.

(1)

There exists a group isomorphism
\iota:\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}\to\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}(4)^{\kappa};r\to[r,\nu_{\theta,2}(r)^{-1}]
.

(2)

\nu_{\theta,2}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\left(\frac{c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{0}(4),\\
\left(\frac{c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-c,d)_{\mathbb{R}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\omega h(2)\in\Gamma_{0}(4)\omega h(2).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

1)
\iota(r_{1})\iota(r_{2})=[r_{1},\nu_{\theta,2}(r_{1})^{-1}]\cdot[r_{2},\nu_{\theta,2}(r_{2})^{-1}]=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})]=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}[r_{1}r_{2},\nu_{\theta,2}(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]=\iota(r_{1}r_{2})
.

2)
Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
,
\overline{r}=[r,\lambda(r)^{-1}]\in\Gamma_{\theta}^{\prime}
and
\overline{r}=[r,1]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. Then:

\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&2b\\
\tfrac{1}{2}c&d\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathfrak{w}\omega h(2)\mathfrak{w}^{-1}=\omega;

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}\omega h(2),\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. \ref {prog0}}}}{{=}}1;

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}\overline{r}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r)\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\lambda(r)^{-1}]=[\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\lambda(r)^{-1}];

\lambda(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {gammar}}}{{=}}\left(\frac{c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d};

\lambda(\omega h(2))=\overline{\lambda}^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}([\omega h(2),1])=\overline{\lambda}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}}[\omega h(2),1]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1})=\overline{\lambda}([\omega,1])=e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}};

\lambda(r\omega h(2))=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega h(2))\lambda(r)\lambda(\omega h(2));

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega h(2))

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega h(2))\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega,h(2))

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r\omega,h(2))

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\omega)=(-c,d)_{\mathbb{R}}.

∎

In this case, we conclude:

Proposition 11.3
.

\theta(rz)=\nu_{\theta,2}(r)\sqrt{cz+d}\theta(z)
, for
r\in\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}
.

Remark 11.4
.

For
r\in\Gamma_{0}(4)
, it is comparable with the classical result in Shimura paper
[
29
]
. We extend it to a little bigger group
\Gamma_{0}(4)^{\kappa}
by following Lion-Vergne’s paper
[
18
]
.

11.2.
Example 2 to Sect.
9.6

We consider where
N=1
and
\chi\equiv 1
. In this case,
\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[u(1),1],\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[1,1],\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[u_{-}(-1),1]
. In this case, the theta series are related to concepts introduced by Don Zagier in
[
39
, p. 27]
, specifically the “minus sign theta series” and the “fermionic theta series.”

\theta^{M,1/2}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}e^{i\pi n^{2}z}\quad\quad\textrm{(minus sign)}

\theta^{F,1/2}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}z}\quad\quad\textrm{(fermionic)}.

Then:

(1)

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}e^{i\pi zn^{2}}=\theta^{M,1/2}(z)
;

(2)

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi zn^{2}}
;

(3)

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\theta^{F,1/2}(z)
.

(4)

\Theta^{1/2}:\mathbb{H}\longrightarrow M_{1\times 3}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto\Big([\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z),[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z),[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big)
.

1)

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{1}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}(z+1)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}e^{i\pi zn^{2}}
.

3)

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(A)(\tfrac{1}{2}e)=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}A(l+\tfrac{1}{2}e)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}A(n+\tfrac{1}{2}).

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(\tfrac{1}{2}e)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(n+\tfrac{1}{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}.

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}}\widetilde{g})

\displaystyle=\theta^{1/2}(u_{-}(-1)p_{g})

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(u_{-}(-1)p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(u_{-}(-1)p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(u_{-}(-1),p_{g}z_{0})\pi_{L,\psi}(u_{-}(-1))\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Sect. \ref {Gamma2I2}}\ \textrm{Case 8}}}{{=}}J_{1/2}(u_{-}(-1),z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(-1))^{-1}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(\tfrac{1}{2}e)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}},z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(-1))^{-1}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}.

So
[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)=m_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(-1))^{-1}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}
.

In this case, the result is the following:

Proposition 11.5
.

Let
\overline{r}=(r,\epsilon)\in\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
. Then

\Theta^{1/2}(\overline{r}z)=\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{cz+d}\;\Theta^{1/2}(z)\;\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

11.3.
Example 3 to Sect.
9.5

Let
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[u(1),1]
, for
\mathfrak{w}=u(1)=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
.
Let
\overline{s}=[s,\lambda({s})^{-1}]\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\mu_{8}}_{\theta}\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
r=u(-1)su(1)
. Note:

u(-1)\Gamma_{\theta}u(1)=\Gamma^{0}(2),\quad u(-1)\Gamma(2)u(1)=\Gamma(2),\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[u(-1),1].

Lemma 11.6
.

(1)

\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},g)=1
, for
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(2)

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]
.

Proof.

1)
\nu_{2}(u(-1),g)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Ex.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1)^{-1},g)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1)^{-1}g,u(-1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {prog0}}}{{=}}1
.

2)

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[u(-1)su(1),\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1),s)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1)s,u(1))\lambda({s})^{-1}]=[r,\lambda({s})^{-1}].

∎

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}e^{i\pi zn^{2}}.

Definition 11.7
.

(1)

\theta^{M,1/2}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}e^{i\pi zn^{2}}
.

(2)

\Gamma^{0^{\prime}}(2)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\{[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]\mid r\in\Gamma^{0}(2)\}
.

(3)

\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)
.

Lemma 11.8
.

There exists a group isomorphism
\iota:\Gamma^{0}(2)\simeq\Gamma^{0^{\prime}}(2);r\to[r,\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r)^{-1}]
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\iota(r_{1})\iota(r_{2})

\displaystyle=[r_{1},\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r_{1})^{-1}]\cdot[r_{2},\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]=\iota(r_{1}r_{2}).

∎

Note that

\Gamma(2)/\{\pm I\}\simeq\langle\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\rangle=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\mid a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)\}.

Lemma 11.9
.

Let
\delta_{M}:\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\mid a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)\}\to\mu_{4};g\to i^{c/2}
. Then
\delta_{M}
is a character.

Proof.

g_{1}g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
c_{1}&d_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
c_{2}&d_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}a_{2}+b_{1}c_{2}&a_{1}b_{2}+b_{1}d_{2}\\
c_{1}a_{2}+d_{1}c_{2}&c_{1}b_{2}+d_{1}d_{2}\end{pmatrix},a_{2}=1+4k_{1},d_{1}=1+4l_{2},2\mid c_{1},2\mid c_{2};

\delta_{M}(g_{1}g_{2})=i^{\tfrac{c_{1}a_{2}+d_{1}c_{2}}{2}}=i^{\tfrac{c_{2}+c_{1}+4k_{1}c_{1}+4l_{2}c_{2}}{2}}=i^{c_{1}/2+c_{2}/2}=\delta_{M}(g_{1})\delta_{M}(g_{2}).

∎

So
\delta_{M}
is also a character of
\Gamma(2)
such that
\delta_{M}(-I)=1=\delta_{M}(u(2))
and
\delta_{M}(u_{-}(2))=i
. More precisely,

\delta_{M}(\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix})=i^{c/2}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2}.

Lemma 11.10
.

Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
. Then
\lambda^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(r)=\lambda(r)\delta_{M}(r)
.

Proof.

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=1.

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}},r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chap3crao2})}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}\lambda(r_{1})\lambda(r_{2})\lambda(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}\lambda(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})\lambda(r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})\lambda(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})^{-1}.

So
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\delta^{\prime}(r)=\lambda(r)\delta^{\prime}(r)
, for some character
\delta^{\prime}
of
\Gamma(2)
. Note that
\Gamma(2)
is generated by
u(2)
,
u_{-}(2)
and
-I
.

(I)

If
r=-I
,
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)
and

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {relacto}(1)}}{{=}}1.

So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=1=\delta_{M}(r)
.

(II)

If
r=u(2)
,
r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}=u(1)u(2)u(-1)=u(2)
, then
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)
. So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=1=\delta_{M}(r)
.

(III)

If
r=u_{-}(2)
,
r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}=u(1)u_{-}(2)u(-1)=\begin{pmatrix}3&2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix}
, then

\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(\begin{pmatrix}3&2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {gammar}}}{{=}}(\frac{-4}{-1})i^{-1}=i,\quad\lambda(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {gammar}}}{{=}}1

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}=\nu_{2}(\omega,r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\omega,r^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}\nu_{2}(\omega,\begin{pmatrix}3&-2\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}1.

So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=i=\delta_{M}(r)
.

∎

Lemma 11.11
.

\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\lambda(r)^{-1}\delta_{M}(r)^{-1},&\textrm{ if }r\in\Gamma(2),\\
e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\lambda(r_{1})^{-1}\delta_{M}(r_{1})^{-1},&\textrm{ if }r=r_{1}u_{-}(-1)\textrm{ with }r_{1}\in\Gamma(2).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The first statement comes from the above lemma. If
r=r_{1}u_{-}(1)
, then:

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u_{-}(-1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},u_{-}(-1)\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(-1),\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=1;

\lambda(\mathfrak{w}u_{-}(-1)\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\lambda(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.\ref {lambda}}}}{{=}}m_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix})\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix})=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}};

\displaystyle\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1}u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},u(1))^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda(r_{1})^{-1}\delta_{M}(r_{1})^{-1}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

∎

As a consequence, we have:

Lemma 11.12
.

\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2}i^{c/2},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2),\\
\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}i^{c/2}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2}e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)u_{-}(-1).\end{array}\right.

For
r^{\prime}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]\in\Gamma^{0^{\prime}}(2)\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

J_{1/2}(r^{\prime},z)=J_{1/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)=J_{1/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r).

In this case, the result is the following:

Proposition 11.13
.

\theta^{M,1/2}(rz)=\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r)\sqrt{cz+d}\theta^{M,1/2}(z)
, for
r\in\Gamma^{0}(2)
.

Remark 11.14
.

The result is similar as Prop.
11.3
. This means that Zagier’s minus theta function is a
1/2
-modular form on
\Gamma^{0}(2)
. Certainly, it is just a consequence of Lion-Vergne
[
18
]
.

11.4.
Example 4 to Sect.
9.5

Let
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[u_{-}(-1),1]
, for
\mathfrak{w}=u_{-}(-1)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}
.
Let
\overline{s}=[s,\lambda({s})^{-1}]\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\mu_{8}}_{\theta}\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
r=u_{-}(1)su_{-}(-1)
. Note:

u_{-}(1)\Gamma_{\theta}u_{-}(-1)=\Gamma_{0}(2),\quad u_{-}(1)\Gamma(2)u_{-}(-1)=\Gamma(2),\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[u_{-}(1),1].

Lemma 11.15
.

(1)

\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},g)=\nu_{2}(\omega,g)\nu_{2}(\omega,g^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})
, for
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
.

(2)

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]
.

Proof.

1)
\nu_{2}(u(-1),g)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Ex.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1)^{-1},g)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(-1)^{-1}g,u(-1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {prog0}}}{{=}}1
,

\nu_{2}(-I,g)=1
. Note that
u_{-}(1)=\omega^{-1}u(-1)\omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\nu_{2}(u_{-}(1),g)

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\omega^{-1}u(-1)\omega,g)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {chap3twocover}}}{{=}}\nu_{2}(\omega^{-1},g)\nu_{2}(u(-1)\omega,g^{\omega^{-1}})

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\omega^{-1},g)\nu_{2}(u(-1),g^{\omega^{-1}})\nu_{2}(\omega,g^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\omega^{-1},g)\nu_{2}(\omega,g^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(\omega,g)\nu_{2}(\omega,g^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)}).

2)
\nu_{2}(u_{-}(-1),s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Ex.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(1),s)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(1)s,u_{-}(-1))
.

1=\nu_{2}(u_{-}(-1)u_{-}(1),s)=\nu_{2}(u_{-}(-1),s)\nu_{2}(u_{-}(1),s^{u_{-}(-1)})=\nu_{2}(u_{-}(-1),s)\nu_{2}(u_{-}(1),r).

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}\overline{s}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}

\displaystyle=[u_{-}(1)su_{-}(-1),\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(1),s)\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(1)s,u_{-}(-1))\lambda({s})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[u_{-}(1)su_{-}(-1),\nu_{2}(u_{-}(-1),s)\lambda({s})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[r,\nu_{2}(u_{-}(1),r)^{-1}\lambda({s})^{-1}].

∎

[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=m_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(-1))^{-1}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}.

Definition 11.16
.

(1)

\theta^{F,1/2}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}
.

(2)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}(2)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\{[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}]\mid r\in\Gamma_{0}(2)\}
.

(3)

\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Def.}}}{{=}}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)
.

Lemma 11.17
.

There exists a group isomorphism
\iota:\Gamma_{0}(2)\simeq\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}(2);r\to[r,\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r)^{-1}]
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\iota(r_{1})\iota(r_{2})

\displaystyle=[r_{1},\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r_{1})^{-1}]\cdot[r_{2},\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}\mathfrak{w}^{-1},\mathfrak{w}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}[r_{1}r_{2},\lambda(\mathfrak{w}r_{1}r_{2}\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]

\displaystyle=[r_{1}r_{2},\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}]=\iota(r_{1}r_{2}).

∎

Note that

\Gamma(2)/\{\pm I\}\simeq\langle\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\rangle=\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\mid a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)\}.

Lemma 11.18
.

Let
\delta_{F}:\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\mid a\equiv d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)\}\to\mu_{4};g\to i^{b/2}
. Then
\delta_{F}
is a character.

Proof.

g_{1}g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
c_{1}&d_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
c_{2}&d_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}a_{2}+b_{1}c_{2}&a_{1}b_{2}+b_{1}d_{2}\\
c_{1}a_{2}+d_{1}c_{2}&c_{1}b_{2}+d_{1}d_{2}\end{pmatrix},a_{1}=1+4k_{1},d_{2}=1+4l_{2},2\mid b_{1},2\mid b_{2};

\delta_{F}(g_{1}g_{2})=i^{\tfrac{a_{1}b_{2}+b_{1}d_{2}}{2}}=i^{\tfrac{b_{2}+b_{1}+4k_{1}b_{2}+4l_{2}b_{1}}{2}}=i^{b_{1}/2+b_{2}/2}=\delta_{F}(g_{1})\delta_{F}(g_{2}).

∎

So
\delta_{F}
is also a character of
\Gamma(2)
such that
\delta_{F}(-I)=1=\delta_{F}(u_{-}(2))
and
\delta_{F}(u(2))=i
. More precisely,

\delta_{F}(\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix})=i^{b/2}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{b/2}.

Lemma 11.19
.

Let
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
. Then
\lambda^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(r)=\lambda(r)v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\delta_{F}(r)
.

Proof.

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1}).

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}},r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref {chap3crao2})}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}\lambda(r_{1})\lambda(r_{2})\lambda(r_{1}r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{2})^{-1}v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem. }\ref {mutirr12}}}{{=}}\lambda(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})\lambda(r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})\lambda(r_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}r_{2}^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})^{-1}.

So
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\delta^{\prime}(r)
, for some character
\delta^{\prime}
of
\Gamma(2)
. Note that
\Gamma(2)
is generated by
u(2)
,
u_{-}(2)
and
-I
.

(I)

If
r=-I
,
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)
and

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {relacto}(1)}}{{=}}1.

So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=1=\delta_{F}(r)
.

(II)

If
r=u(2)
,
r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}=u_{-}(-1)u(2)u_{-}(1)=\begin{pmatrix}3&2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix}
, then:

\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(\begin{pmatrix}3&2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {gammar}}}{{=}}(\frac{-4}{-1})i^{-1}=i,\quad\lambda(r)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {gammar}}}{{=}}1,

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}=\nu_{2}(\omega,r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\omega,r^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}\nu_{2}(\omega,\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-2&1\end{pmatrix})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}1.

So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=i=\delta_{F}(r)
.

(III)

If
r=u_{-}(2)
,
r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}}=u_{-}(-1)u_{-}(2)u_{-}(1)=r
, then
\lambda(r^{\mathfrak{w}^{-1}})=\lambda(r)
and

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}=\nu_{2}(\omega,r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\omega,r^{\omega^{-1}u(-1)})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}\nu_{2}(\omega,\begin{pmatrix}3&-2\\
2&-1\end{pmatrix})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {nu2abcd}}}{{=}}1.

So
\delta^{\prime}(r)=1=\delta_{F}(r)
.

∎

Lemma 11.20
.

\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\lambda(r)^{-1}\delta_{F}(r)^{-1},&\textrm{ if }r\in\Gamma(2),\\
e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\lambda(r_{1})^{-1}\delta_{F}(r_{1})^{-1},&\textrm{ if }r=r_{1}u(1)\textrm{ with }r_{1}\in\Gamma(2).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The first statement comes from the above lemma. If
r=r_{1}u(1)
, then:

v_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u(1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {twocoverex}}}{{=}}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\mathfrak{w},u(1)\mathfrak{w}^{-1})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u(1),\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix})=1;

\lambda(\mathfrak{w}u(1)\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\lambda(\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.\ref {lambda}}}}{{=}}m_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}};

\displaystyle\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1}u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1}u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},u(1))^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r_{1})^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r_{1})^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(u(1))^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},u(1))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda(r_{1})^{-1}\delta_{F}(r_{1})^{-1}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

∎

As a consequence, we have:

Lemma 11.21
.

\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{b/2}i^{b/2},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2),\\
\left(\frac{2c}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}i^{b/2}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{b/2}e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)u(1).\end{array}\right.

For
r^{\prime}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)^{-1}\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]\in\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}(2)\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

J_{1/2}(r^{\prime},z)=J_{1/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\nu_{2}(\mathfrak{w}^{-1},r)\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)=J_{1/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r).

In this case, the result is the following:

Proposition 11.22
.

\theta^{F,1/2}(rz)=\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r)\sqrt{cz+d}\theta^{F,1/2}(z)
, for
r\in\Gamma_{0}(2)
.

In particular, if
r=\begin{pmatrix}1&8\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
, then
\theta^{F,1/2}(rz)=\theta^{F,1/2}(z)
.

Remark 11.23
.

The result is similar as Prop.
11.3
. This means that Zagier’s fermionic theta function is a
1/2
-modular form on
\Gamma_{0}(2)
, not just
\Gamma(2)
or
\Gamma_{0}(4)
. Certainly, it is just a consequence of Lion-Vergne
[
18
]
.

11.5.
Example 5 to Sect.
9.5

Let now
\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
with positive integer
t
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]
. Then:

\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{\theta}\mathfrak{w}=\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\sqcup\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}0&-t^{-1}\\
t&0\end{pmatrix}.

If
r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{\theta}(2)\mathfrak{w}
, then
r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
. We define:

\nu_{\theta,t}(r)=\nu_{\theta}(r^{\prime}).

If
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}
, then
r^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
. Hence,

\nu_{\theta,t}(r)=\nu_{\theta}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\left(\frac{2c/t}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}=\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}.

If
r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}0&-t^{-1}\\
t&0\end{pmatrix}
, we write:

r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-t^{-1}\\
t&0\end{pmatrix},\quad r^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\nu_{\theta,t}(r)=\nu_{\theta}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\left(\frac{2ct^{-1}}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-ct^{-1},d)_{\mathbb{R}}=\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-ct,d)_{\mathbb{R}}.

Let:

r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}[\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})]\mathfrak{w},\quad\quad r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2}).

Then:

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1},1],\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[r^{\prime},\nu_{\theta}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1}r^{\prime}\mathfrak{w},\nu_{\theta}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]=[r,\nu_{\theta}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}],

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{1/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)e^{\pi itzn^{2}},

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(tz)=\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{3/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)ne^{\pi itzn^{2}}.

Recall
\theta^{1/2}_{\chi,t}
and
\theta^{3/2}_{\chi,t}
from Def.
9.8
.

Definition 11.24
.

\nu_{\theta,t}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2),\\
\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}e^{-\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}(-ct,d)_{\mathbb{R}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)\omega.\end{array}\right.

Then the result is the following:

Proposition 11.25
.

\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi,t}(rz)=\nu_{\theta,t}(r)(cz+d)^{\kappa/2}\chi(d)\theta^{\kappa/2}_{\chi,t}(z)
, for
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}[\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})]\mathfrak{w}
,
\kappa=1
or
3
.

11.6.
Example 6 to Sect.
9.5

Let
N
be a positive natural number.
Let
\mathfrak{w}=u(1)\in\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]\in\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. Then:

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(z+1)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi(z+1)n^{2}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi zn^{2}}

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(z+1)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)ne^{\pi izn^{2}}.

Let
s=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
,
s^{\prime}=[s,\lambda(s)^{-1}]\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\mu_{8}}_{\theta}\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
r=\mathfrak{w}^{-1}s\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}a-c&(a-c)+(b-d)\\
c&c+d\end{pmatrix}
and
r^{\prime}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. According to Sect.
11.3
,

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1},1],\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]=[r,\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r)^{-1}]\in\Gamma^{0^{\prime}}(2).

If
s\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

\chi^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(r^{\prime})=\chi(s^{\prime})=\chi(s)=\chi(d)=\chi(c+d).

Definition 11.26
.

•

\theta^{M,1/2}_{\chi}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi zn^{2}}
.

•

\theta^{M,3/2}_{\chi}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)ne^{\pi izn^{2}}
.

.

11.6.1.

Let now
\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
with positive integer
t>0
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]
. Then:

\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma^{0}(2)\mathfrak{w}=\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\sqcup\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-t&1\end{pmatrix}.

If
r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma^{0}(2)\mathfrak{w}
, then
r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\in\Gamma^{0}(2)
. We define:

\nu_{\theta^{M},t}(r)=\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r^{\prime}).

If
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}
, then
r^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
. Hence,

\nu_{\theta^{M},t}(r)=\nu_{\theta^{M}}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\left(\frac{2c/t}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2t}i^{c/2t}=\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2t}i^{c/2t}.

If
r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-t&1\end{pmatrix}

r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-t&1\end{pmatrix},\quad r^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\nu_{\theta^{M},t}(r)=\nu_{\theta^{M}}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{c/2t}i^{c/2t}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

Let:

r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}[\Gamma^{0}(2)\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})]\mathfrak{w},\quad\quad r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma^{0}(2)\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2}).

Then:

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1},1],\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[r^{\prime},\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1}r^{\prime}\mathfrak{w},\nu_{\theta^{M}}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]=[r,\nu_{\theta^{M}}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}],

[\theta^{M,1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{M,1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=\theta^{M,1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{1/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)\,e^{i\pi tzn^{2}},

[\theta^{M,3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{3/4}J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{M,3/2}_{\chi}(tz)=\theta^{M,3/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{3/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}\chi(n)\,ne^{i\pi tzn^{2}}.

.

•

\nu_{\theta^{M},t}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{\tfrac{c}{2t}}i^{\tfrac{c}{2t}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2),\\
\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{\tfrac{c}{2t}}i^{\tfrac{c}{2t}}e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}},&&r=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\

11.7.
Example 7 to Sect.
9.5

Let
N
be an odd positive natural number.
Let
\mathfrak{w}=u_{-}(-N^{2})
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]\in\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
. Then:

[\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\mathfrak{w}z)

[\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\mathfrak{w}z).

1)

\displaystyle\theta^{1/2}_{\chi}(\mathfrak{w}g)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w}g,z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\mathfrak{w}g)A\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},p_{g}(z_{0}))\pi_{L,\psi}(\mathfrak{w})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Sect. \ref {Gamma2I2}}\ \textrm{Case 8}}}{{=}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\tfrac{k}{N})\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)([\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}-kNe+\tfrac{1}{2}e])

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\psi(\tfrac{1}{4}\tfrac{k}{N})\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)([\tfrac{k}{N}e^{\ast}+\tfrac{1}{2}e])\psi(-\tfrac{k^{2}}{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A(\tfrac{1}{2}+n)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})\psi(\tfrac{-k^{2}}{2}+\tfrac{k}{2N})

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\chi}(k)J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A(\tfrac{1}{2}+n)\psi(\tfrac{kn}{N})\psi(\tfrac{k}{2}+\tfrac{k}{2N})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A(\tfrac{1}{2}+n)\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{k}{N}[n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}])

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{k\bmod N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{k}{N}[n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}])

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\tfrac{1}{G(1,\chi)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\Big(\sum_{k\bmod N}\overline{\chi}(k)\psi(\tfrac{k}{N}[n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}])\Big)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\chi(n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}).

2) Similarly, we have:

\displaystyle\theta^{3/2}_{\chi}(\mathfrak{w}g)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\mathfrak{w},z_{g})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})e^{i\pi z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\chi(n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}).

Let
s=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
,
s^{\prime}=[s,\lambda(s)^{-1}]\in\overline{\Gamma}^{\mu_{8}}_{\theta}\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{SL}}^{\mu_{8}}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
r=\mathfrak{w}^{-1}s\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}a-bN^{2}&b\\
c+(a-d)N^{2}-bN^{4}&d+bN^{2}\end{pmatrix}
and
r^{\prime}=\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}
. According to Sect.
11.4
,

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1},1],\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}s^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[r,\lambda^{\mathfrak{w}}(r)^{-1}]=[r,\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r)^{-1}]\in\Gamma_{0}(2).

If
s\in\Gamma_{\theta}\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})
, we have:

\chi^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(r^{\prime})=\chi(s^{\prime})=\chi(s)=\chi(d)=\chi(bN^{2}+d).

Definition 11.30
.

•

\theta^{F,1/2}_{\chi}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\chi(n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}).

\theta^{F,3/2}_{\chi}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(n+\tfrac{1}{2})e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\chi(n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}).

11.7.1.

Let now
\mathfrak{w}=\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w},1]

\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{0}(2)\mathfrak{w}=\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\sqcup\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}1&t^{-1}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma_{0}(2)\mathfrak{w}

r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\in\Gamma_{0}(2)
. We define:

\nu_{\theta^{F},t}(r)=\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r^{\prime}).

If
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}

t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)

r\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}\Gamma(2)\mathfrak{w}\begin{pmatrix}1&t^{-1}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&t^{-1}\\

t^{-1}c&d\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\nu_{\theta^{F},t}(r)=\nu_{\theta^{F}}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})=\left(\frac{2ct}{d}\right)\epsilon^{-1}_{d}(\tfrac{-1}{d})^{bt/2}i^{bt/2}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\mathfrak{w}^{-1}[\Gamma_{0}(2)\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2})]\mathfrak{w},\quad\quad r^{\prime}=\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&tb\\
ct^{-1}&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{0}(2)\cap\Gamma_{0}(N^{2}).

Then:

\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1},1],\quad\overline{\mathfrak{w}}^{-1}[r^{\prime},\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=[\mathfrak{w}^{-1}r^{\prime}\mathfrak{w},\nu_{\theta^{F}}(r^{\prime})^{-1}]=[r,\nu_{\theta^{F}}(\mathfrak{w}r\mathfrak{w}^{-1})^{-1}],

[\theta^{F,1/2}_{\chi}]^{\overline{\mathfrak{w}}}(z)=t^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{\mathfrak{w}},z)^{-1}\theta^{F,1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=\theta^{F,1/2}_{\chi}(tz)=t^{1/4}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\pi z(n+\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}}\chi(n+\tfrac{N+1}{2}),

.

11.8.
Example 8: The discriminant function

Let
\eta(z)
denote the classical Dedekind eta function and
\Delta(z)
the discriminant cusp form (see Zagier’s treatise
[
39
]
). We keep the notations
[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}}}
from Section
11.2
.

Theorem 11.34
.

(1)

\eta(z)^{3}=\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)=\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}[\theta^{1/2}]^{\otimes}(z)
.

(2)

\Delta(z)=\tfrac{1}{256}\Big[[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big]^{8}
.

Proof.

See
[
39
, p.29]
.
∎

Let
g\in G=\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
H=\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. According to Thm.
9.16
, we have:

Proposition 11.35
.

For
g\in G
,
\eta^{3}(gz)=J_{1/2}(g,z)^{3}\eta^{3}(z)\overline{\lambda}(V_{G\to H}(g))
.

11.8.1.
Four powers

The purpose of this subsection is to prove the similar result as Theorem
9.12
for four powers. Recall the notations from Section
4.1
. Let
\mathfrak{w}=g_{p}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{w}}=\overline{g}_{p}
. Let:

M_{q_{1}^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad M_{q_{2}^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad M_{q_{3}^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix}.

\overline{M}_{q_{1}^{\prime}}=[M_{q_{1}^{\prime}},1],\quad\overline{M}_{q_{2}^{\prime}}=[M_{q_{2}^{\prime}},1],\quad\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}=[M_{q_{3}^{\prime}},1].

Lemma 11.36
.

(1)

g_{p}^{-1}M_{q_{1}^{\prime}}g_{p}=M_{q_{3}^{\prime}}
and
g_{p}^{-1}M_{q_{3}^{\prime}}g_{p}=M_{q_{1}^{\prime}}
.

(2)

\overline{g}_{p}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}\overline{g}_{p}=\overline{M}_{q_{1}^{\prime}}
and
\overline{g}_{p}^{-1}\overline{M}_{q_{1}^{\prime}}\overline{g}_{p}=\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

g_{p}M_{q_{1}^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&-p\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}=M_{q_{3}^{\prime}}g_{p},

g_{p}M_{q_{3}^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-p&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix}=M_{q_{1}^{\prime}}g_{p},

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{ Lem.}\ref {chap6Cover}}}{{=}}1,

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\nu_{2}(p,\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}((\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix})^{h_{p}},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix})

\displaystyle=1,

\overline{g}_{p}\overline{M}_{q_{1}^{\prime}}=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&-p\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},1]=\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}\overline{g}_{p}.

\overline{g}_{p}\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}-p&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-p&0\end{pmatrix},1]=\overline{M}_{q_{1}^{\prime}}\overline{g}_{p}.

∎

Let us define:

\theta^{4}(z)=[\theta^{1/2}(z)]^{4}.

Lemma 11.37
.

[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}^{\prime}}}(z)=[[\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}^{\prime}}}(z)]^{4}
.

Proof.

[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{i}^{\prime}}}(z)=\det(M_{q_{i}^{\prime}})J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}^{\prime}},z)^{-4}\theta^{4}(M_{q_{i}^{\prime}}z)=[\det(M_{q_{i}^{\prime}})^{1/4}J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{i}^{\prime}},z)^{-1}\theta^{1/2}(M_{q_{i}^{\prime}}z)]^{4}
.
∎

Lemma 11.38
.

\eta(z)^{12}=-\tfrac{1}{16}[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{1}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{2}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{3}}}(z)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
11.34
,

\displaystyle\eta(z)^{12}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{16}(-1)\Big([\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)\Big)^{4}\cdot\Big([\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\Big)^{4}\cdot\Big([\theta^{1/2}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z)\Big)^{4}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{16}[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{1}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{2}}}(z)\cdot[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z).

We change the coset representatives from
\overline{M}_{q_{i}}
to
\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{i}}
. Note that
\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{1}}
and
\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{2}}
. For
i=3
, let
s=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p+1&1\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{\theta}
and
\overline{s}=[s,1]\in\overline{\Gamma}_{\theta}
. Then:

\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-p+1&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix})=1,

\overline{M}_{q^{\prime}_{3}}=\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{3}},

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{s})=m_{X^{\ast}}(s)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(s)=e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}=1.

\displaystyle[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}^{\prime}}}(z)

\displaystyle=\theta^{4}(\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{3}}z)\,J_{1/2}(\overline{s}\overline{M}_{q_{3}},z)^{-4}

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{3}}z)^{4}\overline{\lambda}(\overline{s})^{4}\theta^{4}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{s},\overline{M}_{q_{3}}z)^{-4}J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}},z)^{-4}

\displaystyle=\theta^{4}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}}z)J_{1/2}(\overline{M}_{q_{3}},z)^{-4}

\displaystyle=[\theta^{4}]^{\overline{M}_{q_{3}}}(z).

∎

According to Thm.
9.16
, we have:

Proposition 11.39
.

For
g\in G=\overline{\operatorname{SL}}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
\eta^{12}(gz)=J(g,z)^{6}\eta^{12}(z)\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(g))
.

Lemma 11.40
.

\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(-))
is a character of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
determined by
\overline{\lambda}^{4}|_{\Gamma(2)}=1
and
\overline{\lambda}^{4}=
the sign character on
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})/\Gamma(2)\simeq S_{3}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.8
,
\overline{\lambda}^{4}(g)=1
on
\overline{\Gamma}(2)
and
\overline{\lambda}^{4}([\omega,1])=-1
. So the result follows. In the following, let us check the other elements:

(1)

If
g=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1]
, then

g\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1]

g\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix},1]

g\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]

[\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}-2&-1\\
5&2\end{pmatrix},1]\notin\overline{\Gamma}(2).

So
\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(g))=-1
.

(2)

If
g=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]
, then:

g\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1],

g\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1],

\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix})=(-1,-1)_{\mathbb{R}}(-1,-1)_{\mathbb{R}}=1,

g\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix},1],

[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}-2&5\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix},1].

So
\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(g))=-1
.

(3)

If
g=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1]
,
then:

g\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1],

g\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1],

g\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}-1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1],

[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
2&5\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}-1&2\\
-2&5\end{pmatrix},-1].

So
\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(g))=1
.

(4)

If
g=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1]
,
then:

g\overline{M}_{q_{1}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
2&3\end{pmatrix},1],

g\overline{M}_{q_{2}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1],

g\overline{M}_{q_{3}}=[\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1],

[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
2&3\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
2&5\end{pmatrix},1][\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1]=[\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
2&3\end{pmatrix},1].

So
\overline{\lambda}^{4}(V_{G\to H}(g))=1
.

∎

As a consequence, we have:

Proposition 11.41
.

\eta^{12}(gz)=(cz+d)^{6}\eta^{12}(z)\operatorname{sgn}(g)
, for
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
\operatorname{sgn}:\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})/\Gamma(2)\to\{\pm 1\}
.

It is comparable with the following results from Ono
[
21
]
.

Lemma 11.42
.

(1)

\eta^{12}(z)
is a cusp form of
\Gamma(2)
of weight
6
.

(2)

\eta^{12}(2z)
is a cusp form of
\Gamma_{0}(4)
of weight
6
.

Proof.

See
[
21
, p.3]
.
∎
